<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_power.md === -->

# db\_to\_power[](#db-to-power "Link to this heading")

db\_to\_power(*db: Number*, */*, *ref=1*) → PythonNumber[](#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_power "Link to this definition")

db\_to\_power(*db: NumericNDArrayLike*, */*, *ref=1*) → NumericNDArray
:   Convert decibels to linear power value or ratio.

    The power is calculated as ref \* 10\*\*(db / 10). This function is the
    inverse of [`power_to_db()`](keysight.pwdatatools.calc.power_to_db.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.power_to_db "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.power_to_db").

    Parameters:
    :   * **db** (*numeric numpy.ndarray, array-like, scalar, or Var*) – The input data in decibels.
        * **ref** (*float or int, default 1*) – Reference value for ratio. Default is 1.

    Returns:
    :   **power** – The linear power of the input.

    Return type:
    :   numeric numpy.ndarray or Python scalar


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_voltage.md === -->

# db\_to\_voltage[](#db-to-voltage "Link to this heading")

db\_to\_voltage(*db: Number*, */*, *ref=1*) → PythonNumber[](#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_voltage "Link to this definition")

db\_to\_voltage(*db: NumericNDArrayLike*, */*, *ref=1*) → NumericNDArray
:   Convert decibels to linear voltage value or ratio.

    The voltage is calculated as ref \* 10\*\*(db / 20). This function is the
    inverse of [`voltage_to_db()`](keysight.pwdatatools.calc.voltage_to_db.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.voltage_to_db "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.voltage_to_db").

    Parameters:
    :   * **db** (*numeric numpy.ndarray, array-like, scalar, or Var*) – The input data in decibels.
        * **ref** (*float or int, default 1*) – Reference value for ratio. Default is 1.

    Returns:
    :   **voltage** – The linear voltage of the input.

    Return type:
    :   numeric numpy.ndarray or Python scalar


---

gamma\_to\_gamma(*gamma: Number*, */*, *zref=50*, *zref\_new=50*) → complex[](#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.gamma_to_gamma "Link to this definition")

gamma\_to\_gamma(*gamma: NumericNDArrayLike*, */*, *zref=50*, *zref\_new=50*) → NumericNDArray
:   Convert Gamma referenced to zref to a new Gamma referenced to zref\_new.

    Parameters:
    :   * **gamma** (*numeric numpy.ndarray, array-like, scalar, or Var*) – The input gamma data.
        * **zref** (*numeric numpy.ndarray, array-like, scalar, or Var*) – The reference impedance of the input gamma data.
        * **zref\_new** (*numeric numpy.ndarray, array-like, scalar, or Var*) – The reference impedance of the output gamma data.

    Returns:
    :   **gamma\_new** – The output gamma data referenced to zref\_new.

    Return type:
    :   numeric numpy.ndarray or Python scalar

# power\_to\_db[](#power-to-db "Link to this heading")

power\_to\_db(*power: Number*, */*, *ref=1*) → PythonNumber[](#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.power_to_db "Link to this definition")

power\_to\_db(*power: NumericNDArrayLike*, */*, *ref=1*) → NumericNDArray
:   Convert a linear power value or ratio to decibels.

    The power in dB is calculated as 10 \* log10(abs(power) / ref).

    Parameters:
    :   * **power** (*numeric numpy.ndarray, array-like, scalar, or Var*) – The input data in linear power.
        * **ref** (*float or int, default 1*) – Reference value for ratio. Default is 1.

z\_to\_gamma(*z: Number*, */*, *zref=50*) → PythonNumber[](#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.z_to_gamma "Link to this definition")

z\_to\_gamma(*z: NumericNDArrayLike*, */*, *zref=50*) → NumericNDArray
:   Calculate gamma from an impedance and a reference impedance.

    Parameters:
    :   * **z** (*numeric numpy.ndarray, array-like, scalar, or Var*) – The input impedance data.
        * **zref** (*numeric numpy.ndarray, array-like, scalar, or Var*) – The reference impedance of the input impedance data.

    Returns:
    :   **gamma** – The output Gamma data.

    Return type:
    :   numeric numpy.ndarray or Python scalar

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.VARNAMES_DEFAULT.md === -->

# VARNAMES\_DEFAULT[](#varnames-default "Link to this heading")

VARNAMES\_DEFAULT *= VarnamesDict({'angle.phi': 'phi', 'angle.theta': 'theta', 'current.direct.enumerated': 'I\*', 'current.direct.input': 'Iin', 'current.direct.output': 'Iout', 'current.direct.input.quiescent': 'Iq\_in', 'current.direct.output.quiescent': 'Iq\_out', 'distortion.ampm': 'AMPM', 'efficiency.drain': 'DrainEff', 'efficiency.power\_added': 'PAE', 'field.electric.phi': 'EPhi', 'field.electric.theta': 'ETheta', 'frequency.enumerated': 'F\*', 'frequency.primary': 'freq', 'gain.power': 'GainP', 'gain.transducer': 'GainT', 'gain.s\_parameters.transducer': 'GainT\_s', 'gain.vector': 'VectorGain', 'gain.transducer.vector': 'VectorGainT', 'gamma.input': 'GammaIn', 'gamma.load': 'GammaLoad', 'gamma.output': 'GammaOut', 'gamma.source': 'GammaSource', 'gamma.optimum.source': 'Sopt', 'impedance.input': 'Zin', 'impedance.load': 'ZLoad', 'impedance.source': 'ZSource', 'impedance.reference': 'Zref', 'impedance.load.reference': 'ZrefLoad', 'impedance.port': 'PortZ', 'impedance.reference.source': 'ZrefSource', 'name.port': 'PortName', 'power.available.input': 'PinAvail', 'power.delivered.input': 'PinDel', 'power.delivered.load': 'PLoad', 'power.available.source': 'PSource', 'noise\_figure.minimum': 'NFmin', 'resistance.noise': 'Rn', 'voltage.dc.enumerated': 'V\*', 'voltage.dc.input': 'Vin', 'voltage.dc.output': 'Vout', 'voltage.dc.input.quiescent': 'Vq\_in', 'voltage.dc.output.quiescent': 'Vq\_out', 'wave.enumerated.incident': 'a\*', 'wave.enumerated.reflected': 'b\*'})*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.roles.VARNAMES_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   An immutable mapping of variable roles to their names.

    Notes

    The \* character is a placeholder for any positive integer, and, if
    present, there must be only one and it must be located at the end of
    the string.

    For example, the default value for the enumerated frequency
    variable is `F*`. This allows for the following enumerated
    frequency variables:

    * `F1`
    * `F2`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.complex_vector_to_str_series.md === -->

# complex\_vector\_to\_str\_series[](#complex-vector-to-str-series "Link to this heading")

complex\_vector\_to\_str\_series(*data: ndarray | Series*, *form: Literal['rect', 'polar']*) → Series[](#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.complex_vector_to_str_series "Link to this definition")
:   Return a pandas Series of stringified complex numbers in rect or polar form.

    This is useful for creating labels for legends.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** – A 1D ndarray or pd.Series of complex numbers.
        * **form** – Either `'rect'` or `'polar'`.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools.viz import complex_vector_to_str_series
    >>> data = np.array([1+1j, 2+2j, 3+3j])
    >>> series = complex_vector_to_str_series(data, form="rect")
    >>> series
    0    1.000 + j 1.000
    1    2.000 + j 2.000
    2    3.000 + j 3.000
    dtype: string
    >>> series = complex_vector_to_str_series(data, form="polar")
    >>> series
    0    1.414 | 45.0
    1    2.828 | 45.0
    2    4.243 | 45.0
    dtype: string
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.contourplot.md === -->

# contourplot[](#contourplot "Link to this heading")

contourplot(*\**, *x: KeyorVector*, *y: KeyorVector*, *z: KeyorVector*, *ax=None*, *sca: bool = False*, *data: 'VizDataInput' | None = None*, *levels: int | ArrayLike | None = None*, *add\_xlabel: bool = True*, *add\_ylabel: bool = True*, *xlabel: str | None = None*, *ylabel: str | None = None*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.contourplot "Link to this definition")
:   Plot contour lines on a matplotlib Axes.

    Parameters:
    :   * **x** – A str key in data, a 1D ndarray, or pd.Series to use as
          the x variable.
        * **y** – A str key in data, a 1D ndarray, or pd.Series to use as
          the y variable.
        * **z** – A str key in data, a 1D ndarray, or pd.Series that
          represents z variable to contour.
        * **ax** – The matplotlib Axes to plot on. If `None`, use the
          current axes. Defaults to `None`.
        * **sca** – A bool that determines whether to set the Axes used for plotting
          as the current Axes. Invokes `matplotlib.pyplot.sca()`.
          Defaults to `False`.
        * **data** – An optional DataFrame, Block, or mapping with all or some of
          the x, y, and z variables.
        * **levels** (*int or array-like, optional*) – Determines the number and positions of the contour lines or
          regions. If an int, use matplotlib’s MaxNLocator, which tries to
          automatically choose no more than n+1 “nice” contour levels
          between minimum and maximum numeric values of Z. If array-like,
          draw contour lines at the specified levels. The values must be in
          increasing order.
        * **add\_xlabel** – If `True`, add an x label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **add\_ylabel** – If `True`, add a y label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **xlabel** – If `add_xlabel` is `True`, use this as the x label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use the
          name of the x variable. If name of the variable is unknown, use
          the default of label, which is `'x'`. Defaults to `None`.
        * **ylabel** – If `add_ylabel` is `True`, use this as the y label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use the
          name of the y variable. If name of the variable is unknown, use
          the default of label, which is `'y'`. Defaults to `None`.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Additional keyword arguments are passed on to
          `matplotlib.pyplot.contour`. See the matplotlib documentation
          for more info.

    Return type:
    :   matplotlib.contour.QuadContourSet


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.make_contour_levels.md === -->

# make\_contour\_levels[](#make-contour-levels "Link to this heading")

make\_contour\_levels(*z: KeyorVector*, *\**, *data: 'VizDataInput' | None = None*, *stepsize: float | int = nan*, *nlevels: int = 10*, *limit: float | int = nan*, *limit\_type: Literal['max', 'min'] = 'max'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.make_contour_levels "Link to this definition")
:   Make array of contour levels for use in contour plotting.

    Parameters:
    :   * **z** – The z data. Can be a key to a column in data or a vector
          (a 1D numpy ndarray or pandas Series).
        * **data** – An optional pandas DataFrame, Block, or mapping. If provided,
          x, y, and z can be str keys. Defaults to `None`.
        * **stepsize** – The step size between each contour level. If NaN, use
          auto-calculated step size. Defaults to `numpy.nan`.
        * **nlevels** – The number of contour levels to plot. Defaults to 5.
        * **limit** – A maximum or minimum value for the levels. If NaN, the max or
          min of the gridded data is used. Defaults to `numpy.nan`.
        * **limit\_type** – Either `'max'` or `'min'`. Defaults to `'max'`.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.smith_chart.md === -->

# smith\_chart[](#smith-chart "Link to this heading")

smith\_chart(*ax=None*, *\**, *j\_labels: bool = True*, *r\_labels: bool = True*, *sca: bool = False*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.smith_chart "Link to this definition")
:   Create a Smith Chart on a matplotlib Axes.

    Parameters:
    :   * **ax** (*Axes, optional*) – The Axes to draw the Smith Chart on. If None, the current Axes is
          used.
        * **j\_labels** (*bool, default True*) – Whether to label the arcs with the reactance values. If False,
          the labels are not shown.
        * **r\_labels** (*bool, default True*) – Whether to label the circles with the resistance values. If False,
          the labels are not shown.
        * **sca** (*bool, default False*) – A bool that determines whether to set the Axes used for plotting
          as the current Axes. Invokes `matplotlib.pyplot.sca()`.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Additional keyword arguments are not used by this function but
          required to work with seaborn.


---

# ADSWriteInvalid.\_\_init\_\_[](#adswriteinvalid-init "Link to this heading")

ADSWriteInvalid.\_\_init\_\_(*boolean: Literal['float\_w\_nan', 'int\_w\_null'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *complexfloating: Literal['complex\_w\_nan'] = 'complex\_w\_nan'*, *floating: Literal['float\_w\_nan'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *integer: Literal['float\_w\_nan', 'int\_w\_null'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *string: Literal['str\_w\_null'] = 'str\_w\_null'*, *nan\_rep: float = nan*, *integer\_null\_rep: int = 99999*, *string\_null\_rep: str = 'null'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize instance of ADSWriteInvalid.

    Parameters:
    :   * **boolean** (*{“float\_w\_nan”, “int\_w\_null”}, default “float\_w\_nan”*) – Setting for writing boolean variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **complexfloating** (*{“complex\_w\_nan”}, default “complex\_w\_nan”*) – Setting for writing complex variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **floating** (*{“float\_w\_nan”}, default “float\_w\_nan”*) – Setting for writing float variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **integer** (*{“float\_w\_nan”, “int\_w\_null”}, default “float\_w\_nan”*) – Setting for writing integer variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **string** (*{“str\_w\_null”}, default “str\_w\_null”*) – Setting for writing string variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **nan\_rep** (*float, default NaN*) – Setting for writing NaN values in complex or float
          variables.
        * **integer\_null\_rep** (*int, default 99999*) – Setting for writing null values in integer variables.
        * **string\_null\_rep** (*str, default “null”*) – Setting for writing null values in string variables.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.md === -->

# ADSWriteInvalid[](#adswriteinvalid "Link to this heading")

*class* ADSWriteInvalid[](#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid "Link to this definition")
:   Options for writing invalid datapoints to ADS datasets.

    Each of the 5 generic datatypes has one or more available options for
    writing invalid data to ADS datasets. Additionally, there are options
    for NaN and null representations. NaN values only apply to floating
    and complexfloating datatypes. Note that the current implementation
    does not allow for separate definition of NaN for floating and
    complexfloating.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`boolean`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean") | Option for boolean data. |
    | [`boolean_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean_options "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean_options") | All valid options for [`boolean`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.boolean") property. |
    | [`complexfloating`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.complexfloating.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.complexfloating "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.complexfloating") | Return option for complexfloating data. |
    | [`complexfloating_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.complexfloating_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.complexfloating_options "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.complexfloating_options") | Return all options for complexfloating data. |
    | [`floating`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.floating.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.floating "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.floating") | Return option for floating data. |
    | [`floating_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.floating_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.floating_options "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.floating_options") | Return all options for floating data. |
    | [`integer`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer") | Return option for integer data. |
    | [`integer_null_rep`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer_null_rep.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer_null_rep "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer_null_rep") | Return int that represents null values. |
    | [`integer_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer_options "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.integer_options") | Return all options for integer data. |
    | [`nan_rep`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.nan_rep.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.nan_rep "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.nan_rep") | Return float that represents NaN values. |
    | [`read_or_write`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.read_or_write.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.read_or_write "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.read_or_write") | Return literal 'read' or 'write'. |
    | [`string`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string") | Return option for string data. |
    | [`string_null_rep`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string_null_rep.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string_null_rep "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string_null_rep") | Return str that represents null values. |
    | [`string_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string_options "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.string_options") | Return all options for string data. |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.__init__")([boolean, complexfloating, ...]) | Initialize instance of ADSWriteInvalid. |
    | [`get_formats`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.get_formats.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.get_formats "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.get_formats")() | Return all supported formats. |
    | [`mapping`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.mapping.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.mapping "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.mapping")() | Return dictionary mapping of the options. |
    | [`replace`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.replace.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.replace "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.replace")(\*\*kwargs) | Replace property value(s). |

# ADSWriteInvalid.replace[](#adswriteinvalid-replace "Link to this heading")

ADSWriteInvalid.replace(*\*\*kwargs*) → [ADSWriteInvalid](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.replace "Link to this definition")
:   Replace property value(s).

    Since all instances of ADSWriteInvalid are immutable, this returns a
    new instance with all property values copied from self, except any
    new value(s) specified as kwarg(s).

    Parameters:

        * **complexfloating** (*{“complex\_w\_nan”}*) – Defaults to `self.complexfloating`.
        * **floating** (*{“float\_w\_nan”}*) – Defaults to `self.floating`.

# AttrsDict[](#attrsdict "Link to this heading")

*class* AttrsDict[](#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "Link to this definition")
:   A type-restricted dict that stores arbitrary attributes (metadata) for [`Var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var"), [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block"), and [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group").

    All keys must be str, and all values must be bool, complex, float, int, str, None, numpy ndarray, or an instance of
    `CustomProperty`.

    See also

    For more information and examples, see the [Use the Var Class](../../howto/use_var_class.md#use-var-class), [Use the Block Class](../../howto/use_block_class.md#use-block-class) and [Use the Group Class](../../howto/use_group_class.md#use-group-class).

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.__init__")([mapping]) | Initialize new instance of PropertyDict. |
    | [`clear`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.clear.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.clear "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.clear")() |  |
    | [`copy`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.copy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.copy "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.copy")() | Return a deep copy of the PropertyDict. |
    | [`get`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.get.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.get "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.get")(k[,d]) |  |
    | [`items`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.items.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.items "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.items")() |  |
    | [`keys`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.keys.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.keys "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.keys")() |  |
    | [`pop`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.pop.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.pop "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.pop")(k[,d]) | If key is not found, d is returned if given, otherwise KeyError is raised. |
    | [`popitem`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.popitem.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.popitem "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.popitem")() | as a 2-tuple; but raise KeyError if D is empty. |
    | [`setdefault`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.setdefault.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.setdefault "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.setdefault")(k[,d]) |  |
    | [`update`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.update.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.update "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.update")([E, ]\*\*F) | If E present and has a .keys() method, does: for k in E.keys(): D[k] = E[k] If E present and lacks .keys() method, does: for (k, v) in E: D[k] = v In either case, this is followed by: for k, v in F.items(): D[k] = v |
    | [`values`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.values.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.values "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.values")() |  |


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.pop.md === -->

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.update.md === -->

# AttrsDict.update[](#attrsdict-update "Link to this heading")

AttrsDict.update([*E*, ]*\*\*F*) → None.  Update D from mapping/iterable E and F.[](#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.update "Link to this definition")
:   If E present and has a .keys() method, does: for k in E.keys(): D[k] = E[k]
    If E present and lacks .keys() method, does: for (k, v) in E: D[k] = v
    In either case, this is followed by: for k, v in F.items(): D[k] = v

# Block.\_\_init\_\_[](#block-init "Link to this heading")

Block.\_\_init\_\_(*variables: Variables = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *idxnames: Iterable[str | None] | None = None*, *exprs: ExprsDictLike | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize an instance of Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*DataFrame, sequence of Var, str to VarLike dict, optional*) – Several types of inputs are supported: a pandas DataFrame, a
          mapping of str variable name to ArrayLikes or Vars, or a sequence
          of Vars. Examples of ArrayLike objects are numpy ndarrays and
          MaskedArrays, Python lists, and pandas Series. If a mapping or
          sequence is provided, all data objects must have the same length
          for axis 0.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the Block. If not specified, the name is set to
          an empty string.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables (ivars).
        * **idxnames** (*iterable of str | None, optional*) – Optional iterable of names for variables that will be converted
          into indexes (idxs) for the ivars. If any of the ivars do not have
          an associated idx, the corresponding idxname should be None.
        * **exprs** (*ExprsDictLike, optional*) – A mapping of str expression name to Expr objects. The Expr objects
          represent expressions that can be applied to variables or other
          expressions.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Block-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.

    Other Parameters:
    :   **data** (*DataFrame, str to ArrayLike mapping, or None*) – A keyword-only argument that exists mostly for backwards
        compatibility. It is recommended to use variables instead of this
        keyword-only argument. You cannot specify values for both data
        and variables.

    Examples

    Let’s initialize a Block with a dict containing some data. The keys
    must be strings (these become variable names) and the values can be
    any array-like objects (these become variable data).

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> data = {'freq': np.array([1, 2, 3]), 'Vout': [1, 2, 3]}
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(data)
    >>> block
    Block(
        <'freq', 'Vout', ... with 3 observations>,
        name='',
        ivarnames=(),
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Initializing a Block with a pandas DataFrame is also supported. The
    below code results in a Block that is identical to the Block we
    created with a dict.

    ```
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> df = pd.DataFrame(data)
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(df)
    ```

    For more control when creating a Block from a pandas DataFrame, use
    [`Block.from_pandas_dataframe()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe"). It has additional parameters
    related to how the DataFrame is converted to a Block.

    So far, we’ve only intialized Blocks with data. However, a Block can
    be initialized with various metadata. Here we initialize the Block
    with a name, the names of the independent variables (ivarnames), the
    names of index variables (idxnames), variable-level metadata (using
    instances of [`Var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")), and some arbitrary Block-level metadata
    (as attrs).

    ```
    >>> freq = pwdt.Var(data=[1e9, 2e9, 3e9], name='freq', unit='Hz')
    >>> vout = pwdt.Var(data=[5, 6, 7], name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    >>> variables = {'ifreq': np.array([1, 2, 3]), 'freq': freq, 'Vout': vout}
    >>> attrs = {'temperature': 150, 'comments': 'This is measured data'}
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(
    ...     variables,
    ...     name='foo',
    ...     ivarnames=['freq'],
    ...     idxnames=['ifreq'],
    ...     attrs=attrs,
    ... )
    >>> print(block)
    Block(
        <'Vout', ... with 3 observations>,
        name='foo',
        ivarnames=('freq',),
        attrs={'temperature': ..., 'comments': ...},
    )
    ```

    See also

    [`Block.from_file()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file"), [`Block.from_pandas_dataframe()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs.md === -->

# Block.attrs[](#block-attrs "Link to this heading")

*property* Block.attrs*: [AttrsDict](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools._core.properties.AttrsDict")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs "Link to this definition")
:   Get or set a mapping of arbitrary Block-level metadata.

    The attributes are stored in an [`AttrsDict`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict"), which is a
    type-restricted dict. The keys must be str, and the values must
    be one of the following types: str, int, float, complex, bool,
    None, `numpy.ndarray`, dict, or list. Nested dicts and
    lists are also supported.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.copy.md === -->

# Block.copy[](#block-copy "Link to this heading")

Block.copy() → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Return a deep copy of self.

    The underlying data in each Var does not need to be copied; only
    metadata needs to be copied. Therefore, this is relatively inexpensive.

    This corresponds to the length of axis 0 of the Block’s Vars.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_observations.md === -->

# Block.drop\_observations[](#block-drop-observations "Link to this heading")

Block.drop\_observations(*condition: BoolArrayLike1D*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_observations "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new filtered Block without the specified observations.

    Parameters:
    :   **condition** (*1D bool array-like*) – A 1D boolean array-like with the same length as the Block’s
        axis 0, with True values at the indexes of the observations
        to drop.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the observations dropped where condition
        is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(data={'Vout': [1, 2, 3], 'Vin': [4, 5, 6]})
    >>> vout = block['Vout']
    >>> vin = block['Vin']
    >>> block.drop_observations(vout > 2)
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    >>> block.drop_observations((vout == 1) & (vin >= 4))
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    >>> block.drop_observations(vout == 1 | vin >= 4)
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    ```


---

Block.drop\_vars(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Drop the specified variables and return this Block or a new Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** (*Iterable[str]*) – The names of the variables to drop.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, return a new Block with the specified variables dropped,
          leaving the original Block unmodified. If `True`, drop the variables
          in this Block and return a reference to this Block.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified variables dropped, or this Block
        if in\_place is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any of the variable names in varnames are not in the
        Block.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.eval_expr_as_var.md === -->

# Block.eval\_expr\_as\_var[](#block-eval-expr-as-var "Link to this heading")

Block.eval\_expr\_as\_var(*key: str*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.eval_expr_as_var "Link to this definition")
:   Evaluate the expression and return the result as a Var.

    Parameters:
    :   **key** (*str*) – The name of the expression to evaluate. It must exist in the
        Block’s [`exprs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs") attribute.

    Notes

    The returned Var is not automatically added to the Block. If you
    want to add the Var to the Block, add it like this:

    ```
    >>> block["x"] = block.eval_expr_as_var("x")
    ```

    But note that not all expressions can be added as a Var to the Block.
    Only expressions that evaluate to the same length as the Block’s
    observations can be added as a Var.


---

:   Get or set a mapping of expression names to Expr objects.

    The Expr objects represent expressions that can be applied to variables or other
    expressions.

    Notes

    Expressions are experimental.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_nan.md === -->

# Block.fill\_nan[](#block-fill-nan "Link to this heading")

Block.fill\_nan(*value: FloatNumber*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_nan "Link to this definition")
:   FIll all NaN values in all Vars and return a new Block or a reference to this Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **value** (*float | int | np.floating | np.integer*) – The value to use for filling NaN values.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, fill NaN values in place and return a reference to this Block.
          If `False`, return a new Block with NaN values filled.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Notes

    Unlike [`Var.fill_nan()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan"), this method does not allow for a
    complex fill value, since that doesn’t work for float variables.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_null.md === -->

# Block.fill\_null[](#block-fill-null "Link to this heading")

Block.fill\_null(*values: [FillValues](keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues.md#keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues "keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues") | None = None*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_null "Link to this definition")
:   Fill all null values in all Vars and return a new Block or a reference to this Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **values** (*FillValues, optional*) – An optional instance of [`FillValues`](keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues.md#keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues "keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues"). If not provided,
          the default fill values are used.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, fill null values in place and return a reference to this Block.
          If `False`, return a new Block with null values filled.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Notes

    Here are the default fill values for each dtype:

    * float: NaN
    * complex: NaN + 1j \* NaN
    * integer: 0
    * boolean: False
    * string: ‘’


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file.md === -->

# Block.from\_file[](#block-from-file "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Block.from\_file(*src: str | os.PathLike[str] | BaseDataFile*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file "Link to this definition")
:   Read a datafile and return a new instance of Block.

    If the source file src has a known file extension that maps
    to a supported format, then the format can be automatically
    inferred.

    If the source file has a missing or unknown file extension, you
    must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a `format_override`
    as `src`, or 2) use the `src_format` parameter. Known readable
    file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extensions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in
    `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Block. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents the name or position of a Block in
          the source file (for example, `'/0'` or `'/SP`).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are [`ADSReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions"),
          [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions"), and [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If data cannot be represented as a single Block.

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.lpd)
    >>> b1 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/home/device.lpd')
    >>> # Using a pathlib.Path with known ext (.spl)
    >>> b2 = pwdt.Block.from_file(Path('/home/device.spl'))
    >>> # Using a DataFile with a known ext (.mdm)
    >>> b3 = pwdt.Block.from_file(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use
    the `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an
    instance of `DataFile` as
    the `src` argument and use DataFile’s `format_override`
    parameter to define the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using the ``src_format`` parameter since the file extension is missing
    >>> b1 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using ``src_format`` since the file extension is non-standard
    >>> b2 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using a DataFile with ``format_override`` is another option
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/data/noise', format_override='mdif')
    >>> b3 = pwdt.Block.from_file(datafile)
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter.
    You have to know either the name or the position of the part that
    you want to read from `src`. A Block is always returned. If the
    contents of the part cannot be represented by a single Block, a
    ValueError is raised.

    ```
    >>> # Read a Block by name
    >>> b1 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP')
    >>> # Read a Block by position
    >>> b2 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/0')
    ```

    See also

    `read_file_as_block()`
    `read_file()`
    `Group.from_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# Block.from\_pandas\_dataframe[](#block-from-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Block.from\_pandas\_dataframe(*dataframe: pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *name: str = ''*, *idxnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *combine\_multidim: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*) → [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a Block from a pandas DataFrame and optional metadata.

    The Block’s dims are derived from the DataFrame’s columns.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dataframe** (*pandas.DataFrame*) – The Block’s variables are constructed from the DataFrame’s columns
          and named row indexes (if present).
        * **name** (*str, default ‘’*) – A str name for the Block.
        * **idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the index variables (idxs) in the Block.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables (ivars) in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables into a single
          variable. If `False`, keep multidimensional variables as
          separate variables. Defaults to `True`.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*3-tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – The characters used to separate the dimension scales in the
          column names of the DataFrame. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter.
          For example, the default delimiters would interpret columns
          like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'` as variables `'S'` and
          `'PortZ'` with dimension scales of (2,1) and (1),
          respectively. Defaults to `('[', ',', ']')`.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – Arbitrary Block-level metadata.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get.md === -->

# Block.get[](#block-get "Link to this heading")

Block.get(*varname: str*, *default=None*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get "Link to this definition")
:   Return a variable by name, or None if the variable does not exist.

    Similar to `__getitem__()` and [`get_var()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var") methods, except
    that this method returns None if the varname is not found, while
    the other two methods raise a KeyError.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varname** – The name of the variable to retrieve.
        * **default** – The value to return if the variable is not found. Only
          None is supported.

    Return type:
    :   *Optional*[[*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")]

# Block.get\_var[](#block-get-var "Link to this heading")

Block.get\_var(*varname: str*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var_as_expr.md === -->

# Block.get\_var\_as\_expr[](#block-get-var-as-expr "Link to this heading")

Block.get\_var\_as\_expr(*varname: str*) → [Expr](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr "keysight.pwdatatools._core.expressions.Expr")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var_as_expr "Link to this definition")
:   Get a variable as an expression.

    Parameters:
    :   **varname** (*str*) – The name of the variable to retrieve as an expression.

    Returns:
    :   An expression representing the variable.

    Return type:
    :   [*Expr*](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr")

    Notes

    The returned Expr is not automatically added to the Block.
    If you want to add the Expr to the Block, add it to the [`exprs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs")
    attribute, giving it any arbitrary key like this:

    ```
    >>> block.exprs["x_modified"] = block.get_var_as_expr("x")
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames.md === -->

# Block.idxnames[](#block-idxnames "Link to this heading")

*property* Block.idxnames*: tuple[str | None, ...]*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames "Link to this definition")
:   Get the names of the indexes (idxs) associated with the ivars.

    The names are auto-generated from the ivarnames by prepending ‘i’.
    If an ivar does not have an associated idx, its idxname is None.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.info.md === -->

# Block.info[](#block-info "Link to this heading")

Block.info(*varnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.info "Link to this definition")
:   Return a DataFrame containing information about the specified variable(s).

    The information on each variables includes the kind (ivar, dvar, or
    idx), role, dtype, min, max, NaN count, and masked value count.

    For complex variables, the min and max values are the minimum and
    maximum magnitudes. For string, boolean and other non-numeric types,
    the min and max values are not defined.

    Parameters:
    :   **varname** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – Name(s) of a variable(s) on which to gather info. If `None`,
        return info for all variables. If `varnames` is a str,
        return a DataFrame with that variable’s info. If `varnames`
        is an iterable of str, return a DataFrame containing all of
        those variables’ info. Defaults to `None`.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.items.md === -->

# Block.items[](#block-items "Link to this heading")

Block.items() → ItemsView[str, [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.items "Link to this definition")
:   Return an items view iterator over (varname, Var) pairs.

    Return type:
    :   An ItemsView iterator over (varname, [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")) pairs.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_sweep_nodes.md === -->

# Block.iter\_sweep\_nodes[](#block-iter-sweep-nodes "Link to this heading")

Block.iter\_sweep\_nodes(*\**, *tol: float | None | Mapping[str, float | None] = None*) → Iterator[Self][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_sweep_nodes "Link to this definition")
:   Iterate over unique values of ivars (or their idxs), yielding sub-Blocks.

    The Block is split into sections using the ivars. If the ivars have
    associated idxs, those are used. If not, then the ivars’ data values are used.
    Splitting on data values can cause precision issues for float and complex
    dtypes, so tolerance(s) can be specified. With the exception of the innermost
    ivar, the ivar values (or idxs) in each sub-Block will be constant (within
    tolerances).

    Parameters:
    :   **tol** (*optional float or dict of (ivarname, optional float) pairs, default None*) – The absolute tolerance to use when splitting the Block into sections
        based upon ivars with inexact dtypes. Inexact dtypes include all float
        and complex dtypes. If tol is a float, it is the tolerance used for
        all ivars. If tol is a dict-like, the keys are ivarnames and the values
        are float tolerances for each variable. If any tolerance is None or NaN,
        the Block is split into sections based on exact value matches for that variable.

    Yields:
    :   *Block* – A sub-Block containing observations where each ivar (or its idx) has
        constant value, with exception of innermost ivar.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars.md === -->

# Block.keep\_vars[](#block-keep-vars "Link to this heading")

Block.keep\_vars(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*, *keep\_crucial: bool = True*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Keep the specified variables and return this Block or a new Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** – The names of the variables to keep.
        * **in\_place, default False** – If `False`, return a new Block with the specified variables
          kept, leaving the original Block unmodified. If `True`, keep
          the variables in this Block and return a reference to this Block.
        * **keep\_crucial, default True** – If `True`, keep all vars returned by the
          [`crucial_varnames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames") method. Otherwise, only keep them
          if they are explicitly included in varnames.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified variables kept, or this Block
        if in\_place is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any of the varnames are not in the Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars_in_place.md === -->

# Block.keep\_vars\_in\_place[](#block-keep-vars-in-place "Link to this heading")

Block.keep\_vars\_in\_place(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *keep\_crucial: bool = True*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Keep only the specified variables (modifies Block in place).

    Deprecated since version 0.12.0: Use [`keep_vars()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars") with in\_place=True instead.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** – The names of the variables to keep.
        * **keep\_crucial, default True** – If `True`, keep all vars returned by the
          [`crucial_varnames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames") method. Otherwise, only keep them
          if they are explicitly included in varnames.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any of the variable names in varnames are not in data.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.make_idxs.md === -->

# Block.make\_idxs[](#block-make-idxs "Link to this heading")

Block.make\_idxs(*\**, *tol: float | None | Mapping[str, float | None] = None*, *existing: Literal['raise', 'replace'] = 'raise'*, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.make_idxs "Link to this definition")
:   Create integer indexes (idxs) for the ivars and return this Block or a new Block.

    The idxs are created by iterating through the combinations of values of the ivar(s).

    Parameters:
    :   * **tol** (*optional float or dict of (ivarname, optional float) pairs, default None*) – The absolute tolerance to use for finding unique values for ivars with
          inexact dtypes. Inexact dtypes include all float and complex dtypes. If
          tol is a float, it is the tolerance used for all ivars. If tol is a
          dict-like, the keys are ivarnames and the values are float tolerances for
          each variable. If any tolerance is None or NaN, the unique values are
          created based on exact value matches for that variable.
        * **existing** (*{‘raise’, ‘replace’}, default ‘raise’*) – What to do if any idx already exists. If ‘raise’, raise a
          ValueError. If ‘replace’, replace existing idxs with new ones.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, return a new Block with the new idxs, leaving the
          original Block unmodified. If `True`, add the new idxs to this
          Block and return a reference to this Block.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import keysight.pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(
    ...     {
    ...         "bias": [1.2, 1.2, 1.3, 1.3],
    ...         "freq": [1e9, 2e9, 1e9, 2e9],
    ...         "z": [1, 2, 3, 4],
    ...     },
    ...     ivarnames=["bias", "freq"],
    ... )
    >>> block_idxs = block.make_idxs()
    >>> block_idxs.idxnames
    ('ibias', 'ifreq')
    ```

    Let’s view the the new Block’s data as a pandas DataFrame to help
    visualize the new idxs.

    ```
    >>> block_idxs.to_pandas_dataframe()
       ibias  ifreq  bias          freq  z
    0      0      0   1.2  1.000000e+09  1
    1      0      1   1.2  2.000000e+09  2
    2      1      0   1.3  1.000000e+09  3
    3      1      1   1.3  2.000000e+09  4
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md === -->

# Block[](#block "Link to this heading")

*class* Block[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "Link to this definition")
:   A collection of Vars that represents a non-hierarchical dataset or a portion of a hierarchical dataset.

    The Block is one of the most important and fundamental classes in the `pwdatatools`
    library. It primarily behaves as a dict-like object that maps variable names to [`Var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")
    instances. Each Var in a Block holds the data and metadata for a single dataset variable.
    In many ways, a Block is similar to a DataFrame (and the Var is similar to a Series).
    However, instead of using the DataFrame concepts of “rows” and “columns”, the Block uses
    “observations” and “variables”. The other primary difference between a Block and a
    DataFrame is the Block’s extra focus on metadata. A non-hierarchical dataset can be
    represented as a single Block. A hierarchical dataset must typically be represented by a
    combination of one or more Block(s) and Group(s).

    Examples

    Initialize a Block from a dict, a DataFrame, or a file:

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> # first, initialize from a dict
    >>> data_dict = {'freq': np.array([1, 2, 3]), 'Vout': [1, 2, 3]}
    >>> block_from_dict = pwdt.Block(data_dict)
    >>> # now, initialize from a DataFrame
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> dataframe = pd.DataFrame(data_dict)
    >>> block_from_df = pwdt.Block(dataframe)
    >>> # now, initialize from a file
    >>> block_from_file = pwdt.Block.from_file('/path/to/file.pwdt')
    ```

    See also

    For more control when creating a Block from a pandas DataFrame, use
    [`Block.from_pandas_dataframe()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe"). It has additional parameters
    related to how the DataFrame is converted to a Block.

    Initialize with additional metadata such as a name, the names of the
    independent variables, the names of index variables (idxnames), variable-level
    metadata (using instances of [`Var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")), and some arbitrary Block-level
    metadata (as attrs):

    ```
    >>> freq = pwdt.Var(data=[1e9, 2e9, 3e9], name='freq', unit='Hz')
    >>> vout = pwdt.Var(data=[5, 6, 7], name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    >>> variables = {'ifreq': np.array([1, 2, 3]), 'freq': freq, 'Vout': vout}
    >>> attrs = {'temperature': 150, 'comments': 'This is measured data'}
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(
    ...     variables,
    ...     name='foo',
    ...     ivarnames=['freq'],
    ...     idxnames=['ifreq'],
    ...     attrs=attrs,
    ... )
    ```

    Access the Block’s metadata properties:

    ```
    >>> block.name
    'foo'
    >>> block.ivarnames
    ('freq',)
    >>> block.idxnames
    ('ifreq',)
    >>> block.attrs
    AttrsDict({'temperature': 150, 'comments': 'This is measured data'})
    ```

    Access a Var from the Block:

    ```
    >>> block['Vout']
    Var(
        <Int32 data with shape (3,)>,
        name='Vout',
        dims=<empty Dims>,
        role='voltage',
        unit='V',
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    See also

    [Use the Block Class](../../howto/use_block_class.md#use-block-class) in the “How To” section of the documentation.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`attrs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs") | Get or set a mapping of arbitrary Block-level metadata. |
    | [`dvarnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.dvarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.dvarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.dvarnames") | Get the names of the dependent variables (dvars) in the Block. |
    | [`exprs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs") | Get or set a mapping of expression names to Expr objects. |
    | [`idxnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames") | Get the names of the indexes (idxs) associated with the ivars. |
    | [`ivarnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames") | Get or set the names of the independent variables (ivars) in the Block. |
    | [`name`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.name "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.name") | Get or set an arbitrary string identifier for the Block. |
    | [`varnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.varnames") | Get the names of all variables in the Block (dependent and independent). |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__init__")([variables, name, ivarnames, ...]) | Initialize an instance of Block. |
    | [`clear`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.clear.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.clear "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.clear")() | Remove all Vars from the Block and empty the ivarnames property. |
    | [`copy`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.copy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.copy "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.copy")() | Return a deep copy of self. |
    | [`count_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.count_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.count_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.count_observations")() | Return the number of observations in the Block. |
    | [`crucial_varnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames")() | Return varnames that are crucial to keep during certain operations. |
    | [`drop_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_observations")(condition) | Return a new filtered Block without the specified observations. |
    | [`drop_vars`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_vars")(varnames, \*[, in\_place]) | Drop the specified variables and return this Block or a new Block. |
    | [`drop_vars_in_place`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_vars_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_vars_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_vars_in_place")(varnames) | Drop the specified variables (modifies Block in place). |
    | [`eval_expr_as_var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.eval_expr_as_var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.eval_expr_as_var "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.eval_expr_as_var")(key) | Evaluate the expression and return the result as a Var. |
    | [`fill_nan`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_nan")(value, \*[, in\_place]) | FIll all NaN values in all Vars and return a new Block or a reference to this Block. |
    | [`fill_null`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_null.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_null "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_null")([values, in\_place]) | Fill all null values in all Vars and return a new Block or a reference to this Block. |
    | [`from_file`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file")(src[, loc, data, format\_specific, ...]) | Read a datafile and return a new instance of Block. |
    | [`from_pandas_dataframe`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe")(dataframe, \*[, name, ...]) | Create a Block from a pandas DataFrame and optional metadata. |
    | [`get`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get")(varname[, default]) | Return a variable by name, or None if the variable does not exist. |
    | [`get_var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var")(varname) | Get a variable from the Block by name. |
    | [`get_var_as_expr`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var_as_expr.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var_as_expr "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var_as_expr")(varname) | Get a variable as an expression. |
    | [`info`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.info.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.info "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.info")([varnames]) | Return a DataFrame containing information about the specified variable(s). |
    | [`items`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.items.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.items "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.items")() | Return an items view iterator over (varname, Var) pairs. |
    | [`iter_sweep_nodes`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_sweep_nodes.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_sweep_nodes "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_sweep_nodes")(\*[, tol]) | Iterate over unique values of ivars (or their idxs), yielding sub-Blocks. |
    | [`iter_vars`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_vars")() | Return an iterator over all the Vars in this Block. |
    | [`keep_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_observations")(condition) | Return a new filtered Block with only the desired observations. |
    | [`keep_vars`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars")(varnames, \*[, in\_place, keep\_crucial]) | Keep the specified variables and return this Block or a new Block. |
    | [`keep_vars_in_place`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars_in_place")(varnames, \*[, keep\_crucial]) | Keep only the specified variables (modifies Block in place). |
    | [`keys`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keys.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keys "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keys")() | Return an iterable view of the variable names. |
    | [`make_idxs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.make_idxs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.make_idxs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.make_idxs")(\*[, tol, existing, in\_place]) | Create integer indexes (idxs) for the ivars and return this Block or a new Block. |
    | [`pop`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.pop.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.pop "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.pop")(varname[, default]) | Remove and return a variable by name. |
    | [`popitem`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.popitem.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.popitem "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.popitem")() | as a 2-tuple; but raise KeyError if D is empty. |
    | [`rename_vars`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.rename_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.rename_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.rename_vars")(old\_to\_new\_map[, in\_place]) | Rename variables and return this Block or a new Block. |
    | [`rename_vars_in_place`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.rename_vars_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.rename_vars_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.rename_vars_in_place")(old\_to\_new\_map) | Rename variables (modifies Block in place). |
    | [`set_data`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data")(data, \*[, combine\_multidim\_vars, ...]) | Replace the data for variables in the Block, and return this Block or a new Block. |
    | [`set_data_in_place`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place")(data, \*[, ...]) | Set the data for variable(s) in the Block (in place). |
    | [`set_vars`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars")(variables, \*[, in\_place]) | Set variables and return this Block or a new Block. |
    | [`set_vars_in_place`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars_in_place")(variables) | Set variables in the Block (in place). |
    | [`setdefault`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.setdefault.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.setdefault "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.setdefault")(key[, default]) | Get a variable by name, or set it to a default value if it does not exist. |
    | [`sort_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_observations")(indices, \*[, in\_place]) | Sort Vars along axis 0 using indices and return a new Block or a reference to this Block. |
    | [`sort_observations_by`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_observations_by.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_observations_by "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_observations_by")(varnames, \*[, in\_place]) | Sort all Vars along axis 0 by values of the given Var(s) and return a new Block or reference to this Block. |
    | [`sort_vars`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.sort_vars")(varnames, \*[, in\_place]) | Sort the variables in the Block by name. |
    | [`to_file`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_file "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_file")(dst[, dst\_mode, dst\_format, ...]) | Write Block data (and possibly metadata) to a file. |
    | [`to_pandas_dataframe`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_pandas_dataframe")(\*[, index, ...]) | Create a pandas DataFrame from this Block. |
    | [`update`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.update.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.update "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.update")([other]) | Update the Block with Vars and/or data from another mapping. |
    | [`values`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.values.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.values "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.values")() | Return an iterable view of the Vars. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data.md === -->

# Block.set\_data[](#block-set-data "Link to this heading")

Block.set\_data(*data: Mapping[str, ArrayLike | Scalar] | pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *combine\_multidim\_vars: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data "Link to this definition")
:   Replace the data for variables in the Block, and return this Block or a new Block.

    If a variable does not exist, it is created. If it does exist,
    its data is replaced and all other attributes are preserved.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*mapping of str to array-like/scalar values or pandas.DataFrame*) – The new data to set in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim\_vars** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables in data into
          a single variable. This also has the effect of combining the
          existing multidimensional variables in the Block. If
          `False`, do not combine multidimensional variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*length 3 tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the varnames of flattened multidim variables. Only applies if
          `combine_multidim_vars=True`. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters would work for flattened
          multidim varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'`.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, return a new Block with the specified data.
          If `True`, set the data in this Block and return a reference
          to this Block.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified data, or this Block if
        in\_place is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If the length along axis 0 of any of the array-likes in
          data does not match the Block’s observation count.
          - If data is a pandas DataFrame and combine\_multidim\_vars
          is `True`, and the dimscales of a multidimensional variable
          in the DataFrame do not match the dimscales of the
          corresponding multidimensional variable in the Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place.md === -->

# Block.set\_data\_in\_place[](#block-set-data-in-place "Link to this heading")

Block.set\_data\_in\_place(*data: Mapping[str, ArrayLike | Scalar] | pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *combine\_multidim\_vars: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Set the data for variable(s) in the Block (in place).

    If a variable does not exist, it is created. If it does exist,
    its data is replaced and all other attributes are preserved.

    Deprecated since version 0.12.0: Use [`set_data()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data") with in\_place=True instead.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*mapping of str to array-like/scalar values or pandas.DataFrame*) – The new data to set in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim\_vars** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables in data into
          a single variable. This also has the effect of combining the
          existing multidimensional variables in the Block. If
          `False`, do not combine multidimensional variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*length 3 tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the varnames of flattened multidim variables. Only applies if
          `combine_multidim_vars=True`. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters would work for flattened
          multidim varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If the length along axis 0 of any of the array-likes in
          data does not match the Block’s observation count.
          - If data is a pandas DataFrame and combine\_multidim\_vars
          is `True`, and the dimscales of a multidimensional variable
          in the DataFrame do not match the dimscales of the
          corresponding multidimensional variable in the Block.

    Notes

    This method does not attempt to extract any idx(s) from the DataFrame.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars.md === -->

# Block.set\_vars[](#block-set-vars "Link to this heading")

Block.set\_vars(*variables: [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var") | Iterable[[Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")]*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Set variables and return this Block or a new Block.

    This method adds new Vars and/or replaces existing ones. If a Var
    by the same name already exists in the Block, it is replaced.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*Var or iterable of Var*) – Var instance(s) to set in the Block.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, return a new Block with the specified variables,
          leaving the original Block unmodified. If `True`, set the
          variables in this Block and return a reference to this Block.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified variables, or this Block if
        in\_place is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Notes

    Implicit copies are made and warings are generated if any Vars
    already belong to another Block.

Block.set\_vars\_in\_place(*variables: [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | Iterable[[Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")]*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars_in_place "Link to this definition")
