<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_file.md === -->

# Block.to\_file[](#block-to-file "Link to this heading")

Block.to\_file(*dst: str | PathLike[str] | BaseDataFile*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificWriteOptions | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *loc: str = '/'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_file "Link to this definition")
:   Write Block data (and possibly metadata) to a file.

    If the destination file dst has a known file extension that maps
    to a supported format, then the file is written in that format.
    If the destination file has a missing or unknown file extension,
    you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
    `format_override` as `dst`, or 2) use the `dst_format`
    parameter. Known writeable file extensions are stored in
    `options.writing.extensions`. Known writeable file formats
    are stored in `options.writing.formats`.

    Whether metadata is written depends on the format of output file.
    If the output file’s format has places to store metadata (such as
    in the native pwdatatools format and HDF5 format), then metadata
    is automatically included in the output file.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored
          in `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificWriteOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for writing `dst`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.writing.format_specific` sets format-specific
          writing options, but this parameter overrides the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificWriteOptions`. See the docs for a list
          of all classes that are format-specific write options. Some
          examples are `ADSWriteOptions`,
          `MDIFWriteOptions`, [`ADSWriteInvalid`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid"), and
          [`MDIFWriteInvalid`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid").
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The write location within `dst`. Only applies to file
          formats that support multiple levels of hierarchy (for
          example, native and HDF5 formats). This parameter sets the
          write location within the file’s directory-like (or tree-like)
          structure. The “root” location is ‘/’.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   * **FileExistsError** – If `dst_mode='x'` and `dst` already exists.
        * **ValueError** – If `dst_mode='a'` and append mode is not supported for the
          format of `dst`.

    Examples

    The below examples assume that `block` is an instance of
    [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block") and the following
    imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, you can
    simply input `dst` as a str, `pathlib.Path`, or
    `DataFile`. Known file
    extensions are stored in `options.writing.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> block.to_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> block.to_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # DataFile with known ext (.pwdt)
    >>> block.to_file(DataFile('/home/noise_param.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a missing or non-standard file
    extension, you can use the `dst_format` parameter. Alternatively,
    you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `dst`
    argument and use the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to
    define the destination’s format.

    ```
    >>> # no ext solved by dst_format
    >>> block.to_file('/data/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # non-standard ext solved by dst_format
    >>> block.to_file('/data/noise_param.txt', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # no ext solved by DataFile
    >>> block.to_file(DataFile('/data/noise_param', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> block.to_file('/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')  # overwrite dst if it exists
    >>> block.to_file('/noise_param.mdf', dst_mode='a') # append to dst if it exists
    >>> block.to_file('/run1.pwdt', dst_mode='a') # append to dst if it exists
    ```

    See also

    `write_file()`, `translate_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# Block.to\_pandas\_dataframe[](#block-to-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

Block.to\_pandas\_dataframe(*\**, *index: Literal['default', 'ivars'] = 'default'*, *cols\_nlevels: int = 0*, *cols\_level\_names: bool = True*, *cols\_dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *cols\_dimscales\_pref: Literal[None, 'nums', 'names'] = None*, *cols\_default\_ints: Literal[1, 0] = 1*, *cols\_default\_ints\_forced: bool = False*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a pandas DataFrame from this Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **index** (*{‘default’, ‘ivars’}, default ‘default’*) – The index to use for the rows in the returned DataFrame. If
          `'default'`, use a default pandas integer RangeIndex. If
          `'ivars'`, use the idxs (preferred) or the values of the
          independent variables (ivars) to create a pandas Index or
          MultiIndex. If `'ivars'` is the chosen option but this
          Block does not have ivars, a default integer RangeIndex is created.
        * **cols\_nlevels** (*int, default 0*) – The number of levels to use for MultiIndex cols. If `-1`,
          let the max number of dims in the Vars determine the number of
          levels. If `0`, use a flat Index for the cols (instead of
          MultiIndex). If `1`, use a MultiIndex with 1 level. If the
          value is greater than max ndim + 1, fill the extra levels with
          empty strings.
        * **cols\_level\_names** (*bool, default True*) – If `True` set the names of the levels in the returned
          MultiIndex to `'varname'`, `'i'`, `'j'`, etc. Ignored if
          the return cols Index is flat.
        * **cols\_dimscales\_delim** (*3-tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. A 3-tuple of str
          characters used for embedding dimscales into the varnames in a
          flat string columns index. The first character is the opening
          delimiter, the second is the delimiter between dimscales, and
          the third is the closing delimiter. For example, the default
          delimiters would create varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and
          `'PortZ[1]'`.
        * **cols\_dimscales\_pref** (*{None, ‘nums’, ‘names’}, optional*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The type of
          dimension scales preferred for the DataFrame columns if the
          Vars’ dims are used. If `'nums'`, use the numeric
          dimscales. If `'names'`, use the string dimscales.
          If `None` (the default), prefer numeric dimscales if they
          exist, otherwise use string dimscales. In all cases, if the
          dimscales are not present, use the integers set by
          cols\_default\_ints.
        * **cols\_default\_ints** (*{1, 0}, default 1*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The integer-based
          indexing to use for the columns in the event that any Var’s
          dimscales are not used. If `1`, use 1-based ints for the
          index labels. If `0`, use 0-based ints.
        * **cols\_default\_ints\_forced** (*bool, default False*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. If `True`, use
          the integers set by cols\_default\_ints for all columns in the
          DataFrame, even if the Var’s dimscales are present. If `False`
          prefer the dimscales if they exist.

    Returns:
    :   A pandas DataFrame with the Block’s data.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame

    Notes

    The dtypes in the DataFrame are automatically set based on the
    dtypes of the data in the Block, taking into account whether Vars
    have nulls or not.

    Examples

    Let’s create a sample Block and convert it to a pandas DataFrame
    using the default settings. Here, we create a Block with two
    ivars and three dvars. Two of the dvars are multi-dimensional:
    the `PortR` variable has two dimensions, and the `magS`
    variable has three dimensions.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Block
    >>> pd.options.display.width = 200  # all DataFrame printouts use this width
    >>> data = {
    ...     "sample": [1, 2, 1, 2],
    ...     "bias": [4.1, 4.1, 5.2, 5.2],
    ...     "Pout": [30.5, 31.2, 29.4, 30.6],
    ...     "PortR": np.random.randint(30, 60, (4, 2)),
    ...     "magS": np.random.random((4, 2, 2)),
    ... }
    >>> block = Block(data, ivarnames=["sample", "bias"])
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    Each of the 1D variables are able to fit in one column, but the
    multi-dimensional variables are split into multiple columns. The
    columns for the multi-dimensional variables are created by
    embedding default integers into the varnames. The DataFrame’s
    index is a default RangeIndex.

    Let’s create a new DataFrame from this Block once again, but this
    time with a non-default index created from the Block’s ivars.
    This creates a MultiIndex from the Block’s independent variables
    since there are two ivars.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(index="ivars")
    >>> print(df)
                 Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    sample bias
    1      4.1   30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    2      4.1   31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    1      5.2   29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    2      5.2   30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    Now, let’s explore the options for the columns. In the absence
    of dimensional metadata for the multi-dimensional variables,
    the default integers are used for the column labels. There
    are two options for the default integers: 0-based and 1-based.
    Let’s use 0-based integers.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_default_ints=0)
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR[0]  PortR[1]  magS[0,0]  magS[0,1]  magS[1,0]  magS[1,1]
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    By default square brackets and commas are used as the delimiters
    for embedding the dimension scales into the column labels for
    multi-dimensional variables. Let’s change the delimiters to
    parentheses and semicolons.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_dimscales_delim=('(', ';', ')'))
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR(1)  PortR(2)  magS(1;1)  magS(1;2)  magS(2;1)  magS(2;2)
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    Now, let’s add dimensional metadata to our multi-dimensional
    variables. This is done assigning an instance of [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")
    to the [`Var.dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims") attribute. Each instance of [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")
    may define numeric (“nums”) and/or string (“names”) dimension
    scales.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> block["PortR"].dims = Dims(ndim=2, i_nums=[5, 6], i_names=["Port5", "Port6"])
    >>> block["magS"].dims = Dims(
    ...     ndim=3,
    ...     i_nums=[3, 4],
    ...     i_names=["P3", "P4"],
    ...     j_nums=[1, 2],
    ...     j_names=["P1", "P2"],
    ... )
    ```

    Now, let’s create a pandas DataFarme using the default settings.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR[5]  PortR[6]  magS[3,1]  magS[3,2]  magS[4,1]  magS[4,2]
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    As you can see, the column labels now use the numeric dimension
    scales. We can optionally use the string dimension scales instead.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_dimscales_pref="names")
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR['Port5']  PortR['Port6']  magS['P3','P1']  magS['P3','P2']  magS['P4','P1']  magS['P4','P2']
    0       1   4.1  30.5              51              37         0.893557         0.767820         0.689058         0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2              45              44         0.513656         0.677648         0.320666         0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4              39              43         0.376806         0.278813         0.136329         0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6              42              52         0.871437         0.230952         0.506557         0.064608
    ```

    We can choose to use the default integers for the column labels
    instead of the dimension scales.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_default_ints_forced=True)
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    Instead of creating flat columns, we can create a MultiIndex.
    if we use `cols_nlevels=-1`, the columns will have as many
    levels as the maximum number of dimensions in the Vars.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1)
    >>> print(df)
    varname sample bias  Pout PortR          magS
    i                             5   6         3                   4
    j                                           1         2         1         2
    0            1  4.1  30.5    51  37  0.893557  0.767820  0.689058  0.205936
    1            2  4.1  31.2    45  44  0.513656  0.677648  0.320666  0.210212
    2            1  5.2  29.4    39  43  0.376806  0.278813  0.136329  0.987978
    3            2  5.2  30.6    42  52  0.871437  0.230952  0.506557  0.064608
    ```

    We can optionally turn off the level names in the columns if we
    are using a MultiIndex.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1, cols_level_names=False)
    >>> print(df)
      sample bias  Pout PortR          magS
                            5   6         3                   4
                                          1         2         1         2
    0      1  4.1  30.5    51  37  0.893557  0.767820  0.689058  0.205936
    1      2  4.1  31.2    45  44  0.513656  0.677648  0.320666  0.210212
    2      1  5.2  29.4    39  43  0.376806  0.278813  0.136329  0.987978
    3      2  5.2  30.6    42  52  0.871437  0.230952  0.506557  0.064608
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.update.md === -->

# Block.update[](#block-update "Link to this heading")

Block.update(*other=None*, *\*\*kwargs*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.update "Link to this definition")
:   Update the Block with Vars and/or data from another mapping.

    Parameters:
    :   * **other** (*mapping of str to VarLike, optional*) – If a VarLike object is an ArrayLike, it is assumed to be the
          Var’s data, and a Var is created. If a [`Var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var"), it is
          directly added to the Block.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Vars and/or data to add to the Block.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If any value in other is not a Var and the varname already
        exists. In that scenario, use the [`set_data()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data")
        method instead.

# Boolean.from\_name[](#boolean-from-name "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Boolean.from\_name(*name: Literal['Boolean', 'Complex32', 'Complex64', 'Complex128', 'Float32', 'Float64', 'Int8', 'Int16', 'Int32', 'Int64', 'String', 'UInt8', 'UInt16', 'UInt32', 'UInt64']*, *complex\_name: Literal['python', 'rust'] = 'python'*) → [DataType](keysight.pwdatatools.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools._core.datatypes.DataType")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Boolean.from_name "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize a concrete DataType from a string identifier.

          “python”, which uses “Complex64” and “Complex128”. The
          alternative is “rust”, which uses “Complex32” and “Complex64”.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.calc[](#module-keysight.pwdatatools.calc "Link to this heading")

The PathWave Data Tools calculation module.

The [`keysight.pwdatatools.calc`](#module-keysight.pwdatatools.calc "keysight.pwdatatools.calc") module contains functions for performing commonly-used engineering calculations.
Here are the functions available in this module:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`db_to_power`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_power.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_power "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_power")() | Convert decibels to linear power value or ratio. |
| [`db_to_voltage`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_voltage.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_voltage "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.db_to_voltage")() | Convert decibels to linear voltage value or ratio. |
| [`dbm_to_w`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.dbm_to_w.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.dbm_to_w "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.dbm_to_w")() | Convert power in dBm to watts. |
| [`deg_to_rad`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.deg_to_rad.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.deg_to_rad "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.deg_to_rad")() | Convert degrees to radians. |
| [`gamma_to_gamma`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.gamma_to_gamma.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.gamma_to_gamma "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.gamma_to_gamma")() | Convert Gamma referenced to zref to a new Gamma referenced to zref\_new. |
| [`gamma_to_z`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.gamma_to_z.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.gamma_to_z "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.gamma_to_z")() | Calculate impedance from Gamma and a reference impedance. |
| [`polar_to_rect`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.polar_to_rect.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.polar_to_rect "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.polar_to_rect")() | Return a tuple of real/imaginary from magnitude/angle. |
| [`power_to_db`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.power_to_db.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.power_to_db "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.power_to_db")() | Convert a linear power value or ratio to decibels. |
| [`rad_to_deg`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.rad_to_deg.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.rad_to_deg "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.rad_to_deg")() | Convert radians to degrees. |
| [`rect_to_polar`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.rect_to_polar.md#keysight.pwdatatools.calc.rect_to_polar "keysight.pwdatatools.calc.rect_to_polar")() | Return a tuple of magnitude/angle from real/imaginary. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.CITIReadOptions.__init__.md === -->

# CITIReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_[](#citireadoptions-init "Link to this heading")

CITIReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_(*\**, *engine\_pref: Literal['python', 'ads'] | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.CITIReadOptions.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize CITIReadOptions instance.

    Parameters:
    :   **engine\_pref** – If `"python"`, CITIfiles will be directly read using an
        engine written in Python. If `"ads"`, the CITIfile is
        converted to an intermediate ADS dataset, then the dataset is
        read using the `engine_pref` setting for ADS reading. If
        `None`, internal logic is used to determine which engine to
        use. Defaults to `None`.


---

concatenate\_blocks(*blocks: Iterable[[Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")]*, *\**, *varname\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'null'] = 'raise'*, *metadata\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'first'] = 'raise'*) → [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_blocks "Link to this definition")
:   Concatenate multiple Blocks into a single Block.

    The concatenation is performed along the observations (axis 0 for all
    data).

    Parameters:
    :   * **blocks** (*iterable of Block*) – An iterable of Blocks to concatenate.
        * **varname\_mismatch** (*{“raise”, “null”}, default “raise”*) – Determines how to handle variable name mismatches between the
          Blocks. If `"raise"`, a ValueError is raised if the variable
          names are not exactly the same for all input Blocks. If
          `"null"`, missing variable(s) are handled by inserting null
          values at the proper observations.
        * **metadata\_mismatch** (*{“raise”, “first”}, default “raise”*) – Determines how to handle metadata mismatches between the Blocks.
          If `"raise"`, a ValueError is raised if any metadata is
          mismatched. If `"first"`, the metadata from the first Block is
          used for the output Block and metadata mismatches are ignored.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block that is the concatenation of the input Blocks along
        the observations (axis 0 for all Vars).

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    Let’s demonstrate by concatenating two identical Blocks.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims, Block, Var, concatenate_blocks
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> freq_var = Var(
            [1e9, 2e9, 3e9],
            name="freq",
            dims=Dims(ndim=1, idx=[100, 200, 300]),
            role="frequency",
            unit="Hz",
            attrs={"foo": np.array([1, 2, 3])},
        )
    >>> portz_var = Var(
            np.array([50, 75, 100, 125, 150, 175]).reshape(3, 2),
            name="PortZ",
            dims=Dims(ndim=2, i_names=["P1", "P2"]),
            unit="Ohm",
            role="impedance.port",
            attrs={"spam": 42},
        )
    >>> block1 = Block(
            [freq_var, portz_var],
            name="myblock",
            ivarnames=("freq",),
            attrs={"python": 3.10},
        )
    >>> block2 = Block(
            [freq_var, portz_var],
            name="myblock",
            ivarnames=("freq",),
            attrs={"python": 3.10},
        )
    >>> block_concat = concatenate_blocks([block1, block2])
    >>> block_concat.name
    'myblock'
    >>> block_concat.attrs["python"]
    3.10
    >>> block_concat.ivarnames
    ('freq',)
    >>> block_concat["freq"].to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([1.e+09, 2.e+09, 3.e+09, 1.e+09, 2.e+09, 3.e+09])
    >>> block_concat["freq"].dims
    ```

    Now, let’s add a variable to one of the Blocks and see what happens.

    ```
    >>> block2["spam"] = Var([1, 2, 3], name="spam")
    >>> block_concat = concatenate_blocks([block1, block2])
    <raises ValueError>
    ```

    By default, a ValueError is raised if there is a variable name
    mismatch. However, we can use the `varname_mismatch` parameter to
    handle the mismatch differently.

    ```
    >>> block_concat = concatenate_blocks([block1, block2], varname_mismatch="null")
    >>> block_concat["spam"].to_numpy_ndarray()
    masked_array(data=[--, --, --, 1, 2, 3],
                 mask=[ True,  True,  True, False, False, False],
           fill_value=999999)
    ```

    See also

    [`concatenate_vars()`](keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_vars"), [`concatenate_loadpullblocks()`](keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_loadpullblocks.md#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_loadpullblocks "keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_loadpullblocks")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_loadpullblocks.md === -->

# concatenate\_loadpullblocks[](#concatenate-loadpullblocks "Link to this heading")

concatenate\_loadpullblocks(*loadpullblocks: Iterable[[LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")]*, *\**, *varname\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'null'] = 'raise'*, *metadata\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'first'] = 'raise'*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_loadpullblocks "Link to this definition")
:   Concatenate multiple LoadPullBlocks into a single LoadPullBlock.

    The concatenation is performed along the observations (axis 0 for all
    data).

    Parameters:
    :   * **loadpullblocks** (*iterable of LoadPullBlock*) – An iterable of LoadPullBlocks to concatenate.
        * **varname\_mismatch** (*{“raise”, “null”}, default “raise”*) – Determines how to handle variable name mismatches between the
          LoadPullBlocks. If `"raise"`, a ValueError is raised if the
          variable names are not exactly the same for all input
          LoadPullBlocks. If `"null"`, missing variable(s) are handled
          by inserting null values at the proper observations.
        * **metadata\_mismatch** (*{“raise”, “first”}, default “raise”*) – Determines how to handle metadata mismatches between the
          LoadPullBlocks. If `"raise"`, a ValueError is raised if any
          metadata is mismatched. If `"first"`, the metadata from the
          first LoadPullBlock is used for the output LoadPullBlock and
          metadata mismatches are ignored.

    Returns:
    :   A new LoadPullBlock that is the concatenation of the input
        LoadPullBlocks along the observations (axis 0 for all data).

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    Let’s assume we have already created LoadPUllBlocks by using the
    function
    `keysight.pwdatatools.examples.loadpull.make_swept_gamma_loadpullblock()`
    and demonstrate by concatenating two identical LoadPullBlocks. They
    are stored in Python variables called lpblock1 and lpblock2.

    ```
    >>> import keysight.pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock1
    LoadPullBlock(
        <'Gain', 'DrainEff', ... with 9 observations>,
        name='example',
        gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> lpblock2
    LoadPullBlock(
        <'Gain', 'DrainEff', ... with 9 observations>,
        name='example',
        gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Note that each LoadPullBlock has 9 observations. We can concatenate
    them into a single LoadPullBlock with 18 observations.

    ```
    >>> lpblock_concat = pwdt.concatenate_loadpullblocks([lpblock1, lpblock2])
    >>> lpblock_concat
    LoadPullBlock(
        <'Gain', 'DrainEff', ... with 18 observations>,
        name='example',
        gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Now, let’s add a variable to one of the LoadPullBlocks and see what
    happens.

    ```
    >>> lpblock1["new_var"] = pwdt.Var([1], name="new_var")
    >>> lpblock_concat = pwdt.concatenate_loadpullblocks([lpblock1, lpblock2])
    <raises ValueError>
    ```

    By default, a ValueError is raised if there is a variable name
    mismatch. However, we can use the `varname_mismatch` parameter to
    handle the mismatch differently.

    ```
    >>> lpblock_concat = pwdt.concatenate_loadpullblocks(
    ...     [lpblock1, lpblock2], varname_mismatch="null"
    ... )
    >>> lpblock_concat["new_var"].to_numpy_ndarray()
    masked_array(data=[1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, --, --, --, --, --, --, --,
                       --, --],
                 mask=[False, False, False, False, False, False, False, False,
                       False,  True,  True,  True,  True,  True,  True,  True,
                       True,  True],
           fill_value=999999)
    ```

    See also

    [`concatenate_vars()`](keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_vars"), [`concatenate_blocks()`](keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_blocks.md#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_blocks "keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_blocks")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_vars.md === -->

# concatenate\_vars[](#concatenate-vars "Link to this heading")

concatenate\_vars(*vars: Iterable[[Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")]*, *\**, *metadata\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'first'] = 'raise'*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Concatenate multiple Vars into a single Var.

    The data is concatenated along axis 0. The new Var’s name, dims, role,
    and unit are taken from the first Var in the iterable.

    Parameters:
    :   * **vars** (*iterable of Var*) – Iterable of Vars to concatenate.
        * **metadata\_mismatch** (*{‘raise’, ‘first’}, default ‘raise’*) – If ‘raise’, raise an error if any of the Vars have inconsistent
          metadata. If ‘first’, use the metadata from the first Var in the
          iterable and ignore any metadata mismatches.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If any of the Vars have inconsistent metadata and metadata\_mismatch
        is set to ‘raise’.

    See also

    [`concatenate_blocks()`](keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_blocks.md#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_blocks "keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_blocks"), [`concatenate_loadpullblocks()`](keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_loadpullblocks.md#keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_loadpullblocks "keysight.pwdatatools.concatenate_loadpullblocks")


---

*class* CSVReadOptions[](#keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions "Link to this definition")

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.__init__")(\*[, engine\_pref, pandas\_kwargs]) | Initialize CSVReadOptions instance. |
    | [`get_formats`](keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.get_formats.md#keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.get_formats "keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.get_formats")() | Return formats supported by this class. |
    | [`mapping`](keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.mapping.md#keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.mapping "keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.mapping")() | Return a dict of property names to property values. |
    | [`replace`](keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.replace.md#keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.replace "keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.replace")(\*\*kwargs) | Return a new instance with the specified properties replaced. |

# CSVWriteOptions.\_\_init\_\_[](#csvwriteoptions-init "Link to this heading")

CSVWriteOptions.\_\_init\_\_(*\**, *engine\_pref: Literal['pandas'] | None = None*, *pandas\_kwargs: dict[str, Any] | None = None*, *cols\_default\_ints: Literal[1, 0] = 1*, *cols\_default\_ints\_forced: bool = False*, *cols\_dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.CSVWriteOptions.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize CSVWriteOptions.

    Parameters:
    :   * **engine\_pref** (*{‘pandas’, None}, default None*) – Preference for writing CSV files. If `"pandas"`, use the
          pandas library. If None, use internal logic to determine the
          best engine.
        * **pandas\_kwargs** (*dict, optional*) – Keyword arguments to pass to the pandas writer. Only used if
          engine\_pref is `"pandas"`.
        * **cols\_default\_ints** (*{1, 0}, default 1*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The integer-based
          indexing to use for the columns in the event that any Var’s
          dimscales are not used. If `1`, use 1-based ints for
          the index labels. If `0`, use 0-based ints.
        * **cols\_default\_ints\_forced** (*bool, default False*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. If `True`, use
          the integers set by cols\_default\_ints for the columns even if
          the Var has dimscales. If `False`, use the dimscales if they
          exist.
        * **cols\_dimscales\_delim, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)** – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. A 3-tuple of str
          characters used for embedding dimscales into the varnames in a
          flat string columns index. The first character is the opening
          delimiter, the second is the delimiter between dimscales, and
          the third is the closing delimiter. For example, the default
          delimiters would create varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and
          `'PortZ[1]'`.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__init__.md === -->

# DataFile.\_\_init\_\_[](#datafile-init "Link to this heading")

DataFile.\_\_init\_\_(*path\_or\_obj: StrPathLike | BaseDataFile*, *format\_override: str | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize with a file path or object, and optional format override.

    The file may or may not exist on the filesystem.

    Parameters:
    :   * **path\_or\_obj** – A str, path object, or file object that points to a file
          location. The file may or may not exist.
        * **format\_override** – Optionally override the format that would be inferred by the
          file extension. Also allows to assign a format to files with
          missing file extensions. If `None`, the format is
          determined by the file extension on `path_or_obj`. Defaults
          to `None`.

    Raises:
    :   * **ValueError** – If `path_or_obj` is a directory.
          If invalid `format_override` value.
        * **TypeError** – If `path_or_obj` is wrong type.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.copy.md === -->

# DataFile.copy[](#datafile-copy "Link to this heading")

DataFile.copy(*dst: StrPathLike | BaseDataFile*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w'] = 'x'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Copy file to another location.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** – The destination as a str path, a `pathlib.Path`, or a
          `pwdatatools.BaseDataFile`.
        * **dst\_mode** – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, both modes yield the same behavior. However, if
          attempting to copy to a pre-existing file, `'w'` overwrites
          the existing file, and `'x'` results in a FileExistsError.
          Defaults to `'x'`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If `dst_mode` is not `'x'` or `'w'`.
        If `dst_mode='x'` and `dst` already exists.


---

# DataFile.find\_diffs[](#datafile-find-diffs "Link to this heading")

DataFile.find\_diffs(*other\_datafile: str | PathLike[str] | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.find_diffs "Link to this definition")

    Return type:
    :   *list* of *str*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If file is not Unicode or ASCII.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md === -->

# DataFile[](#datafile "Link to this heading")

*class* DataFile[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "Link to this definition")
:   An object that represents a datafile on a filesystem.

    The datafile may or may not exist.

    Parameters:
    :   * **path\_or\_obj** – A str, path object, or file object that points to a file
          location. The file may or may not exist.
        * **format\_override** – Optionally override the format that would be inferred by the
          file extension. Also allows to assign a format to files with
          missing file extensions. If `None`, the format is
          determined by the file extension on `path_or_obj`. Defaults
          to `None`.

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    You can create a DataFile by passing in a str path, a
    `pathlib.Path`, or another DataFile object.

    ```
    >>> # using a str path
    >>> datafile = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> # using a pathlib.Path
    >>> datafile = DataFile(Path('/home/s_param.s4p'))
    >>> # using another DataFile
    >>> datafile2 = DataFile(datafile)
    ```

    If the file has a known file extension, the format is inferred
    automatically.

    ```
    >>> datafile = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> datafile.get_format()
    'touchstone'
    ```

    If the file has a missing or unknown file extension, you can
    specify the `format_override` parameter.

    ```
    >>> datafile = DataFile('/home/noise.txt', format_override='mdif')
    >>> datafile.get_format()
    'mdif'
    ```

    Raises:
    :   * **ValueError** – If `path_or_obj` is a directory.
          If invalid `format_override` value.
        * **TypeError** – If `path_or_obj` is wrong type.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`ext`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.ext.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.ext "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.ext") | The file extension (an empty str if missing extension). |
    | [`folder`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.folder.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.folder "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.folder") | Full path to folder containing DataFile. |
    | [`format_override`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.format_override.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.format_override "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.format_override") | Optional str format which takes precedence over the file extension. |
    | [`name`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.name "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.name") | The file's stem plus extension, if present. |
    | [`path`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.path.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.path "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.path") | The full path to the file. |
    | [`stem`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.stem.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.stem "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.stem") | Base filename (filename without extension). |
    | [`suffix`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.suffix.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.suffix "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.suffix") | File extension (alias for ext property). |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__init__")(path\_or\_obj[, format\_override]) | Initialize with a file path or object, and optional format override. |
    | [`copy`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.copy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.copy "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.copy")(dst[, dst\_mode]) | Copy file to another location. |
    | [`delete`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.delete.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.delete "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.delete")([missing\_ok]) | Delete file on disk, if it exists. |
    | [`exists`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.exists.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.exists "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.exists")() | Return bool indicating if file exists. |
    | [`find_diffs`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.find_diffs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.find_diffs "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.find_diffs")(other\_datafile) | Return list of diffs found comparing this file to another. |
    | [`get_format`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format")() | Return format of file. |
    | [`has_format`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.has_format.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.has_format "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.has_format")(fmt) | Return boolean indicating whether instance has format provided. |
    | [`has_modtime_match`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.has_modtime_match.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.has_modtime_match "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.has_modtime_match")(otherfile) | Return bool indicating whether this file has same modtime as other. |
    | [`is_ads`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_ads.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_ads "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_ads")() | Return bool indicating whether file is an ADS dataset file. |
    | [`is_citi`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_citi.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_citi "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_citi")() | Return bool indicating whether file is a CITIFile. |
    | [`is_csv`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_csv.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_csv "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_csv")() | Return bool indicating whether file is a CSV file. |
    | [`is_farfieldio`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_farfieldio.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_farfieldio "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_farfieldio")() | Return bool indicating whether file is a FarfieldIO file. |
    | [`is_hfss_ffd`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_hfss_ffd.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_hfss_ffd "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_hfss_ffd")() | Return bool indicating whether file is an HFSS FFD file. |
    | [`is_loadpull`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_loadpull.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_loadpull "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_loadpull")() | Return bool indicating whether file is a loadpull file. |
    | [`is_mdif`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_mdif.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_mdif "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_mdif")() | Return bool indicating whether file is an MDIF file. |
    | [`is_mdm`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_mdm.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_mdm "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_mdm")() | Return bool indicating whether file is an MDM file. |
    | [`is_native`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_native.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_native "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_native")() | Return bool indicating whether file is a native pwdatatools file. |
    | [`is_s2pmdif`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_s2pmdif.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_s2pmdif "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_s2pmdif")() | Return bool indicating whether file is an S2PMDIF file. |
    | [`is_same`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_same.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_same "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_same")(other\_datafile) | Return True if other datafile has same contents as instance. |
    | [`is_smatrixio`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_smatrixio.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_smatrixio "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_smatrixio")() | Return bool indicating whether file is a SMatrixIO file. |
    | [`is_systemvue`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_systemvue.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_systemvue "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_systemvue")() | Return bool indicating whether file is a SystemVue workspace. |
    | [`is_touchstone`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_touchstone.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_touchstone "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_touchstone")() | Return bool indicating whether is a Touchstone S-parameter file. |
    | [`lines`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.lines.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.lines "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.lines")() | Return lines from file, excluding blank lines. |
    | [`modtime`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.modtime.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.modtime "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.modtime")() | Return the modification time of the file (nan if file does not exist). |
    | [`modtime_datetime`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.modtime_datetime.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.modtime_datetime "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.modtime_datetime")() | Return a human-readable modtime (None if file does not exist). |
    | [`read_as_block`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_block "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_block")([loc, data, format\_specific, ...]) | Read the DataFile and return a new instance of Block. |
    | [`read_as_group`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_group "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_group")([loc, data, format\_specific, ...]) | Read the DataFile and return a new instance of Group. |
    | [`read_as_loadpullblock`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_loadpullblock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_loadpullblock "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_loadpullblock")(\*\*kwargs) | Read the DataFile and return a LoadPullBlock. |
    | [`remove`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.remove.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.remove "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.remove")([missing\_ok]) | Remove file on disk, if it exists. |
    | [`set_modtime`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.set_modtime.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.set_modtime "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.set_modtime")(new\_modtime) | Set the modification time (modtime) of file. |
    | [`translate`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.translate.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.translate "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.translate")(dst[, dst\_mode, dst\_format, iffs]) | Translate a DataFile to another file format. |
    | [`tree`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.tree.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.tree "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.tree")() | Return a tree view of this DataFile, similar to a directory tree. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_block.md === -->

# DataFile.read\_as\_block[](#datafile-read-as-block "Link to this heading")

DataFile.read\_as\_block(*loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_block "Link to this definition")
:   Read the DataFile and return a new instance of Block.

    If the DataFile has a missing or unknown file extension, you
    must specify the `Datafile.format_override` parameter. Known
    readable file extensions are stored in
    `options.reading.extensions`. Known readable file formats
    are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **loc** – The location within the DataFile to return as a Block. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire DataFile is read. Otherwise,
          it represents some portion of the DataFile. This may be the
          name of a single datablock (for example, in an MDIF or ADS
          dataset), or the full HDF5 path to a block (an IvITrace) for
          a .pwdt file. Defaults to `'/'`.
        * **data** – A bool that determines whether to read the data. If `False`,
          only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata can be useful
          for quickly examining information about the variables. Defaults
          to `True`.
        * **format\_specific** – By default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets all format-
          specific reading options, but this parameter can override the
          global options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are [`ADSReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions"),
          [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions"), and [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **iffs** – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing. Defaults to `False`.
        * **varnames** – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If data cannot be represented as a single Block.

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following import:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the DataFile has a known file extension, and you want to read
    the entire file, you can call `read_as_block` with any arguments.
    Known file extensions are stored in
    `options.reading.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a path with known ext (.lpd)
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/home/device.lpd')
    >>> block = datafile.read_as_block()
    ```

    If the DataFile has a missing or unknown file extension, you must
    specify the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter.

    ```
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/data/noise.txt', format_override='mdif')
    >>> block = datafile.read_as_block()
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter.
    You have to know the name of the part that you want to read. A
    Block is always returned. If the contents of the part cannot be
    represented by a single Block, a ValueError is raised.

    ```
    >>> block = datafile.read_as_block(loc='/SP1.SP')
    ```

    See also

    `from_file()`, [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block"), [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_group.md === -->

# DataFile.read\_as\_group[](#datafile-read-as-group "Link to this heading")

DataFile.read\_as\_group(*loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_group "Link to this definition")
:   Read the DataFile and return a new instance of Group.

    If the DataFile file has a missing or unknown file extension, you
    must specify the `DataFile.format_override`. Known readable file
    extensions are stored in `options.reading.extensions`. Known
    readable file formats are stored in
    `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **loc** – The location within the source file to return as a
          [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group"). If `'/'` (the “root”), the entire DataFile
          is read. Otherwise, it represents some portion of the file.
          This may be the name of a single datablock (for example in an
          MDIF or ADS dataset). Or, it may be the full HDF5 path in a
          .pwdt file. Defaults to `'/'`, which results in the full
          file contents returned as a [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group").
        * **dtypes** – Datatypes used for numpy ndarrays. Defaults to `None`, which
          results in automatic determination of the dtypes.
        * **data** – A bool that determines whether to read the data. If `False`,
          only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata can be useful
          for quickly examining the variable names and hierarchy.
          Defaults to `True`.
        * **format\_specific** – Defines the format-specific reading options. By default, the
          global option `options.reading.format_specific` sets
          all format-specific reading options, but this parameter can
          override the global options. If provided, it must be an
          instance of `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs
          for a list of all classes that can act as format-specific read
          options. Some examples are [`ADSReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions"),
          [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions"), and [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **iffs** – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing. Defaults to `False`.
        * **varnames** – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following import:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the DataFile has a known file extension, and you want to read
    the entire file, you can simply call `read_as_group` without any
    arguments. Known file extensions are stored in
    `pwdatatools.options.reading.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> group = datafile.read_as_group()
    ```

    If the file has a missing or unknown file extension, specify the
    DataFile’s `format_override` parameter.

    ```
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/noise.txt', format_override='mdif')
    >>> group = datafile.read_as_group()
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter. You
    have to know the name of the part that you want to read from `src`.
    A Group will always be returned, even if the contents could be
    contained within a single Block (the Block is placed into a Group and
    the Group is returned).

    ```
    >>> # partial read of ADS dataset returns Group containing one Block
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> group = datafile.read_as_group(loc='/SP1.SP')
    ```

    See also

    `from_file()`, [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group"), [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_loadpullblock.md === -->

# DataFile.read\_as\_loadpullblock[](#datafile-read-as-loadpullblock "Link to this heading")

DataFile.read\_as\_loadpullblock(*\*\*kwargs*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_loadpullblock "Link to this definition")
:   Read the DataFile and return a LoadPullBlock.

    The Datafile must have a known file extension that is associated
    with Keysight, Focus, or Maury load pull files. Known readable load
    pull file extensions are stored in
    `options.reading.format_to_extension_map['loadpull']`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions*) – Defines the format-specific reading options. By default, the
          global option `options.reading.format_specific.loadpull`
          sets format-specific loadpull reading options, but this
          parameter can override the global options. If provided, it
          must be an instance of `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See
          the docs for a list of all classes that can act as format-
          specific read options. Some examples are
          [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions"), and [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[RoleLike, str]*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    Using all the default options:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile("/data/loadpull.cst")
    >>> lpblock = datafile.read_as_loadpullblock()
    ```

    Modifying global options to control how variables are named and
    which variables are derived from a and b waves:

    ```
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.varnames["power.delivered.load"] = "Pout"
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.format_specific.loadpull.derived_vars.ampm = False
    >>> lpblock = datafile.read_as_loadpullblock() # uses global options
    ```

    Passing in a dict of roles to varnames and an instance of
    [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars") to override the global settings:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = datafile.read_as_loadpullblock(
    ...     "/data/loadpull.cst",
    ...     varnames={'gamma.load': 'GL'},
    ...     format_specific=pwdt.LoadPullDerivedVars(ampm=False),
    ... )
    ```

    Notes

    The indexing in the pandas DataFrame always uses `'ivars-idxs'`
    as the rows index and `'flat'` column index. The global
    indexing options are ignored.

    See also

    [`LoadPullBlock`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock"), [`LoadPullBlock.from_file()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.translate.md === -->

# DataFile.translate[](#datafile-translate "Link to this heading")

DataFile.translate(*dst: str | PathLike[str] | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.translate "Link to this definition")
:   Translate a DataFile to another file format.

    The destination format can be set explicitly using an optional
    keyword argument. However, if the `dst` has a recognized
    file extension, its format can be automatically inferred and
    the keyword argument isn’t needed.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path. If `dst_format=None`,
          attempts are made to ascertain the format by examining the
          destination file’s extension. Known file extensions are
          stored in `options.writing.extensions`.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a
          pre-existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file.
          `'a'` appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored
          in `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.

    Notes

    This method always uses the global format-specific read and
    write options. The only way to override the global options is to
    perform translation as a two-step process (read then write).

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the datafile’s [`get_format()`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format") is able to be determined and
    the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, simply
    input `dst` and nothing else.

    ```
    >>> src = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> src.translate('/home/s_param.ds')  # known extensions (.s4p and .ds)
    >>> src.translate(Path('/home/noise.mdf')) # known extensions (.s4p and .mdf)
    ```

    If the the destination file `dst` has a missing or unknown file
    extension, use the `dst_format` parameter or a DataFile with
    `format_override` set.

    ```
    >>> src = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> # using dst_format:
    >>> src.translate('/home/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> using a DataFile as dst:
    >>> dst = DataFile('/home/noise', format_override='mdif')
    >>> src.translate(dst)
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> src = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> src.translate('/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')  # overwrite dst, if it exists
    >>> src.translate('/s_param.mdf', dst_mode='a') # append to dst, if it exists
    ```

    See also

    `translate_file()`, `read_file()`, `write_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.tree.md === -->

# DataFile.tree[](#datafile-tree "Link to this heading")

DataFile.tree() → str[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.tree "Link to this definition")
:   Return a tree view of this DataFile, similar to a directory tree.

    In the tree, each Group is represented as a “folder” and each
    Block is represented as a “file”. The tree is returned as a string
    with newlines separating each level of the tree. The tree is
    indented to show the hierarchy. The string is meant to be printed
    to the console or shown in a GUI, not for programmatic use.

    Returns:
    :   Representation of the DataFile as a tree.

    Return type:
    :   *str*

    Notes

    This method scans the datafile to build the tree. Whenever possible,
    it does this without reading the data. The speed of the scan depends
    on the file format and the size of the file. For binary file formats,
    the scan can be very fast, even for large files. For text-based file
    formats, the scan can be slower.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/home/s_param_and_HB.ds')
    >>> print(datafile.tree())
    <[/] Group 's_param_and_HB'>
    ├── <[0] Block 'Sweep2.Sweep1.SP1.SP' with 5 Vars>
    ├── <[1] Block 'HB1.HB' with 4 Vars>
    └── <[2] Block 'aele_0.HB1.HB' with 2 Vars>
    ```


---

*classmethod* DataType.from\_name(*name: Literal['Boolean', 'Complex32', 'Complex64', 'Complex128', 'Float32', 'Float64', 'Int8', 'Int16', 'Int32', 'Int64', 'String', 'UInt8', 'UInt16', 'UInt32', 'UInt64']*, *complex\_name: Literal['python', 'rust'] = 'python'*) → [DataType](keysight.pwdatatools.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools._core.datatypes.DataType")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataType.from_name "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.__init__.md === -->

# Dims.\_\_init\_\_[](#dims-init "Link to this heading")

Dims.\_\_init\_\_(*\**, *ndim: int*, *idx: DimScaleLike | None = None*, *i\_nums: DimScaleLike | None = None*, *i\_names: DimScaleLike | None = None*, *j\_nums: DimScaleLike | None = None*, *j\_names: DimScaleLike | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize the Dims object.

    Parameters:
    :   * **ndim** (*int*) – The number of dimensions.
        * **idx** (*unsigned integer array-like, optional*) – The scale along the first dimension (also known as axis 0 or the
          “shared” dimension).
        * **i\_nums** (*unsigned integer array-like, optional*) – The numeric scale for the second dimension (also known as the
          “i” dimension).
        * **i\_names** (*string array-like, optional*) – The string scale for the second dimension (also known as the
          “i” dimension).
        * **j\_nums** (*unsigned integer array-like, optional*) – The numeric scale for the third dimension (also known as the
          “j” dimension).
        * **j\_names** (*string array-like, optional*) – The string scale for the third dimension (also known as the
          “j” dimension).

    Examples

    Let’s make a 1D Var with the default empty Dims.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> x = Var([10, 12, 13], name='x')
    >>> x.dims
    Dims(ndim=1)
    >>> x.has_dims()
    True
    ```

    Initializing a 2D Var or 3D Var without explicitly providing a
    dims argument also results in empty Dims, just like the 1D case.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> data_2d = np.random.random(6).reshape((3, 2))
    >>> var_2d = Var(data_2d, name='var_2d')
    >>> var_2d.dims
    Dims(ndim=2)
    >>> var_2d.has_dims()
    True
    >>> data_3d = np.random.random(20).reshape((5, 2, 2))
    >>> var_3d = Var(data_3d, name='var_3d')
    >>> var_3d.dims
    Dims(ndim=3)
    >>> var_3d.has_dims()
    True
    ```

    Now, let’s add an idx to the Dims of a 1D Var.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=1, idx=[1, 2, 3])
    >>> x = Var([10, 12, 13], name='x', dims=dims)
    >>> x.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=1,
        idx=[1, 2, 3],
    )
    ```

    Now, let’s initialize a 2D Var with dims that include i\_names,
    which enables indexing along the “i” dimension using strings
    instead of integers.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=2, i_names=['P1', 'P2'])
    >>> var_2d_dims = Var(data_2d, name='var_2d_dims', dims=dims)
    >>> var_2d_dims.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=2,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=None,
        i_names=['P1', 'P2'],
    )
    ```

    Below, we initialize a 3D Var with dims that include i\_nums,
    which enables indexing along the “i” and “j” dimensions using
    arbitrary integers, rather than just 0-based or 1-based integers.

    ```
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=3, i_nums=[3, 4], j_nums=[7, 8])
    >>> var_3d_dims = Var(data_3d, name='s', dims=dims)
    >>> var_3d_dims.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=3,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=[3, 4],
        i_names=None,
        j_nums=[7, 8],
        j_names=None,
    )
    ```


---

# Dims.keep\_where[](#dims-keep-where "Link to this heading")

Dims.keep\_where(*condition: NDArray[np.bool\_]*) → [Dims](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.keep_where "Link to this definition")
:   Create a new Dims object keeping scale values where the condition is True.

    Along each dimension of the input condition, if any value is True, keep that
    scale value in the returned Dims. If all values are False, drop that scale value.

    Parameters:
    :   **condition** (*numpy.ndarray*) – A boolean array with a shape that is compatible with the Dims object.

    Returns:
    :   The new Dims object.

    Return type:
    :   [*Dims*](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md === -->

# Dims[](#dims "Link to this heading")

*class* Dims[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "Link to this definition")
:   Dimensional metadata for a [`Var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var").

    This class stores metadata for the “idx”, “i” and “j” dimensions,
    which correspond to the first, second, and third dimensions of a Var,
    respectively.

    This object is immutable. To modify a Var’s Dims, create a new Dims
    object with the desired changes and assign it to the Var’s dims property.
    For example, see the [`Dims.replace()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.replace.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.replace "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.replace") method.

    Examples

    Let’s make a 1D Var with the default empty Dims.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> x = Var([10, 12, 13], name='x')
    >>> x.dims
    Dims(ndim=1)
    >>> x.has_dims()
    False
    ```

    Initializing a 2D Var or 3D Var without explicitly providing a
    dims argument also results in empty Dims, just like the 1D case.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> data_2d = np.random.random(6).reshape((3, 2))
    >>> var_2d = Var(data_2d, name='var_2d')
    >>> var_2d.dims
    Dims(ndim=2)
    >>> var_2d.has_dims()
    False
    >>> data_3d = np.random.random(20).reshape((5, 2, 2))
    >>> var_3d = Var(data_3d, name='var_3d')
    >>> var_3d.dims
    Dims(ndim=3)
    >>> var_3d.has_dims()
    False
    ```

    Now, let’s add an idx to the Dims of a 1D Var.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=1, idx=[1, 2, 3])
    >>> x = Var([10, 12, 13], name='x', dims=dims)
    >>> x.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=1,
        idx=[1, 2, 3],
    )
    ```

    Now, let’s initialize a 2D Var with dims that include i\_names,
    which enables indexing along the “i” dimension using strings
    instead of integers.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=2, i_names=['P1', 'P2'])
    >>> var_2d_dims = Var(data_2d, name='var_2d_dims', dims=dims)
    >>> var_2d_dims.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=2,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=None,
        i_names=['P1', 'P2'],
    )
    ```

    Below, we initialize a 3D Var with dims that include i\_nums,
    which enables indexing along the “i” and “j” dimensions using
    arbitrary integers, rather than just 0-based or 1-based integers.

    ```
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=3, i_nums=[3, 4], j_nums=[7, 8])
    >>> var_3d_dims = Var(data_3d, name='s', dims=dims)
    >>> var_3d_dims.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=3,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=[3, 4],
        i_names=None,
        j_nums=[7, 8],
        j_names=None,
    )
    ```

    See also

    [`Var.select()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select")
    :   Index a Var using its Dims.

    [`Var.dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims")
    :   Get or set the Dims for a Var

    [`Var.has_dims()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_dims")
    :   Return True if a Var has non-empty Dims.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`i_names`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.i_names.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.i_names "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.i_names") | Get i\_names as a string DimScale, or None if it is not set. |
    | [`i_nums`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.i_nums.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.i_nums "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.i_nums") | Get the i\_nums scale as an unsigned integer DimScale, or None if it is not set. |
    | [`idx`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.idx.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.idx "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.idx") | Get the idx scale as an unsigned integer DimScale, or None if it is not set. |
    | [`j_names`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.j_names.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.j_names "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.j_names") | Get the j\_names scale as a string DimScale, or None if it is not set. |
    | [`j_nums`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.j_nums.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.j_nums "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.j_nums") | Get the j\_nums scale as an unsigned integer DimScale, or None if it is not set. |
    | [`ndim`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.ndim.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.ndim "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.ndim") | Get the number of dimensions. |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.__init__")(\*, ndim[, idx, i\_nums, i\_names, ...]) | Initialize the Dims object. |

# Dims.partial\_shape[](#dims-partial-shape "Link to this heading")

Dims.partial\_shape() → tuple[int, ...][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.partial_shape "Link to this definition")
:   Return shape of the Dims object, with any unknown dimension sizes equal to None.

    This is needed because Dims may not have scales along all dimensions.
    If there are no scales along a dimension, the size is unknown and set
    to None.

    Returns:
    :   The shape of the Dims object.

    Return type:
    :   *tuple* of *int*


---

:   Create a new Dims object with the specified attribute(s) replaced.

    Parameters:
    :   **kwargs** (*Any*) – The attributes to replace. Must be one or more of the following:
        - ndim : int
        - idx : optional unsigned integer array-like
        - i\_nums : optional unsigned integer array-like
        - i\_names : optional string array-like
        - j\_nums : optional unsigned integer array-like
        - j\_names : optional string array-like

    :   The new Dims object.
