# DimScale[](#dimscale "Link to this heading")

*class* DimScale[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale "Link to this definition")
:   An object representing a index along a single dimension of a Var.

    This object can be used for indexing into Var objects. This object is
    immutable, meaning that once it is created, its values and name cannot be changed.

    See also

    [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`name`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.name "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.name") | Get the name of the DimScale. |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.__init__")(values, /, \*[, name]) | Initialize an instance of DimScale. |
    | [`copy`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.copy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.copy "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.copy")() | Return a copy of this DimScale instance. |
    | [`from_pandas_index`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.from_pandas_index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.from_pandas_index "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.from_pandas_index")(index) | Return a DimScale instance from a pandas Index. |
    | [`has_names_values`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.has_names_values.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.has_names_values "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.has_names_values")() | Return bool indicating whether the values are string. |
    | [`has_nums_values`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.has_nums_values.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.has_nums_values "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.has_nums_values")() | Return bool indicating whether the values are numeric (unsigned integer). |
    | [`rename`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.rename.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.rename "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.rename")(name) | Return a new DimScale with the specified name. |
    | [`to_numpy_ndarray`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.to_numpy_ndarray.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.to_numpy_ndarray "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.to_numpy_ndarray")() | Return a numpy ndarray from this DimScale. |
    | [`view_values`](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.view_values.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.view_values "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.view_values")(\*, short) | Return a string representation of the DimScale's values. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.__init__.md === -->

# Expr.\_\_init\_\_[](#expr-init "Link to this heading")

Expr.\_\_init\_\_(*input: InputLike*, *ops: OpsSequence | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize instance of Expr.

    Parameters:
    :   * **input** (*array-like, scalar, or Expr*) – If a str, it is interpreted as a name of a variable or expression.
          Otherwise, it is interpreted as an array-like or scalar value.
        * **ops** (*sequence of Operation, optional*) – The operations to apply to the input.

    Notes

    The input and operations are stored as attributes of the Expr. If the input
    is a str, it is stored as a string. If the input is an array-like or scalar
    value, it is stored as a NumPy ndarray. The operations are stored as a tuple
    of Operation objects.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.md === -->

# Expr[](#expr "Link to this heading")

*class* Expr[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr "Link to this definition")
:   An object that represents an expression.

    This object stores an input and a list of operations to be performed
    on that input. Expressions are stored in [`Block.exprs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs") attribute
    in an instance of [`ExprsDict`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict"). For more information and examples,
    see [Use the Block Class](../../howto/use_block_class.md#use-block-class).

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`input`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.input.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.input "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.input") | Return the input variable or expression. |
    | [`ops`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.ops.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.ops "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.ops") | Return the operations. |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.__init__")(input[, ops]) | Initialize instance of Expr. |
    | [`abs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.abs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.abs "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.abs")() | Return an expression that represents the absolute value of the expression. |
    | [`angle`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.angle.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.angle "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.angle")(\*[, deg]) | Return an expression that represents the angle of the expression. |
    | [`copy`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.copy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.copy "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.copy")() | Return a deep copy of the Expr. |
    | [`dB`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.dB.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.dB "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.dB")(\*[, power]) | Return an expression that represents the decibel of the expression. |
    | [`decibel`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.decibel.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.decibel "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.decibel")(\*[, power]) | Return an expression that represents the decibel of the expression. |
    | [`eval_as_numpy_ndarray`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.eval_as_numpy_ndarray.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.eval_as_numpy_ndarray "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.eval_as_numpy_ndarray")([block]) | Return the result of the expression as a NumPy ndarray. |
    | [`imag`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.imag.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.imag "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.imag")() | Return an expression that represents the imaginary part of the expression. |
    | [`mag`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.mag.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.mag "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.mag")() | Return an expression that represents the magnitude of the expression. |
    | [`real`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.real.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.real "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.real")() | Return an expression that represents the real part of the expression. |


---

# ExprsDict[](#exprsdict "Link to this heading")

*class* ExprsDict[](#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict "Link to this definition")
:   A mapping of string names (keys) to [`Expr`](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr") objects (values).

    This object stores expressions for [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block"), stored in the [`Block.exprs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs") attribute.
    For more information and examples, see [Use the Block Class](../../howto/use_block_class.md#use-block-class). It behaves like a
    type-restricted dict.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`block`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.block "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.block") | Return the block. |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.__init__")([mapping]) | Initialize instance of ExprsDict. |
    | [`clear`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.clear.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.clear "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.clear")() |  |
    | [`copy`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.copy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.copy "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.copy")() | Return a deep copy of the ExprsDict. |
    | [`get`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.get.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.get "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.get")(k[,d]) |  |
    | [`items`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.items.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.items "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.items")() |  |
    | [`keys`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.keys.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.keys "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.keys")() |  |
    | [`pop`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.pop.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.pop "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.pop")(k[,d]) | If key is not found, d is returned if given, otherwise KeyError is raised. |
    | [`popitem`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.popitem.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.popitem "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.popitem")() | as a 2-tuple; but raise KeyError if D is empty. |
    | [`setdefault`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.setdefault.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.setdefault "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.setdefault")(k[,d]) |  |
    | [`update`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.update.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.update "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.update")([E, ]\*\*F) | If E present and has a .keys() method, does: for k in E.keys(): D[k] = E[k] If E present and lacks .keys() method, does: for (k, v) in E: D[k] = v In either case, this is followed by: for k, v in F.items(): D[k] = v |
    | [`values`](keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.values.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.values "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.values")() |  |

:   as a 2-tuple; but raise KeyError if D is empty.

# ExprsDict.setdefault[](#exprsdict-setdefault "Link to this heading")

---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.update.md === -->

# ExprsDict.update[](#exprsdict-update "Link to this heading")

ExprsDict.update([*E*, ]*\*\*F*) → None.  Update D from mapping/iterable E and F.[](#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.update "Link to this definition")
:   If E present and has a .keys() method, does: for k in E.keys(): D[k] = E[k]
    If E present and lacks .keys() method, does: for (k, v) in E: D[k] = v
    In either case, this is followed by: for k, v in F.items(): D[k] = v

# Grid.apply[](#grid-apply "Link to this heading")

Grid.apply(*dataframe: DataFrame*, *grid\_varname: str*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.apply "Link to this definition")
:   Apply grid to DataFrame, returning new DataFrame.

    The input DataFrame must have an integer index (and not a
    MultiIndex). The output DataFrame will have an integer index with
    the same index name as the input (but not the same index values,
    unless the input and output DataFrames happen to have the same
    number of rows).

    Parameters:
    :   * **dataframe** (*DataFrame*) – DataFrame to be gridded.
        * **grid\_varname** (*str*) – Name of complex grid variable.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.from_gridded_series.md === -->

# Grid.from\_gridded\_series[](#grid-from-gridded-series "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Grid.from\_gridded\_series(*gridded\_series: Series*) → [Grid](keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.gridding.Grid")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.from_gridded_series "Link to this definition")
:   Create a Grid instance from an already-gridded Series.

    The grid will be calculated from the extents of the gamma Series.
    The number of points in the grid will be determined by the number
    of unique values in the Series.

    Since the coord\_system is unknown, first ‘rect’ is attempted, then
    ‘polar’.

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the gridded series does not meet the requirements for
        being considered gridded.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__init__.md === -->

# Group.\_\_init\_\_[](#group-init "Link to this heading")

Group.\_\_init\_\_(*members: Iterable[Member] | None = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize an instance of Group.

    Parameters:
    :   * **members** (*iterable of Blocks and/or Groups, optional*) – An iterable containing objects which will become members, or
          children, of the Group. These may be Blocks or other Groups.
          Note that there are several subtypes of Block, such as
          LoadPullBlock, which are also supported.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the Group. If not specified, the name is set to
          an empty string.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Group-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.

    Examples

    Initialize a Group with two child Blocks:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> block1 = pwdt.Block(name='block1')
    >>> block2 = pwdt.Block(name='block2')
    >>> group1 = pwdt.Group([block1, block2], name='group1')
    ```

    Initialize a Group with two child Blocks and one child Group:

    ```
    >>> group2 = pwdt.Group([block1, block2, group1], name='group2')
    ```

    See also

    `read_file_as_group()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.attrs.md === -->

# Group.attrs[](#group-attrs "Link to this heading")

*property* Group.attrs*: [AttrsDict](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools._core.properties.AttrsDict")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.attrs "Link to this definition")
:   A dict of arbitrary attributes (metadata).

    The attributes are stored in an [`AttrsDict`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict"), which is a
    type-restricted dict. The keys must be str, and the values must
    be one of the following types: str, int, float, complex, bool,
    None, `numpy.ndarray`, dict, or list. Nested dicts and
    lists are also supported.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.copy.md === -->

# Group.copy[](#group-copy "Link to this heading")

Group.copy(*deep: bool = True*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Make a copy of this Group, returning a new instance of Group.

    Parameters:
    :   **deep** – When `True`, all members are deepcopied, so that the new
        Group is completely independent from self. When `False`, all
        members in the new Group are the same objects in self (only
        references to the members are copied… this means changes made
        to members in the new object will also be reflected in the
        original object). Defaults to `True`.

    Returns:
    :   A new Group instance with either the same members as this
        Group, or copies (depending on setting of deep).

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Notes

    The Group’s metadata stored in attrs is always deep-copied,
    regardless of the value of deep.opied regardless of
    the value of deep. Defaults to `True`.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md === -->

# Group.fill\_membernames[](#group-fill-membernames "Link to this heading")

Group.fill\_membernames(*\**, *recursive: bool = False*, *block\_prefix: str = 'B'*, *group\_prefix: str = 'G'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "Link to this definition")
:   Rename members with empty names (in place).

    The new names are unique and created by appending a unique integer
    to a prefix.

    Parameters:
    :   * **recursive** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, set empty names for all members recursively
          (including members inside child Groups). If `False`, do
          not set empty names for the members inside child Groups.
        * **block\_prefix** (*str, default ‘B’*) – The prefix to use for auto-naming Blocks with empty names.
          The new blocknames are created by appending an integer to the
          prefix. For example, if `block_prefix='B'`, then the
          first auto-named Block will be named `'B0'`. The integer
          gets incremented for each auto-named Block within the same
          Group and gets reset to 0 for each new child Group.
        * **group\_prefix** (*str, default ‘G’*) – The prefix to use for auto-naming Groups with empty names.
          The new groupnames are created by appending an integer to the
          prefix. For example, if `group_prefix='G'`, then the
          first auto-named Group will be named `'G0'`. The integer
          gets incremented for each auto-named Group within the same
          Group and gets reset to 0 for each new child Group.

    Return type:
    :   *None*


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames.md === -->

# Group.filled\_membernames[](#group-filled-membernames "Link to this heading")

Group.filled\_membernames(*\**, *recursive: bool = False*, *block\_prefix: str = 'B'*, *group\_prefix: str = 'G'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames "Link to this definition")
:   Context manager to temporarily fill empty membernames (in place).

    The new names are unique and created by appending a unique integer
    to a prefix.

    This is a context manager version of the [`fill_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames")
    method, and it restores the original membernames after the
    context exits.

    Parameters:
    :   * **recursive** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, set empty names for all members recursively
          (including members inside child Groups). If `False`, do
          not set empty names for the members inside child Groups.
        * **block\_prefix** (*str, default ‘B’*) – The prefix to use for auto-naming Blocks with empty names.
          The new blocknames are created by appending an integer to the
          prefix. For example, if `block_prefix='B'`, then the
          first auto-named Block will be named `'B0'`. The integer
          gets incremented for each auto-named Block within the same
          Group and gets reset to 0 for each new child Group.
        * **group\_prefix** (*str, default ‘G’*) – The prefix to use for auto-naming Groups with empty names.
          The new groupnames are created by appending an integer to the
          prefix. For example, if `group_prefix='G'`, then the
          first auto-named Group will be named `'G0'`. The integer
          gets incremented for each auto-named Group within the same
          Group and gets reset to 0 for each new child Group.

    Yields:
    :   *None*

    See also

    [`fill_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten.md === -->

# Group.flatten[](#group-flatten "Link to this heading")

Group.flatten(*\**, *fill\_membernames: bool = True*, *hierarchical\_membernames: bool = True*, *sep: str = '.'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten "Link to this definition")
:   Flatten child Groups so that only Blocks remain as members.

    Note that this method modifies the Group in-place and cannot be
    undone. Optionally rename Blocks with hierarchical names using
    the `sep` parameter.

    Parameters:
    :   * **fill\_membernames** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, fill empty member names using the default naming
          scheme. If `False`, leave empty member names as empty
          strings. For more control over renaming members with empty
          names, see methods [`fill_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames") and the
          related context manager [`filled_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames").
        * **hierarchical\_membernames** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, use hierarchical names for all members. If
          `False`, use the original names.
        * **sep** (*str, default ‘.’*) – String separator used as a delimiter when combining
          hierarchical names. For example, if `sep='.'`, then a Block
          named ‘block1’ nested inside a Group named ‘group1’ would be
          named ‘group1.block1’. Ignored if hierarchical\_names is
          `False`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    See also

    [`flattened()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened.md === -->

# Group.flattened[](#group-flattened "Link to this heading")

Group.flattened(*\**, *fill\_membernames: bool = True*, *hierarchical\_membernames: bool = True*, *sep: str = '.'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened "Link to this definition")
:   Context maanger to temporarily flatten a Group.

    This is a context manager version of the [`flatten()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten") method
    that restores the Group to its original state when exiting the
    context.

    Parameters:
    :   * **fill\_membernames** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, fill empty member names using the default naming
          scheme. If `False`, leave empty member names as empty
          strings. For more control over renaming members with empty
          names, see methods [`fill_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames") and the
          related context manager [`filled_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames").
        * **hierarchical\_membernames** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, use hierarchical names for all members. If
          `False`, use the original names.
        * **sep** (*str, default ‘.’*) – String separator used as a delimiter when combining
          hierarchical names. For example, if `sep='.'`, then a Block
          named ‘block1’ nested inside a Group named ‘group1’ would be
          named ‘group1.block1’. Ignored if hierarchical\_names is
          `False`.

    Yields:
    :   *None*

    See also

    [`flatten()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.from_file.md === -->

# Group.from\_file[](#group-from-file "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Group.from\_file(*src: str | os.PathLike[str] | BaseDataFile*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.from_file "Link to this definition")
:   Read a datafile and return a new instance of Group.

    Known readable file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extensions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path. If it has a missing or unknown file
          extension, you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
          `format_override`, or 2) use the `src_format` parameter.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Group. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents the name or position of a Group in
          the source file (for example, `'/0'` or `'/Group1`).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`. If
          `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata can
          be useful for quickly examining the variable names and hierarchy
          of `src`.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are [`ADSReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions"),
          [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions"), and [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other than `None`
          overrides the format inferred from or set by `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to variable names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in
    `pwdatatools.options.reading.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> g1 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # Using pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> g2 = pwdt.Group.from_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # Using DataFile with a known ext (.mdm)
    >>> g3 = pwdt.Group.from_file(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use the
    `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `src`
    argument and use DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to define
    the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using the `src_format` parameter since the file extension is missing
    >>> g1 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using the `src_format` parameter since the file extension is non-standard
    >>> g2 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using DataFile's `format_override` parameter is another option
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/data/noise', format_override='mdif')
    >>> g3 = pwdt.Group.from_file(datafile)
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter. You
    have to know the name of the part that you want to read from `src`.
    A Group will always be returned, even if the contents could be
    contained within a single Block (the Block is placed into a Group and
    the Group is returned).

    ```
    >>> # partial read of ADS dataset returns Group containing one Block
    >>> group1 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP1.SP')
    >>> # partial read of a .pwdt file
    >>> group2 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/DUT5')
    ```

    See also

    `read_file()`
    `Block.from_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member.md === -->

# Group.get\_member[](#group-get-member "Link to this heading")

Group.get\_member(*loc\_or\_index: str | int*) → Member[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member "Link to this definition")
:   Get a member by string location or integer index.

    Parameters:
    :   **loc** (*str or int*) – The location or integer index of the member to get.

    Returns:
    :   The member at the given location or index.

    Return type:
    :   Member

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the location or index is invalid.

    Examples

    Let’s create a Group with two Blocks and get a member by location.

    ```
    >>> b0 = Block(name="b0")
    >>> b1 = Block(name="b1")
    >>> g = Group([b1, b2])
    >>> g.get_member("0").name
    'b0'
    ```

    It’s equivalent if we use a leading forward slash.

    ```
    >>> g.get_member("/0").name
    'b0'
    ```

    In this case, it’s also equivalent to use the Block’s name.
    However, be cautious when using names because it returns
    the first member with a matching name.

    ```
    >>> g.get_member("b0").name
    'b0'
    ```

    Let’s get a member by integer index instead.

    ```
    >>> g.get_member(1).name
    'b1'
    ```

    Now, let’s examine nested Groups.

    ```
    >>> b0 = Block(name="b0")
    >>> b1 = Block(name="b1")
    >>> g0 = Group([b0, b1], name="g0")
    >>> b2 = Block(name="b2")
    >>> b3 = Block(name="b3")
    >>> g1 = Group([b2, b3], name="g1")
    >>> g = Group([g0, g1], name="g")
    >>> g.get_member("0/0").name
    'b0'
    >>> g.get_member("1/1").name
    'b3'
    ```

    We can also use the names of the Groups and Blocks.

    ```
    >>> g.get_member("g0/b0").name
    'b0'
    >>> g.get_member("g1/b3").name
    'b3'
    ```

    We can even mix and match names and indices.

    ```
    >>> g.get_member("g0/1").name
    'b1'
    >>> g.get_member("1/b2").name
    'b2'
    ```

    If the loc is a lone forward slash, it returns the Group itself.

    ```
    >>> g.get_member("/").name
    'g'
    ```

# Group.get\_member\_as\_block[](#group-get-member-as-block "Link to this heading")

Group.get\_member\_as\_block(*loc\_or\_index: str | int*) → [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block "Link to this definition")
:   Return a member as a Block, retrieving it by string location or integer index.

    This method is preferred over `__getitem__` because it is more
    explicit in its return type. It is also more flexible because it
    can retrieve a Block inside of a child Group (if using string loc).

    Parameters:
    :   **loc\_or\_index** (*str or int*) – The string location or integer index of the member to retrieve as
        a Block.

    Returns:
    :   The member as a Block.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If loc or index is not found in the Group.
          - If loc or index corresponds to a Group instead of a Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_loadpullblock.md === -->

# Group.get\_member\_as\_loadpullblock[](#group-get-member-as-loadpullblock "Link to this heading")

Group.get\_member\_as\_loadpullblock(*loc\_or\_index: str | int*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_loadpullblock "Link to this definition")
:   Return a member as a LoadPullBlock, retrieving it by string location or integer index.

    This method is preferred over `__getitem__` because it is more
    explicit in its return type. It is also more flexible because it
    can retrieve a LoadPullBlock inside of a child Group (if using string loc).

    Note that this method cannot cast a Block as a LoadPullBlock, because
    that operation would involve copying data (and violate expectations
    of “getting” a member). Instead, use [`LoadPullBlock.from_block()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block")
    to create a LoadPullBlock from a Block.

    Parameters:
    :   **loc\_or\_index** (*str or int*) – The string loc or integer index of the member to retrieve as
        a LoadPullBlock.

    Returns:
    :   The member as a LoadPullBlock.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If loc or index is not found in the Group.
          - If loc or index corresponds to a Block or Group instead of a
          LoadPullBlock.

    See also

    [`LoadPullBlock.from_block()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block")


---

# Group.index[](#group-index "Link to this heading")

Group.index(*value: Member*, *start: int = 0*, *stop: int | None = None*) → int[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.index "Link to this definition")
:   Return the index of the first occurrence of the given Member.

    Parameters:
    :   * **value** (*Member*) – The Member to match.
        * **start** (*int, default 0*) – The index to start searching from.
        * **stop** (*int, optional*) – The index to stop searching at. If not provided, the search
          continues to the end of the Group.

    Returns:
    :   The index of the Member.

    Return type:
    :   *int*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the Member is not found in the Group.

# Group.iter\_blocks[](#group-iter-blocks "Link to this heading")

Group.iter\_blocks(*\**, *recursive: bool = False*) → Iterator[[Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_blocks "Link to this definition")
:   Iterate over the Blocks in this Group.

    Parameters:
    :   **recursive** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, iterate recursively over all members (including

        `False`, do not iterate over the members inside child
        Groups.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_members.md === -->

# Group.iter\_members[](#group-iter-members "Link to this heading")

Group.iter\_members(*\**, *recursive: bool = False*) → Iterator[Member][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_members "Link to this definition")
:   Iterate over the members.

    Unlike the `__iter__()` method, this method can optionally
    iterate recursively over all members (including members inside
    child Groups).

    Parameters:
    :   **recursive** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, iterate recursively over all members (including
        members inside child Groups). If `False`, do not iterate
        over the members inside child Groups.

    Notes

    Each iteration yields a child Group or Block. If
    `recursive=True` and a child Group exists, the child Group is
    yielded, then its members are yielded one at a time. If a child
    Group has a child Group, that child Group is yielded, then its
    members are yielded one at a time, and so on.

    Return type:
    :   *Iterator*[Member]


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md === -->

# Group[](#group "Link to this heading")

*class* Group[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "Link to this definition")
:   A collection of Members that represents a hierarchical dataset or a portion of one.

    It behaves like a mutable sequence of Member objects, which may be
    Blocks or child Groups. Members can be retrieved not only by integer
    position, but also by string name/location.

    Initialize a Group with two child Blocks:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> block1 = pwdt.Block(name='block1')
    >>> block2 = pwdt.Block(name='block2')
    >>> group1 = pwdt.Group([block1, block2], name='group1')
    ```

    Initialize a Group with two child Blocks and one child Group:

    ```
    >>> group2 = pwdt.Group([block1, block2, group1], name='group2')
    ```

    The [`Group.name`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.name "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.name") attribute:

    ```
    >>> group2.name  # Get the Group's name
    'group2'
    >>> group2.name = 'new_name'  # Set the Group's name
    ```

    Access the members of the Group:

    ```
    >>> group2.members  # Get the members of the Group (can also set them, but it's not shown here)
    MembersList(
        [
            <Block 'block1' with 0 Vars and -1 observations>,
            <Block 'block2' with 0 Vars and -1 observations>,
            <Group 'group1' with 2 Blocks>,
        ]
    )
    >>> group2[0]  # Access the first member (Block)
    Block(
        <no dvars>,
        name='block1',
        ivarnames=(),
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> group2[1]  # Access the second member (Block)
    Block(
        <no dvars>,
        name='block1',
        ivarnames=(),
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Access members by string location:

    ```
    >>> group2['block1']  # Access the Block by name
    Block(
        <no dvars>,
        name='block1',
        ivarnames=(),
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Access child Block using [`get_member_as_block()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block"):

    ```
    >>> group2.get_member_as_block(0)  # Access the Block by index
    Block(
        <no dvars>,
        name='block1',
        ivarnames=(),
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> group2.get_member_as_block('block1')  # Access the Block by name
    Block(
        <no dvars>,
        name='block1',
        ivarnames=(),
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Note

    The [`get_member_as_block()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block") method is preferred over
    `__getitem__` because it is more explicit in its return type.

    Create a Group by reading a file:

    ```
    >>> group3 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    ```

    See also

    `read_file_as_group()`

    Iterate over the Blocks in the Group:

    ```
    >>> for block in group2.iter_blocks():
    ...     print(block.name)
    block1
    block2
    ```

    See also

    For more detailed examples, see the [Use the Group Class](../../howto/use_group_class.md#use-group-class) in the
    “How To” section of the documentation.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`attrs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.attrs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.attrs "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.attrs") | A dict of arbitrary attributes (metadata). |
    | [`members`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.members.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.members "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.members") | Items (child objects) stored in this Group as a MembersList. |
    | [`name`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.name "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.name") | A str name for the Group. |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__init__")([members, name, attrs]) | Initialize an instance of Group. |
    | [`append`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.append.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.append "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.append")(value) | Append a member to the Group. |
    | [`clear`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.clear.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.clear "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.clear")() | Remove all members from the Group. |
    | [`copy`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.copy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.copy "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.copy")([deep]) | Make a copy of this Group, returning a new instance of Group. |
    | [`count`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.count.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.count "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.count")(value) | Return the number of members with given name. |
    | [`extend`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.extend.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.extend "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.extend")(values) | Extend the Group by appending multiple members. |
    | [`fill_membernames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames")(\*[, recursive, ...]) | Rename members with empty names (in place). |
    | [`filled_membernames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames")(\*[, recursive, ...]) | Context manager to temporarily fill empty membernames (in place). |
    | [`flatten`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten")(\*[, fill\_membernames, ...]) | Flatten child Groups so that only Blocks remain as members. |
    | [`flattened`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened")(\*[, fill\_membernames, ...]) | Context maanger to temporarily flatten a Group. |
    | [`from_file`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.from_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.from_file "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.from_file")(src[, loc, data, format\_specific, ...]) | Read a datafile and return a new instance of Group. |
    | [`get_member`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member")(loc\_or\_index) | Get a member by string location or integer index. |
    | [`get_member_as_block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block")(loc\_or\_index) | Return a member as a Block, retrieving it by string location or integer index. |
    | [`get_member_as_group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_group")(loc\_or\_index) | Return a member as a Group, retrieving it by string location or integer index. |
    | [`get_member_as_loadpullblock`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_loadpullblock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_loadpullblock "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_loadpullblock")(loc\_or\_index) | Return a member as a LoadPullBlock, retrieving it by string location or integer index. |
    | [`index`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.index "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.index")(value[, start, stop]) | Return the index of the first occurrence of the given Member. |
    | [`insert`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.insert.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.insert "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.insert")(index, value) | Insert a member at the index position. |
    | [`iter_blocks`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_blocks.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_blocks "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_blocks")(\*[, recursive]) | Iterate over the Blocks in this Group. |
    | [`iter_members`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_members.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_members "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_members")(\*[, recursive]) | Iterate over the members. |
    | [`pop`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.pop.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.pop "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.pop")([index]) | Remove and return a member by index. |
    | [`remove`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.remove.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.remove "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.remove")(value) | Remove member from this Group. |
    | [`reverse`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.reverse.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.reverse "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.reverse")() | Reverse the order of the members in the Group. |
    | [`to_file`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.to_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.to_file "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.to_file")(dst[, dst\_mode, dst\_format, ...]) | Write Group to a file. |
    | [`tree`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.tree.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.tree "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.tree")() | Return a tree view of this Group, similar to a directory tree. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.to_file.md === -->

# Group.to\_file[](#group-to-file "Link to this heading")

Group.to\_file(*dst: str | PathLike[str] | BaseDataFile*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificWriteOptions | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *loc: str = '/'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.to_file "Link to this definition")
:   Write Group to a file.

    If the destination file has a missing or unknown file extension,
    you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
    `format_override` as `dst`, or 2) use the `dst_format`
    parameter. Known writeable file extensions are stored in
    `options.writing.extensions`. Known writeable file formats
    are stored in `options.writing.formats`.

    Whether metadata is written depends on the format of output file.
    If the output file’s format has places to store metadata (e.g.
    such as in the native pwdatatools format or HDF5 format), then
    metadata is automatically included in the output file.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored
          in `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificWriteOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for writing `dst`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.writing.format_specific` sets format-specific
          writing options, but this parameter overrides the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificWriteOptions`. See the docs for a list
          of all classes that are format-specific write options. Some
          examples are `ADSWriteOptions`,
          `MDIFWriteOptions`, [`ADSWriteInvalid`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid"), and
          [`MDIFWriteInvalid`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid").
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The write location within `dst`. Only applies to file
          formats that support multiple levels of hierarchy (for
          example, native and HDF5 formats). This parameter sets the
          write location within the file’s directory-like (or tree-like)
          structure. The “root” location is ‘/’.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   * **FileExistsError** – If `dst_mode='x'` and `dst` already exists.
        * **ValueError** – If `dst_mode='a'` and append mode is not supported for the
          format of `dst`.

    Examples

    The below examples assume that `group` is an instance of
    [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group") and the following
    imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, you can
    simply input `dst` as a str, `pathlib.Path`, or
    `DataFile`. Known file
    extensions are stored in `options.writing.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> group.to_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> group.to_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>>  # DataFile with known ext
    >>> group.to_file(DataFile('/home/noise_param.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a missing or non-standard file
    extension, you can use the `dst_format` parameter. Alternatively,
    you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `dst`
    argument and use the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to
    define the destination’s format.

    ```
    >>> # no ext solved by dst_format
    >>> group.to_file('/data/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # non-standard ext solved by dst_format
    >>> group.to_file('/data/noise_param.txt', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # no ext solved by DataFile
    >>> group.to_file(DataFile('/data/noise_param', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> group.to_file('/sparam.ds', dst_mode='w')  # overwrite dst, if it exists
    >>> group.to_file('/sparam.mdf', dst_mode='a') # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> group.to_file('/run1.pwdt', dst_mode='a') # append to dst, if it exists
    ```

    See also

    `write_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.tree.md === -->

# Group.tree[](#group-tree "Link to this heading")

Group.tree() → str[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.tree "Link to this definition")
:   Return a tree view of this Group, similar to a directory tree.

    In the tree, each sub-Group is represented as a “folder” and each
    Block is represented as a “file”. This Group is treated as the
    “root” directory.

    The tree is returned as a string with newlines separating each
    level of the tree. The tree is indented to show the hierarchy.
    The string is meant to be printed to the console or shown in a
    GUI, not for programmatic use.

    Returns:
    :   Representation of the Group as a tree.

    Return type:
    :   *str*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> group = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> print(group.tree())
    <[/] Group 's_param'>
    ├── <[0] Block 'Sweep1.SP1.SP' with 4 Vars and 6 observations>
    ├── <[1] Block 'aele_0.Sweep1.SP1' with 3 Vars and 6 observations>
    └── <[2] Block 'aele_1.Sweep1.SP1' with 2 Vars and 2 observations>
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__init__.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.\_\_init\_\_[](#loadpullblock-init "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.\_\_init\_\_(*variables: Variables = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *gamma\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *gamma\_idxname: str | None = None*, *z\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *z\_idxname: str | None = None*, *power\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *power\_idxname: str | None = None*, *outer\_ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *outer\_idxnames: Iterable[str | None] | None = None*, *exprs: ExprsDictLike | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*, *copy: bool = False*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize an instance of LoadPullBlock.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*DataFrame, sequence of Var, str to VarLike mapping, or None*) – Several types of inputs are supported: a pandas DataFrame, a
          mapping of str variable name to ArrayLikes or Vars, or a sequence
          of Vars. Examples of ArrayLike objects are numpy ndarrays and
          MaskedArrays, Python lists, and pandas Series. If a mapping or
          sequence is provided, all data objects must have the same length
          for axis 0.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the LoadPullBlock. If not specified, the name is
          set to an empty string.
        * **gamma\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **gamma\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma indepedent variable’s index.
        * **z\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **z\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance indepedent variable’s index.
        * **power\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power independent variable.
        * **power\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power indepedent variable’s index.
        * **outer\_ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer independent variables. Outer ivars can be
          any arbitrary swept variables, such as frequency, bias, etc.
        * **outer\_idxnames** (*iterable of str | None, optional*) – The names of the outer indepedent variable’s indexes.
        * **exprs** (*ExprsDictLike, optional*) – A mapping of str expression name to Expr objects. The Expr objects
          represent expressions that can be applied to variables or other
          expressions.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Block-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.
        * **copy** (*bool, default False*) – If True, make a copy of the input variables. If False, use the
          input variables as-is, if possible.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*DataFrame, str to ArrayLike mapping, or None*) – A keyword-only argument that exists mostly for backwards
          compatibility. It is recommended to use variables instead of this
          keyword-only argument. You cannot specify values for both data
          and variables.
        * **idxnames** (*iterable of str | None, optional*) – Optional iterable of names for variables that will be converted
          into indexes (idxs) for the ivars. If any of the ivars do not have
          an associated idx, the corresponding idxname should be None. This
          is another way to define the names of the gamma, z, power, and
          outer indexes. To use this argument, you must also specify
          ivarnames and variables input must contain Vars with the
          proper gamma/z/power roles. Defaults to `None`.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables. This is another way
          to define the names of the gamma, z, power, and outer
          independent variables. To use this argument, variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.

    Notes

    If an ivarname is provided and its associated idxname is not
    provided, then its idxname is assumed to be “i” + “ivarname”.
    For example, if gamma\_ivarname is “GammaLoad” and gamma\_idxname
    is not provided, then gamma\_idxname is assumed to be “iGammaLoad”.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    Instantiate a LoadPullBlock with a gamma ivar, using the default
    naming pattern for the gamma idxname:

    ```
    >>> data = {
    ...     'GammaLoad': [0.5 + 0.4j, 0.4 + 0.1j, 0.3 + 0.2j],
    ...     'iGammaLoad': [0, 1, 2],
    ...     'Gain': [10, 11, 12]
    ... }
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(data, gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad')
    >>> lpblock.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.gamma_idxname
    'iGammaLoad'
    ```

    Since the default naming pattern was used for gamma\_idxname, we
    did not have to specify it during initialization.

    Instantiate a LoadPullBlock with a gamma ivar and a power ivar. Note
    that when gamma and power are swept, the power sweep is always the
    inner sweep.

    ```
    >>> data = {
    ...     'GammaLoad': [0.5 + 0.4j, 0.5 + 0.4j, 0.4 + 0.1j, 0.4 + 0.1j],
    ...     'iGammaLoad': [0, 0, 1, 1],
    ...     'PSource': [-10.0, -8.0, -10.0, -8.0],
    ...     'iPSource': [0, 1, 0, 1],
    ...     'Gain': [10, 11, 12, 10.5, 11.5]
    ... }
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(
    ...     data,
    ...     gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
    ...     power_ivarname='PSource',
    ... )
    >>> lpblock.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.gamma_idxname
    'iGammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.power_ivarname
    'PSource'
    >>> lpblock.power_idxname
    'iPSource'
    ```

    Since we did not specify the power\_idxname, it was assumed to be
    “iPSource”, which is the default naming pattern (“i” + “PSource”).

    See also

    The `read_file_as_loadpullblock()`
    function is the easiest way to instantiate a LoadPullBlock from a
    file. For more detailed information and examples, see
    [Work with Load Pull Data](../../howto/work_with_loadpull_data.md#work-with-load-pull-data).


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.at\_gcomp[](#loadpullblock-at-gcomp "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.at\_gcomp(*gcomp\_values: float | int | ArrayLike*, *gain\_varname: str*, *\**, *gcomp\_varname: str = 'auto'*, *extrap: bool = False*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock at specified gain compression value(s).

    Interpolation and/or extrapolation is performed if needed.

    Parameters:
    :   * **gcomp\_values** – Gain compression values at which to calculate all responses.
          Can be a single float value, a list of values, or a numpy
          array.
        * **gain\_varname** – The name of the gain variable to use to calculate compression.
        * **gcomp\_varname, default ‘auto’** – The name of the new calculated gain compression variable.
          Defaults to `'auto'`, which results in a varname of
          `'gain_varname' + '_comp'`.
        * **extrap, default False** – If `True`, extrapolate the data (if needed). If `False`,
          points requested that are outside of the available the data
          regions will be filled with NaN values.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Calculate the data at 0.5dB Gain compression point
    >>> lpblock_at_gcomp = lpblock.at_gcomp(0.5, 'Gain')
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.at\_power[](#loadpullblock-at-power "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.at\_power(*power\_values: float | int | ArrayLike*, *power\_varname: str*, *\**, *interp: bool = True*, *extrap: bool = False*, *interp\_method: Literal['cubic', 'linear'] = 'cubic'*, *extrap\_method: Literal['cubic', 'linear', 'constant'] = 'cubic'*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock at specified power value(s).

    Parameters:
    :   * **power\_values** – Power value(s) at which to calculate all responses.
        * **power\_varname** – The name of the power variable to use for calculations.
        * **interp, default True** – If `True`, interpolate the data (if needed). If `False`,
          points requested that fall between existing data points are
          filled with NaNs.
        * **extrap, default False** – If `True`, extrapolate the data (if needed). If `False`,
          points requested that are outside of the available the data
          regions are filled with NaN values.
        * **interp\_method, default ‘cubic’** – The interpolation method to use.
        * **extrap\_method, default ‘cubic’** – The extrapolation method to use.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Notes

    If `interp=False` and `extrap_method='cubic'`, interpolation
    is performed as a side effect of extrapolation.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Calculate the data at 42 dBm PLoad
    >>> lpblock_at_power = lpblock.at_power(42.0, 'PLoad')
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.attrs.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.attrs[](#loadpullblock-attrs "Link to this heading")

*property* LoadPullBlock.attrs*: [AttrsDict](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools._core.properties.AttrsDict")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.attrs "Link to this definition")
:   Get or set a mapping of arbitrary Block-level metadata.

    The attributes are stored in an [`AttrsDict`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict"), which is a
    type-restricted dict. The keys must be str, and the values must
    be one of the following types: str, int, float, complex, bool,
    None, `numpy.ndarray`, dict, or list. Nested dicts and
    lists are also supported.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.clear.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.clear[](#loadpullblock-clear "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.clear() → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.clear "Link to this definition")
:   Clear the variables in the LoadPullBlock (not supported).

    Raises:
    :   **TypeError** – Unlike a regular Block, a LoadPullBlock cannot be cleared.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.contourplot.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.contourplot[](#loadpullblock-contourplot "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.contourplot(*z: str*, *\**, *ax=None*, *sca: bool = False*, *levels: int | ArrayLike | None = None*, *add\_xlabel: bool = True*, *add\_ylabel: bool = True*, *xlabel: str | None = None*, *ylabel: str | None = None*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.contourplot "Link to this definition")
:   Plot contour lines on a matplotlib Axes.

    A matplotlib QuadContourSet object is returned, which can be used
    to further customize the plot. If you need access to the
    underlying Axes object, use the QuadContourSet’s `axes`
    attribute.

    LoadPullBlocks with a power ivar or outer ivars are not supported.
    If you want to plot contours for a LoadPullBlock with a power
    ivar, you must first get rid of the power dependnecy by selecting
    a subset of the data. There are several ways of doing this,
    including `LoadPullBlock.loc` and the following
    LoadPullBlock methods: [`at_power()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power"), [`at_gcomp()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp"), and
    `xs()`.

    The same limitation is true of LoadPullBlocks with outer ivar(s),
    such as frequency, bias, etc. You must select a subset of the
    data before plotting.

    Parameters:
    :   * **z** – Name of the z variable to plot. The x and y variables are
          always the real and imaginary parts of the gamma ivar.
        * **ax** – An optional matplotlib Axes object. If `None`, use the
          current matplotlib Axes. Defaults to `None`.
        * **sca** – A bool that determines whether to set the Axes used for
          plotting as the current Axes. Invokes
          `matplotlib.pyplot.sca()`. Defaults to `False`.
        * **levels** (*int or array-like, optional*) – Determines the number and positions of the contour lines or
          regions. If an int, use matplotlib’s MaxNLocator, which tries
          to automatically choose no more than n+1 “nice” contour levels
          between minimum and maximum numeric values of Z. If array-
          like, draw contour lines at the specified levels. The values
          must be in increasing order.
        * **add\_xlabel** – If `True`, add an x label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **add\_ylabel** – If `True`, add a y label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **xlabel** – If `add_xlabel` is `True`, use this as the x label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use
          the name of the x variable. If name of the variable is
          unknown, use the default of label, which is `'x'`. Defaults
          to `None`.
        * **ylabel** – If `add_ylabel` is `True`, use this as the y label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use
          the name of the y variable. If name of the variable is
          unknown, use the default of label, which is `'y'`. Defaults
          to `None`.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Additional keyword arguments are passed on to
          `matplotlib.pyplot.contour`.

    Returns:
    :   A matplotlib QuadContourSet instance.

    Return type:
    :   QuadContourSet

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If there is no gamma or impedance ivar.
        If there is a power ivar or any outer ivars.

    Examples

    Start with a LoadPullBlock:
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(…)

    Plot Gain contours on a rectangular chart:
    >>> lpblock.contourplot(‘Gain’)

    Plot Gain contours on a Smith chart:
    >>> contourset = lpblock.contourplot(‘Gain’)
    >>> from kesight.pwdatatools import viz
    >>> viz.draw\_smith\_chart(contourset.axes)

    Plot red DrainEff contours on the same Axes as Gain contours:
    >>> lpblock.contourplot(‘DrainEff’, ax=contourset.axes, colors=’red’)

    Return the QuadContourSet object, and use it to further customize:
    >>> cs = lpblock.contourplot(‘Gain’)
    >>> plt.clabel(cs, inline=True, fontsize=9, fmt=’%.1f’)


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.coord_system.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.coord\_system[](#loadpullblock-coord-system "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.coord\_system() → Literal['rect', 'polar'] | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.coord_system "Link to this definition")
:   If data is gridded, return the grid’s coordinate system (‘rect’ or ‘polar’).

    If data is not gridded, return `None`.

    To be considered “gridded”, the following conditions must be met:
    1) The data must have either a gamma or impedance dependency.
    2) The gamma or impedance values must be regularly-spaced.
    3) There must be the same number of y points for each x point.
    4) If the data also has a power-depedency (in addition to gamma or
    impedance), the 2D grid must be identical for each power value.

    If the data does not meet the specified conditions, it is not
    considered “gridded”, and `None` is returned.

    If the data has a frequency dependency, the 2D grid can have
    different spacing, number of points, and extents at each frequency
    point, but the coordinate system (‘rect’ or ‘polar’) must be
    consistent (a ValueError is raised if not). If, at any frequency,
    the data does not meet the conditions to be considered “gridded”,
    `None` is returned.

    Return type:
    :   {‘rect’, ’polar’, None}

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Determine the coordinate system of the data
    >>> coord_system = lpblock.coord_system()
    >>> if coord_system is None:
    ...     print('Data is not gridded.')
    >>> elif coord_system == 'rect':
    ...     print('Data is gridded in rectangular coordinates.')
    >>> elif coord_system == 'polar':
    ...     print('Data is gridded in polar coordinates.')
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.copy.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.copy[](#loadpullblock-copy "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.copy() → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Return a deep copy of self.

    Notes

    The underlying data in each Var does not need to be copied; only
    metadata needs to be copied. Therefore, this is relatively inexpensive.

LoadPullBlock.crucial\_varnames() → tuple[str, ...][](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_grid_edges.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.drop\_grid\_edges[](#loadpullblock-drop-grid-edges "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.drop\_grid\_edges(*depth: int = 1*, *circular: bool | Literal['auto'] = 'auto'*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_grid_edges "Link to this definition")
:   Return new LoadPullBlock with grid pts along outer edges removed.

    Parameters:
    :   * **depth** – The number of “rows” of outer grid points to remove from each
          edge of the grid. For example, if depth=1, the outermost grid
          points are removed. If depth=2, the outermost 2 rows of grid
          points are removed along each edge.
        * **circular** – Either a bool or `'auto'`. Only applies for grids with
          a polar coordinate system and if drop\_edges is `True`. If
          `'auto'`, automatically determine if the grid is circular or
          donut shaped and if so, only drop grid points around the
          circumference. Otherwise, if a bool, respect the parameter
          setting. Defaults to `'auto'`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # The LoadPullBlock must be gridded
    >>> lpblock_gridded = lpblock.grid_data('rect', drop_edges=False)
    >>> # Remove the outermost 2 rows of grid points along each edge
    >>> lpblock_no_edges = lpblock_gridded.drop_grid_edges(depth=2)
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_invalid_regular.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.drop\_invalid\_regular[](#loadpullblock-drop-invalid-regular "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.drop\_invalid\_regular() → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_invalid_regular "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock with all NaN and null values removed.

    The observations are dropped in such a way as to maintain
    regularity of the power sweeps at each Gamma or Impedance value.
    If there are outer ivars, power sweeps are NOT regularized across
    outer ivar values.

    If there is no power sweep, the NaN and null values are simply dropped.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_observations.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.drop\_observations[](#loadpullblock-drop-observations "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.drop\_observations(*condition: BoolArrayLike1D*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_observations "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new filtered Block without the specified observations.

    Parameters:
    :   **condition** (*1D bool array-like*) – A 1D boolean array-like with the same length as the Block’s
        axis 0, with True values at the indexes of the observations
        to drop.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the observations dropped where condition
        is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(data={'Vout': [1, 2, 3], 'Vin': [4, 5, 6]})
    >>> vout = block['Vout']
    >>> vin = block['Vin']
    >>> block.drop_observations(vout > 2)
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    >>> block.drop_observations((vout == 1) & (vin >= 4))
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    >>> block.drop_observations(vout == 1 | vin >= 4)
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_vars.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.drop\_vars[](#loadpullblock-drop-vars "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.drop\_vars(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_vars "Link to this definition")

          leaving the original Block unmodified. If `True`, drop the variables

# LoadPullBlock.drop\_vars\_in\_place[](#loadpullblock-drop-vars-in-place "Link to this heading")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.eval_expr_as_var.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.eval\_expr\_as\_var[](#loadpullblock-eval-expr-as-var "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.eval\_expr\_as\_var(*key: str*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.eval_expr_as_var "Link to this definition")
:   Evaluate the expression and return the result as a Var.

    Parameters:
    :   **key** (*str*) – The name of the expression to evaluate. It must exist in the
        Block’s [`exprs`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs") attribute.

    Notes

    The returned Var is not automatically added to the Block. If you
    want to add the Var to the Block, add it like this:

    ```
    >>> block["x"] = block.eval_expr_as_var("x")
    ```

    But note that not all expressions can be added as a Var to the Block.
    Only expressions that evaluate to the same length as the Block’s
    observations can be added as a Var.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.fill_nan.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.fill\_nan[](#loadpullblock-fill-nan "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.fill\_nan(*value: FloatNumber*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.fill_nan "Link to this definition")
:   FIll all NaN values in all Vars and return a new Block or a reference to this Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **value** (*float | int | np.floating | np.integer*) – The value to use for filling NaN values.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, fill NaN values in place and return a reference to this Block.
          If `False`, return a new Block with NaN values filled.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Notes

    Unlike [`Var.fill_nan()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan"), this method does not allow for a
    complex fill value, since that doesn’t work for float variables.
