<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.fill_null.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.fill\_null[](#loadpullblock-fill-null "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.fill\_null(*values: [FillValues](keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues.md#keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues "keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues") | None = None*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.fill_null "Link to this definition")
:   Fill all null values in all Vars and return a new Block or a reference to this Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **values** (*FillValues, optional*) – An optional instance of [`FillValues`](keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues.md#keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues "keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues"). If not provided,
          the default fill values are used.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, fill null values in place and return a reference to this Block.
          If `False`, return a new Block with null values filled.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Notes

    Here are the default fill values for each dtype:

    * float: NaN
    * complex: NaN + 1j \* NaN
    * integer: 0
    * boolean: False
    * string: ‘’


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.from\_block[](#loadpullblock-from-block "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* LoadPullBlock.from\_block(*block: [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block "Link to this definition")
:   Make an instance of LoadPullBlock from a Block.

    This method copies the Vars and Block metadata from the input
    Block to the new LoadPullBlock.

    Parameters:
    :   **block** (*Block*) – The Block to convert to a LoadPullBlock.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> block = pwdt.Block.from_file("/data/loadpull.cst")
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock.from_block(block)
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.from\_file[](#loadpullblock-from-file "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* LoadPullBlock.from\_file(*src: str | os.PathLike[str] | BaseDataFile*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file "Link to this definition")
:   Make an instance of LoadPullBlock by reading a loadpull datafile.

    The following file formats are supported: “loadpull” (which
    includes Maury, Focus, and Keysight NVNA MDIF-based files),
    and “native” (.pwdt file extension).

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The datafile to read.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a LoadPullBlock. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents the name or position of a Block in
          the source file (for example, `'/0'` or `'/LPData`).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options.
          Examples are [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions") and
          [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[RoleLike, str]*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    Using all the default options:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock.from_file("/data/loadpull.cst")
    ```

    Modifying global options to control how variables are named and
    which variables are derived from a and b waves:

    ```
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.varnames["power.delivered.load"] = "Pout"
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.format_specific.loadpull.derived_vars.ampm = False
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock.from_file("/data/loadpull.cst")
    ```

    Passing in a dict of roles to varnames and an instance of
    [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars") to override the global settings:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock.from_file(
    ...     "/data/loadpull.cst",
    ...     varnames={'gamma.load': 'GL'},
    ...     format_specific=pwdt.LoadPullDerivedVars(ampm=False),
    ... )
    ```

    See also

    :func:read\_file\_as\_loadpullblock

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.from\_pandas\_dataframe[](#loadpullblock-from-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* LoadPullBlock.from\_pandas\_dataframe(*dataframe: DataFrame*, *\**, *name: str = ''*, *gamma\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *gamma\_idxname: str | None = None*, *z\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *z\_idxname: str | None = None*, *power\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *power\_idxname: str | None = None*, *outer\_ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *outer\_idxnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *combine\_multidim: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *\*\*kwargs*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a new LoadPullBlock from a pandas DataFrame.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dataframe** (*pandas.DataFrame*) – A DataFrame containing all variable data. The DataFrame’s row
          index can be either a “default” index (a RangeIndex or
          Int64Index with start=0 & step=1), or non-default index(es)
          with name(s).
        * **name** (*str, default “”*) – A str name for the LoadpullBlock.
        * **gamma\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **gamma\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma indepedent variable’s index.
        * **z\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **z\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance indepedent variable’s index.
        * **power\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power independent variable.
        * **power\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power indepedent variable’s index.
        * **outer\_ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer independent variables. Outer ivars can be
          any arbitrary swept variables, such as frequency, bias, etc.
        * **outer\_idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer indepedent variable’s indexes.
        * **combine\_multidim** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables into a single
          variable. If `False`, keep multidimensional variables as
          separate variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – The characters used to separate the dimension scales in the
          column names of the DataFrame. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter.
          For example, the default delimiters would interpret columns
          like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'` as variables `'S'` and
          `'PortZ'` with dimension scales of (2,1) and (1),
          respectively.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the indexes. This is another way to define the
          names of the gamma, z, power, and outer indexes. To use this
          argument, you must also specify ivarnames and variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables. This is another way
          to define the names of the gamma, z, power, and outer
          independent variables. To use this argument, variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")


---

LoadPullBlock.gamma\_idx() → [DimScale](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale "keysight.pwdatatools._core.indexing.DimScale") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_idx "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivar.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.gamma\_ivar[](#loadpullblock-gamma-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.gamma\_ivar(*form: Literal['complex', 'real', 'imag', 'mag', 'angle', 'polar\_str', 'rect\_str'] = 'complex'*, *\*\*kwargs*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return the gamma independent variable (ivar) in the form specified.

    If a gamma is not present in the ivars, return None.
    A variable is considered to be a gamma if its role base is
    “gamma” in the Var’s `role` metadata.

    Parameters:
    :   **form** – The form of the gamma ivar to return. Must be one of
        `"complex"`, `"real"`, `"imag"`, `"mag"`, `"angle"`,
        `"polar_str"`, or `"rect_str"`. Defaults to `"complex"`.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **enable\_prefix** (*bool*) – If `True`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          prefixed with the real\_prefix, imag\_prefix, mag\_prefix,
          or angle\_prefix, depending on the value of `form`. If
          `False`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          not prefixed. The default is `True`.
        * **enable\_suffix** (*bool*) – If `True`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          suffixed with the real\_suffix, imag\_suffix, mag\_suffix,
          or angle\_suffix, depending on the value of `form`. If
          `False`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          not suffixed. The default is `True`.
        * **real\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="real"`. The default is `'real_of_'`.
        * **real\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="real"`. The default is `""`.
        * **imag\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="imag"`. The default is `'imag_of_'`.
        * **imag\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="imag"`. The default is `""`.
        * **mag\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="mag"`. The default is `'mag_of_'`.
        * **mag\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="mag"`. The default is `""`.
        * **angle\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="angle"`. The default is `'angle_of'`.
        * **angle\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="angle"`. The default is `""`.
        * **angle\_deg** (*bool*) – If `True`, the angle values are returned in degrees.
          If `False`, the angle are returned in radians. The default
          is `True`.

    Returns:
    :   The gamma ivar in the specified form, or `None` if there is
        no gamma ivar.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var") | *None*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Get the gamma ivar in complex form
    >>> gamma_complex = lpblock.gamma_ivar()
    >>> # Get the gamma ivar in real form
    >>> gamma_real = lpblock.gamma_ivar("real")
    >>> # Get the gamma ivar in polar string form (e.g., "0.520 | 170.0")
    >>> # This is ideal for plotting labels
    >>> gamma_polar_str = lpblock.gamma_ivar("polar_str")
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivar_scatterplot.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot[](#loadpullblock-gamma-ivar-scatterplot "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(*\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivar_scatterplot "Link to this definition")
:   Plot the gamma ivar as a scatterplot on a matplotlib Axes.

    All keyword arguments are passed to seaborn’s scatterplot
    function, with the exception of the `data` kwarg. The `data`
    kwarg passed to seaborn’s scatterplot is always this LoadPullBlock
    instance. If no Axes object is supplied with the `ax` keyword
    parameter, then the current Axes is used.

    Return type:
    :   matplotlib.axes.Axes

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If there is no gamma ivar.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    ```

    Plot the gamma ivar on the current Axes:
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot()

    Plot the gamma ivar on a given Axes:
    >>> fig, ax = plt.subplots()
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(ax=ax)

    Plot the gamma ivar on a Smith chart:
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import viz
    >>> fig, ax = plt.subplots()
    >>> viz.draw\_smith\_chart(ax)
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(ax=ax)

    Plot the gamma ivar with swept freq shown with different hues
    (note that you only need to pass the str varname, not a Series):
    >>> fig, ax = plt.subplots()
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(ax=ax, hue=’freq’)

    Customize the color and markers:
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(color=’red’, marker=”x”)

    Return the Axes object to a variable for further customization:
    >>> ax = lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot()
    >>> ax.set\_title(“My custom title”)


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_to_z.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.gamma\_to\_z[](#loadpullblock-gamma-to-z "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.gamma\_to\_z(*z\_name: str*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_to_z "Link to this definition")
:   Return LoadPullBlock with impedance sweep, converted from gamma sweep.

    Parameters:
    :   **z\_name** – The name of the new impedance ivar to be calculated from
        the gamma ivar.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Convert gamma sweep to a z sweep, giving new Z sweep variable a name
    >>> lpblock_z = lpblock.gamma_to_z("ZLoad")
    >>> lpblock_z.z_ivarname
    'ZLoad'
    >>> lpblock_z.gamma_ivarname is None
    True
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.get[](#loadpullblock-get "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.get(*varname: str*, *default=None*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get "Link to this definition")
:   Return a variable by name, or None if the variable does not exist.

    Similar to `__getitem__()` and [`get_var()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var") methods, except
    that this method returns None if the varname is not found, while
    the other two methods raise a KeyError.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varname** – The name of the variable to retrieve.
        * **default** – The value to return if the variable is not found. Only
          None is supported.

    Return type:
    :   *Optional*[[*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")]

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_grid.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.get\_grid[](#loadpullblock-get-grid "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.get\_grid() → [Grid](keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.gridding.Grid") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_grid "Link to this definition")
:   Return a Grid object from self.

    If the sweep includes outer swept variables, a ValueError is
    raised. If you want get a Grid from a LoadPullBlock with
    swept outer variables, you must first take a cross-section
    (for example, use the `xs` method or `loc`) to reduce the
    dimensionality.

    To be considered “gridded”, the following conditions must be met:
    1) The data must have either a gamma or impedance dependency.
    2) The gamma or impedance values must be regularly-spaced.
    3) There must be the same number of y points for each x point.
    4) If the data also has a power-depedency (in addition to gamma or
    impedance), the 2D grid must be identical for each power value.

    If the data does not meet the specified conditions, it is not
    considered “gridded”, and `None` is returned.

    If the data has a frequency dependency, the 2D grid can have
    different spacing, number of points, and extents at each frequency
    point, but the coordinate system (‘rect’ or ‘polar’) must be
    consistent (a ValueError is raised if not). If, at any frequency,
    the data does not meet the conditions to be considered “gridded”,
    `None` is returned.

    Return type:
    :   [*Grid*](keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the sweep includes outer swept variables.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Get the grid from the LoadPullBlock
    >>> grid = lpblock.get_grid()
    >>> if grid is not None:
    >>>     # The data is gridded
    >>>     # Do something with the grid
    >>> else:
    >>>     # The data is not gridded
    >>>     # Do something else
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_sweep.md === -->

LoadPullBlock.get\_var(*varname: str*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var_as_expr.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.get\_var\_as\_expr[](#loadpullblock-get-var-as-expr "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.get\_var\_as\_expr(*varname: str*) → [Expr](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr "keysight.pwdatatools._core.expressions.Expr")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var_as_expr "Link to this definition")
:   Get a variable as an expression.

    Parameters:
    :   **varname** (*str*) – The name of the variable to retrieve as an expression.

    Returns:
    :   An expression representing the variable.

    Return type:
    :   [*Expr*](keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr")

    Notes

    The returned Expr is not automatically added to the Block.
    If you want to add the Expr to the Block, add it to the [`exprs`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs")
    attribute, giving it any arbitrary key like this:

    ```
    >>> block.exprs["x_modified"] = block.get_var_as_expr("x")
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.grid_data.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.grid\_data[](#loadpullblock-grid-data "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.grid\_data(*coord\_system: Literal['rect', 'polar']*, *\**, *npointsx: int = 10*, *npointsy: int = 0*, *xmax: float = nan*, *xmin: float = nan*, *ymax: float = nan*, *ymin: float = nan*, *drop\_edges: bool = True*, *circular: bool | Literal['auto'] = 'auto'*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.grid_data "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock with data interpolated on a 2D grid.

    By default, the 2D grid’s extents are set by the extents of the
    data. But the grid’s extents may be set explicitly by the keyword
    args xmax, xmin, ymax, and ymin.

    Gridding will fail as you approach the edges of the original
    data’s extents. These bad points will contain NaN values. Also, it
    is possible that gridding does not fail, but instead produces
    unreliable data near the edges. For these reasons, the default
    behavior is to drop the outermost grid points around the edges of
    the gridded data.

    Parameters:
    :   * **coord\_system** – Either `'rect'` or `'polar'`.
        * **npointsx** – Number of points in grid’s x dimension. If 0, use same
          value as npointsy. Defaults to 10.
        * **npointsy** – Number of points in grid’s y dimension. If 0, use same
          value as npointsx. Defaults to 0.
        * **xmax** – Maximum value of x. If NaN, the max value of the original
          data is used. Default is NaN.
        * **xmin** – Minimum value of x. If NaN, the min value of the original
          data is used. Default is NaN.
        * **ymax** – Maximum value of y. If NaN, the max value of the original
          data is used. Default is NaN.
        * **ymin** – Minimum value of y. If NaN, the min value of the original
          data is used. Default is NaN.
        * **drop\_edges** – A bool that determines whether the outermost “rows” of gridded
          data around the grid’s edges are removed. Defaults to `True`.
        * **circular** – Either a bool or `'auto'`. Only applies to polar grids and
          when drop\_edges is `True`. If `'auto'`, tries to
          automatically determine if the grid is circular or donut-
          shaped and if so, drops only grid points around the
          circumference. Otherwise, if a bool, respects the parameter
          setting. Defaults to `'auto'`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Apply a rectangular grid with 8 points in x and y
    >>> lpblock_gridded = lpblock.grid_data('rect', npointsx=8)
    >>> # Apply a polar grid with the default 10 points in x and y
    >>> lpblock_gridded = lpblock.grid_data('polar')
    >>> # Apply a polar grid, explicitly setting the extents of the grid
    >>> lpblock_gridded = lpblock.grid_data(
    ...     'polar', xmax=0.5, xmin=0.1, ymax=0.5, ymin=0.1
    ... )
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.has_regular_power_ivar.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.has\_regular\_power\_ivar[](#loadpullblock-has-regular-power-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.has\_regular\_power\_ivar() → bool[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.has_regular_power_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return True if the data has identical power values at each gamma or z.

    If the data has outer ivars (for example, frequency), this
    function returns True even if the data has different power values
    at each outer ivar value.

    Return type:
    :   *bool*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> if lpblock.has_regular_power_ivar():
    >>>     # The data has identical power values at each Gamma or Z
    >>>     # Do something
    >>> else:
    >>>     # The data does not have identical power values at each Gamma or Z
    >>>     # Do something else
    ```

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the data does not have a power ivar.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.info.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.info[](#loadpullblock-info "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.info(*varnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.info "Link to this definition")
:   Return a DataFrame containing information about the specified variable(s).

    The information on each variables includes the kind (ivar, dvar, or
    idx), role, dtype, min, max, NaN count, and masked value count.

    For complex variables, the min and max values are the minimum and
    maximum magnitudes. For string, boolean and other non-numeric types,
    the min and max values are not defined.

    Parameters:
    :   **varname** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – Name(s) of a variable(s) on which to gather info. If `None`,
        return info for all variables. If `varnames` is a str,
        return a DataFrame with that variable’s info. If `varnames`
        is an iterable of str, return a DataFrame containing all of
        those variables’ info. Defaults to `None`.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.is_gridded.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.is\_gridded[](#loadpullblock-is-gridded "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.is\_gridded() → bool[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.is_gridded "Link to this definition")
:   Return bool indicating whether the data is gridded.

    To be considered “gridded”, the following conditions must be met:
    1) The data must have either a gamma or impedance dependency.
    2) The gamma or impedance values must be regularly-spaced.
    3) There must be the same number of y points for each x point.
    4) If the data also has a power-depedency (in addition to gamma or
    impedance), the 2D grid must be identical for each power value.

    If the data does not meet the specified conditions, it is not
    considered “gridded”, and `False` is returned.

    If the data has a frequency dependency, the 2D grid can have
    different spacing, number of points, and extents at each frequency
    point, but the coordinate system (‘rect’ or ‘polar’) must be
    consistent (a ValueError is raised if not). If, at any frequency,
    the data does not meet the conditions to be considered “gridded”,
    `False` is returned.

    Return type:
    :   *bool*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the gridding is not consistent across power values.
        If the coordinate system is not consistent across frequencies.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Check if the data is gridded
    >>> is_gridded = lpblock.is_gridded()
    >>> if is_gridded:
    >>>     # The data is gridded
    >>>     # Do something
    >>> else:
    >>>     # The data is not gridded
    >>>     # Do something else
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.iter_sweep_nodes.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.iter\_sweep\_nodes[](#loadpullblock-iter-sweep-nodes "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.iter\_sweep\_nodes(*\**, *tol: float | None | Mapping[str, float | None] = None*) → Iterator[Self][](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.iter_sweep_nodes "Link to this definition")
:   Iterate over unique values of ivars (or their idxs), yielding sub-Blocks.

    The Block is split into sections using the ivars. If the ivars have
    associated idxs, those are used. If not, then the ivars’ data values are used.
    Splitting on data values can cause precision issues for float and complex
    dtypes, so tolerance(s) can be specified. With the exception of the innermost
    ivar, the ivar values (or idxs) in each sub-Block will be constant (within
    tolerances).

    Parameters:
    :   **tol** (*optional float or dict of (ivarname, optional float) pairs, default None*) – The absolute tolerance to use when splitting the Block into sections
        based upon ivars with inexact dtypes. Inexact dtypes include all float
        and complex dtypes. If tol is a float, it is the tolerance used for
        all ivars. If tol is a dict-like, the keys are ivarnames and the values
        are float tolerances for each variable. If any tolerance is None or NaN,
        the Block is split into sections based on exact value matches for that variable.

    Yields:
    :   *Block* – A sub-Block containing observations where each ivar (or its idx) has
        constant value, with exception of innermost ivar.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.keep_vars.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.keep\_vars[](#loadpullblock-keep-vars "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.keep\_vars(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*, *keep\_crucial: bool = True*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.keep_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Keep the specified variables and return this Block or a new Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** – The names of the variables to keep.
        * **in\_place, default False** – If `False`, return a new Block with the specified variables
          kept, leaving the original Block unmodified. If `True`, keep
          the variables in this Block and return a reference to this Block.
        * **keep\_crucial, default True** – If `True`, keep all vars returned by the
          [`crucial_varnames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames") method. Otherwise, only keep them
          if they are explicitly included in varnames.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified variables kept, or this Block
        if in\_place is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any of the varnames are not in the Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.keep_vars_in_place.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.keep\_vars\_in\_place[](#loadpullblock-keep-vars-in-place "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.keep\_vars\_in\_place(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *keep\_crucial: bool = True*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.keep_vars_in_place "Link to this definition")

    Parameters:

        * **keep\_crucial, default True** – If `True`, keep all vars returned by the
          [`crucial_varnames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames") method. Otherwise, only keep them
          if they are explicitly included in varnames.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.make_idxs.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.make\_idxs[](#loadpullblock-make-idxs "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.make\_idxs(*\**, *tol: float | None | Mapping[str, float | None] = None*, *existing: Literal['raise', 'replace'] = 'raise'*, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.make_idxs "Link to this definition")
:   Create integer indexes (idxs) for the ivars and return this Block or a new Block.

    The idxs are created by iterating through the combinations of values of the ivar(s).

    Parameters:
    :   * **tol** (*optional float or dict of (ivarname, optional float) pairs, default None*) – The absolute tolerance to use for finding unique values for ivars with
          inexact dtypes. Inexact dtypes include all float and complex dtypes. If
          tol is a float, it is the tolerance used for all ivars. If tol is a
          dict-like, the keys are ivarnames and the values are float tolerances for
          each variable. If any tolerance is None or NaN, the unique values are
          created based on exact value matches for that variable.
        * **existing** (*{‘raise’, ‘replace’}, default ‘raise’*) – What to do if any idx already exists. If ‘raise’, raise a
          ValueError. If ‘replace’, replace existing idxs with new ones.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, return a new Block with the new idxs, leaving the
          original Block unmodified. If `True`, add the new idxs to this
          Block and return a reference to this Block.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import keysight.pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(
    ...     {
    ...         "bias": [1.2, 1.2, 1.3, 1.3],
    ...         "freq": [1e9, 2e9, 1e9, 2e9],
    ...         "z": [1, 2, 3, 4],
    ...     },
    ...     ivarnames=["bias", "freq"],
    ... )
    >>> block_idxs = block.make_idxs()
    >>> block_idxs.idxnames
    ('ibias', 'ifreq')
    ```

    Let’s view the the new Block’s data as a pandas DataFrame to help
    visualize the new idxs.

    ```
    >>> block_idxs.to_pandas_dataframe()
       ibias  ifreq  bias          freq  z
    0      0      0   1.2  1.000000e+09  1
    1      0      1   1.2  2.000000e+09  2
    2      1      0   1.3  1.000000e+09  3
    3      1      1   1.3  2.000000e+09  4
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock[](#loadpullblock "Link to this heading")

*class* LoadPullBlock[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "Link to this definition")
:   A collection of Vars that represents load pull data.

    Load pull is a process by which the impedance presented to a device under
    test is systematically varied in order to assess its performance.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    Instantiate a LoadPullBlock with a gamma ivar, using the default
    naming pattern for the gamma idxname:

    ```
    >>> data = {
    ...     'GammaLoad': [0.5 + 0.4j, 0.4 + 0.1j, 0.3 + 0.2j],
    ...     'iGammaLoad': [0, 1, 2],
    ...     'Gain': [10, 11, 12]
    ... }
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(data, gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad')
    >>> lpblock.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.gamma_idxname
    'iGammaLoad'
    ```

    Since the default naming pattern was used for gamma\_idxname, we
    did not have to specify it during initialization.

    Instantiate a LoadPullBlock with a gamma ivar and a power ivar. Note
    that when gamma and power are swept, the power sweep is always the
    inner sweep.

    ```
    >>> data = {
    ...     'GammaLoad': [0.5 + 0.4j, 0.5 + 0.4j, 0.4 + 0.1j, 0.4 + 0.1j],
    ...     'iGammaLoad': [0, 0, 1, 1],
    ...     'PSource': [-10.0, -8.0, -10.0, -8.0],
    ...     'iPSource': [0, 1, 0, 1],
    ...     'Gain': [10, 11, 12, 10.5, 11.5]
    ... }
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(
    ...     data,
    ...     gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
    ...     power_ivarname='PSource',
    ... )
    >>> lpblock.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.gamma_idxname
    'iGammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.power_ivarname
    'PSource'
    >>> lpblock.power_idxname
    'iPSource'
    ```

    Since we did not specify the power\_idxname, it was assumed to be
    “iPSource”, which is the default naming pattern (“i” + “PSource”).

    See also

    The `read_file_as_loadpullblock()`
    function is the easiest way to instantiate a LoadPullBlock from a
    file. For more detailed information and examples, see
    [Work with Load Pull Data](../../howto/work_with_loadpull_data.md#work-with-load-pull-data).

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`attrs`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.attrs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.attrs "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.attrs") | Get or set a mapping of arbitrary Block-level metadata. |
    | [`dvarnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.dvarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.dvarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.dvarnames") | Get the names of the dependent variables (dvars) in the Block. |
    | [`exprs`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs") | Get or set a mapping of expression names to Expr objects. |
    | [`gamma_idxname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_idxname") | Return the name of the gamma index variable (idx var). |
    | [`gamma_ivarname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivarname") | Return the name of the gamma independent variable (ivar). |
    | [`idxnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.idxnames") | Get the names of the indexes (idxs) associated with the ivars. |
    | [`ivarnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.ivarnames") | Get or set the names of the independent variables (ivars) in the Block. |
    | [`name`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.name "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.name") | Get or set an arbitrary string identifier for the Block. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.pop.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.pop[](#loadpullblock-pop "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.pop(*varname: str*, *default: [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None = None*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.pop "Link to this definition")
:   Remove and return a variable by name.

    The ivarnames are updated if necessary. Also, the
    returned Var’s block attribute is set to None.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varname** (*str*) – The name of the variable to remove.
        * **default** – The value to return if the variable is not found. Only
          a Var or None is supported. If None, a KeyError is raised.

    Returns:
    :   The removed variable or the default value if the variable was not found.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_idx.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.power\_idx[](#loadpullblock-power-idx "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.power\_idx() → [DimScale](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale "keysight.pwdatatools._core.indexing.DimScale") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_idx "Link to this definition")
:   Return the power index (idx).

    A variable is considered to be a power if its role base is
    “power” in the Var’s `role` metadata.

    Returns:

# LoadPullBlock.power\_ivar[](#loadpullblock-power-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.power\_ivar() → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return the power independent variable (ivar).

    A variable is considered to be a power if its role base is
    “power” in the Var’s `role` metadata.

    Returns:
    :   The power ivar if it exists, otherwise None.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var") | *None*

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.regularize_power_ivar.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.regularize\_power\_ivar[](#loadpullblock-regularize-power-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.regularize\_power\_ivar(*\**, *points: ndarray | list[float] | None = None*, *npoints: int = 0*, *interp\_method: Literal['cubic', 'linear'] = 'cubic'*, *extrap: bool = False*, *extrap\_method: Literal['cubic', 'linear', 'constant'] = 'cubic'*, *decimals: int = 1*, *drop\_invalid: bool = False*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.regularize_power_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock with regularized power ivar values.

    A regularized power sweep has the exact same values at each gamma
    or z value. If the data has outer ivar(s) (for example,
    frequency), the power is regularized at each outer sweep value,
    but is NOT regularized across outer sweep values.

    If both `points` and `npoints` are supplied, the `points`
    are treated as extra points to be explicitly added to the evenly-
    spaced power sweep calculated by `npoints`. Therefore, the total
    number of power points will be `npoints` plus the number of
    points in `points`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **points** (*array\_like or None, optional*) – The power values to use for each gamma or z point in the new
          regularized LoadPullBlock. If `None`, the power values are
          determined solely by the `npoints` argument. Otherwise,
          if a list or array is provided, the points are explicitly
          included.
        * **npoints** (*int, default 0*) – The number of power points to use for each gamma or z point in
          the new regularized power LoadPullBlock. If 0, the number of
          points is the average number of power points at each gamma or
          z value. However, if `points` is supplied, then those points
          are used instead.
        * **interp\_method** (*{‘cubic’, ‘linear’}, default ‘cubic’*) – The interpolation method to use when interpolating the data
          to fill in missing values. Must be one of `"linear"` or
          `"cubic"`.
        * **extrap** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, extrapolate the data to fill in missing values.
        * **extrap\_method** (*{‘cubic’, ‘linear’, ‘constant’}, default ‘cubic’*) – The extrapolation method to use when extrapolating the data
          to fill in missing values. Must be one of `"linear"`,
          `"cubic"`, or `"constant"`.
        * **decimals** (*int, default 1*) – The number of decimal places to round the power values to.
        * **drop\_invalid** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, observations with NaN or NA values are dropped.
          They are dropped in such a way as to maintain a regular
          power ivar.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    ```

    Regularize the power, providing explicit power points:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize_power_ivar(points=[-10, -5, 0, 5, 10])
    ```

    Regularize the power, providing the number of power points:
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize\_power\_ivar(npoints=5)

    Regularize the power, providing both explicit power points and
    the number of power points resulting in a total of 5 + 10 = 15
    power points, evenly spaced between pmax and pmin and including
    the explicit power points provided:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize_power_ivar(
    ...     points=[-10, -5, 0, 5, 10], npoints=10
    ... )
    ```

    Enable extrapolation (uses default cubic method):

    ```
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize_power_ivar(extrap=True)
    ```

    Use linear interpolation and extrapolation, instead of the
    default cubic interpolation and extrapolation. Note you must also
    set `extrap=True` to enable extrapolation:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize_power_ivar(
    ...     extrap=True,
    ...     interp_method="linear",
    ...     extrap_method="linear",
    ... )
    ```

# LoadPullBlock.rename\_vars[](#loadpullblock-rename-vars "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.rename\_vars(*old\_to\_new\_map: Mapping[str, str]*, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.rename_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Rename variables and return this Block or a new Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **old\_to\_new\_map** (*dict-like*) – A mapping of old variable names to new variable names.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, return a new Block with the specified variables
          renamed, leaving the original Block unmodified. If `True`,
          rename the variables in this Block and return a reference to
          this Block.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified variables renamed, or this Block
        if in\_place is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any old varnames in old\_to\_new\_map are not in the Block.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.set\_data[](#loadpullblock-set-data "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.set\_data(*data: Mapping[str, ArrayLike | Scalar] | pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *combine\_multidim\_vars: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data "Link to this definition")
:   Replace the data for variables in the Block, and return this Block or a new Block.

    If a variable does not exist, it is created. If it does exist,
    its data is replaced and all other attributes are preserved.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*mapping of str to array-like/scalar values or pandas.DataFrame*) – The new data to set in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim\_vars** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables in data into
          a single variable. This also has the effect of combining the
          existing multidimensional variables in the Block. If
          `False`, do not combine multidimensional variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*length 3 tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the varnames of flattened multidim variables. Only applies if
          `combine_multidim_vars=True`. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters would work for flattened
          multidim varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'`.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, return a new Block with the specified data.
          If `True`, set the data in this Block and return a reference
          to this Block.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified data, or this Block if
        in\_place is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If the length along axis 0 of any of the array-likes in
          data does not match the Block’s observation count.
          - If data is a pandas DataFrame and combine\_multidim\_vars
          is `True`, and the dimscales of a multidimensional variable
          in the DataFrame do not match the dimscales of the
          corresponding multidimensional variable in the Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data_in_place.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.set\_data\_in\_place[](#loadpullblock-set-data-in-place "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.set\_data\_in\_place(*data: Mapping[str, ArrayLike | Scalar] | pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *combine\_multidim\_vars: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Set the data for variable(s) in the Block (in place).

    If a variable does not exist, it is created. If it does exist,
    its data is replaced and all other attributes are preserved.

    Deprecated since version 0.12.0: Use [`set_data()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data") with in\_place=True instead.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*mapping of str to array-like/scalar values or pandas.DataFrame*) – The new data to set in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim\_vars** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables in data into
          a single variable. This also has the effect of combining the
          existing multidimensional variables in the Block. If
          `False`, do not combine multidimensional variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*length 3 tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the varnames of flattened multidim variables. Only applies if
          `combine_multidim_vars=True`. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters would work for flattened
          multidim varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If the length along axis 0 of any of the array-likes in
          data does not match the Block’s observation count.
          - If data is a pandas DataFrame and combine\_multidim\_vars
          is `True`, and the dimscales of a multidimensional variable
          in the DataFrame do not match the dimscales of the
          corresponding multidimensional variable in the Block.

    Notes

    This method does not attempt to extract any idx(s) from the DataFrame.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_vars.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.set\_vars[](#loadpullblock-set-vars "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.set\_vars(*variables: [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var") | Iterable[[Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")]*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Set variables and return this Block or a new Block.

    This method adds new Vars and/or replaces existing ones. If a Var
    by the same name already exists in the Block, it is replaced.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*Var or iterable of Var*) – Var instance(s) to set in the Block.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, return a new Block with the specified variables,
          leaving the original Block unmodified. If `True`, set the
          variables in this Block and return a reference to this Block.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified variables, or this Block if
        in\_place is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Notes

    Implicit copies are made and warings are generated if any Vars
    already belong to another Block.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_adscontourblock.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.to\_adscontourblock[](#loadpullblock-to-adscontourblock "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.to\_adscontourblock(*name: str | None = None*) → ADSContourBlock[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_adscontourblock "Link to this definition")
:   Return an ADSContourBlock from self.

    The ADSCountourBlock data is formatted in a special way that
    facilitates contour plotting in PathWave Advanced Design System
    (ADS).

    Parameters:
    :   **name** – The str name of the ADSContourBlock. If `None`, the name of
        the LoadPullBlock is used. Defaults to `None`.

    Return type:
    :   ADSContourBlock

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> adsblock = lpblock.to_adscontourblock()  # convert to ADSContourBlock
    >>> adsblock.to_file("lpdata.ds")  # save the ADSContourBlock to an ADS dataset
    >>> # create a Group of consisting of any groupable members, including
    >>> # Blocks, ADSContourBlocks, LoadPullBlocks, and other Groups
    >>> group = pwdt.Group(members=[adsblock, block])
    >>> group.to_file("/home/group.ds")  # save the Group to an ADS dataset
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_file.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.to\_file[](#loadpullblock-to-file "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.to\_file(*dst: str | PathLike[str] | BaseDataFile*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificWriteOptions | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *loc: str = '/'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_file "Link to this definition")
:   Write data (and possibly metadata) to a file.

    For all destination file formats except native pwdatatools files
    (.pwdt extension), the idxs are used as the ivars in the file, and
    the ivars become dvars in the file. This avoids limitations in
    most file formats with respect to complex ivars.

    If the destination file dst has a known file extension that maps
    to a supported format, then the file is written in that format.
    If the destination file has a missing or unknown file extension,
    you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
    `format_override` as `dst`, or 2) use the `dst_format`
    parameter. Known writeable file extensions are stored in
    `options.writing.extensions`. Known writeable file formats
    are stored in `options.writing.formats`.

    Whether metadata is written depends on the format of output file.
    If the output file’s format has places to store metadata (such as
    in the native pwdatatools .pwdt format), then metadata is
    automatically included in the output file.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** – The destination file as a str path, a `pathlib.Path`,
          or a `pwdatatools.DataFile`.
        * **dst\_mode** – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError. Defaults to `'x'`.
        * **dst\_format** – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored
          in `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
          Defaults to `None`.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificWriteOptions*) – Defines the format-specific options for writing `dst`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.writing.format_specific` sets format-specific
          writing options, but this parameter overrides the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificWriteOptions`. See the docs for a list
          of all classes that are format-specific write options. Some
          examples are `ADSWriteOptions`,
          `MDIFWriteOptions`, [`ADSWriteInvalid`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid"), and
          [`MDIFWriteInvalid`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid").
        * **loc** (*str*) – The write location within `dst`. Only applies to file
          formats that support multiple levels of hierarchy (for
          example, native and HDF5 formats). This parameter sets the
          write location within the file’s directory-like (or tree-like)
          structure. The “root” location is ‘/’. Defaults to `'/'`.
        * **iffs** (*bool*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing. Defaults to `False`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Examples

    The below examples assume that `block` is an instance of
    [`LoadPullBlock`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock") and
    the folllowing imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, you can
    simply input `dst` as a str, `pathlib.Path`, or
    `DataFile`. Known file
    extensions are stored in `options.writing.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> block.to_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> block.to_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # DataFile with known ext (.pwdt)
    >>> block.to_file(DataFile('/home/noise_param.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a missing or non-standard file
    extension, you can use the `dst_format` parameter. Alternatively,
    you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `dst`
    argument and use the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to
    define the destination’s format.

    ```
    >>> # no ext solved by dst_format
    >>> block.to_file('/data/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # non-standard ext solved by dst_format
    >>> block.to_file('/data/noise_param.txt', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # no ext solved by DataFile
    >>> block.to_file(DataFile('/data/noise_param', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> block.to_file('/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')  # overwrite dst if it exists
    >>> block.to_file('/noise_param.mdf', dst_mode='a') # append to dst if it exists
    >>> block.to_file('/run1.pwdt', dst_mode='a') # append to dst if it exists
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.to\_pandas\_dataframe[](#loadpullblock-to-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.to\_pandas\_dataframe(*\**, *index: Literal['default', 'ivars'] = 'default'*, *cols\_nlevels: int = 0*, *cols\_level\_names: bool = True*, *cols\_dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *cols\_dimscales\_pref: Literal[None, 'nums', 'names'] = None*, *cols\_default\_ints: Literal[1, 0] = 1*, *cols\_default\_ints\_forced: bool = False*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a pandas DataFrame from this Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **index** (*{‘default’, ‘ivars’}, default ‘default’*) – The index to use for the rows in the returned DataFrame. If
          `'default'`, use a default pandas integer RangeIndex. If
          `'ivars'`, use the idxs (preferred) or the values of the
          independent variables (ivars) to create a pandas Index or
          MultiIndex. If `'ivars'` is the chosen option but this
          Block does not have ivars, a default integer RangeIndex is created.
        * **cols\_nlevels** (*int, default 0*) – The number of levels to use for MultiIndex cols. If `-1`,
          let the max number of dims in the Vars determine the number of
          levels. If `0`, use a flat Index for the cols (instead of
          MultiIndex). If `1`, use a MultiIndex with 1 level. If the
          value is greater than max ndim + 1, fill the extra levels with
          empty strings.
        * **cols\_level\_names** (*bool, default True*) – If `True` set the names of the levels in the returned
          MultiIndex to `'varname'`, `'i'`, `'j'`, etc. Ignored if
          the return cols Index is flat.
        * **cols\_dimscales\_delim** (*3-tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. A 3-tuple of str
          characters used for embedding dimscales into the varnames in a
          flat string columns index. The first character is the opening
          delimiter, the second is the delimiter between dimscales, and
          the third is the closing delimiter. For example, the default
          delimiters would create varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and
          `'PortZ[1]'`.
        * **cols\_dimscales\_pref** (*{None, ‘nums’, ‘names’}, optional*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The type of
          dimension scales preferred for the DataFrame columns if the
          Vars’ dims are used. If `'nums'`, use the numeric
          dimscales. If `'names'`, use the string dimscales.
          If `None` (the default), prefer numeric dimscales if they
          exist, otherwise use string dimscales. In all cases, if the
          dimscales are not present, use the integers set by
          cols\_default\_ints.
        * **cols\_default\_ints** (*{1, 0}, default 1*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The integer-based
          indexing to use for the columns in the event that any Var’s
          dimscales are not used. If `1`, use 1-based ints for the
          index labels. If `0`, use 0-based ints.
        * **cols\_default\_ints\_forced** (*bool, default False*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. If `True`, use
          the integers set by cols\_default\_ints for all columns in the
          DataFrame, even if the Var’s dimscales are present. If `False`
          prefer the dimscales if they exist.

    Returns:
    :   A pandas DataFrame with the Block’s data.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame

    Notes

    The dtypes in the DataFrame are automatically set based on the
    dtypes of the data in the Block, taking into account whether Vars
    have nulls or not.

    Examples

    Let’s create a sample Block and convert it to a pandas DataFrame
    using the default settings. Here, we create a Block with two
    ivars and three dvars. Two of the dvars are multi-dimensional:
    the `PortR` variable has two dimensions, and the `magS`
    variable has three dimensions.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Block
    >>> pd.options.display.width = 200  # all DataFrame printouts use this width
    >>> data = {
    ...     "sample": [1, 2, 1, 2],
    ...     "bias": [4.1, 4.1, 5.2, 5.2],
    ...     "Pout": [30.5, 31.2, 29.4, 30.6],
    ...     "PortR": np.random.randint(30, 60, (4, 2)),
    ...     "magS": np.random.random((4, 2, 2)),
    ... }
    >>> block = Block(data, ivarnames=["sample", "bias"])
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    Each of the 1D variables are able to fit in one column, but the
    multi-dimensional variables are split into multiple columns. The
    columns for the multi-dimensional variables are created by
    embedding default integers into the varnames. The DataFrame’s
    index is a default RangeIndex.

    Let’s create a new DataFrame from this Block once again, but this
    time with a non-default index created from the Block’s ivars.
    This creates a MultiIndex from the Block’s independent variables
    since there are two ivars.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(index="ivars")
    >>> print(df)
                 Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    sample bias
    1      4.1   30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    2      4.1   31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    1      5.2   29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    2      5.2   30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    Now, let’s explore the options for the columns. In the absence
    of dimensional metadata for the multi-dimensional variables,
    the default integers are used for the column labels. There
    are two options for the default integers: 0-based and 1-based.
    Let’s use 0-based integers.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_default_ints=0)
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR[0]  PortR[1]  magS[0,0]  magS[0,1]  magS[1,0]  magS[1,1]
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    By default square brackets and commas are used as the delimiters
    for embedding the dimension scales into the column labels for
    multi-dimensional variables. Let’s change the delimiters to
    parentheses and semicolons.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_dimscales_delim=('(', ';', ')'))
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR(1)  PortR(2)  magS(1;1)  magS(1;2)  magS(2;1)  magS(2;2)
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    Now, let’s add dimensional metadata to our multi-dimensional
    variables. This is done assigning an instance of [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")
    to the [`Var.dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims") attribute. Each instance of [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")
    may define numeric (“nums”) and/or string (“names”) dimension
    scales.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> block["PortR"].dims = Dims(ndim=2, i_nums=[5, 6], i_names=["Port5", "Port6"])
    >>> block["magS"].dims = Dims(
    ...     ndim=3,
    ...     i_nums=[3, 4],
    ...     i_names=["P3", "P4"],
    ...     j_nums=[1, 2],
    ...     j_names=["P1", "P2"],
    ... )
    ```

    Now, let’s create a pandas DataFarme using the default settings.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR[5]  PortR[6]  magS[3,1]  magS[3,2]  magS[4,1]  magS[4,2]
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    As you can see, the column labels now use the numeric dimension
    scales. We can optionally use the string dimension scales instead.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_dimscales_pref="names")
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR['Port5']  PortR['Port6']  magS['P3','P1']  magS['P3','P2']  magS['P4','P1']  magS['P4','P2']
    0       1   4.1  30.5              51              37         0.893557         0.767820         0.689058         0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2              45              44         0.513656         0.677648         0.320666         0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4              39              43         0.376806         0.278813         0.136329         0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6              42              52         0.871437         0.230952         0.506557         0.064608
    ```

    We can choose to use the default integers for the column labels
    instead of the dimension scales.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_default_ints_forced=True)
    >>> print(df)
       sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    0       1   4.1  30.5        51        37   0.893557   0.767820   0.689058   0.205936
    1       2   4.1  31.2        45        44   0.513656   0.677648   0.320666   0.210212
    2       1   5.2  29.4        39        43   0.376806   0.278813   0.136329   0.987978
    3       2   5.2  30.6        42        52   0.871437   0.230952   0.506557   0.064608
    ```

    Instead of creating flat columns, we can create a MultiIndex.
    if we use `cols_nlevels=-1`, the columns will have as many
    levels as the maximum number of dimensions in the Vars.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1)
    >>> print(df)
    varname sample bias  Pout PortR          magS
    i                             5   6         3                   4
    j                                           1         2         1         2
    0            1  4.1  30.5    51  37  0.893557  0.767820  0.689058  0.205936
    1            2  4.1  31.2    45  44  0.513656  0.677648  0.320666  0.210212
    2            1  5.2  29.4    39  43  0.376806  0.278813  0.136329  0.987978
    3            2  5.2  30.6    42  52  0.871437  0.230952  0.506557  0.064608
    ```

    We can optionally turn off the level names in the columns if we
    are using a MultiIndex.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1, cols_level_names=False)
    >>> print(df)
      sample bias  Pout PortR          magS
                            5   6         3                   4
                                          1         2         1         2
    0      1  4.1  30.5    51  37  0.893557  0.767820  0.689058  0.205936
    1      2  4.1  31.2    45  44  0.513656  0.677648  0.320666  0.210212
    2      1  5.2  29.4    39  43  0.376806  0.278813  0.136329  0.987978
    3      2  5.2  30.6    42  52  0.871437  0.230952  0.506557  0.064608
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.tricontourplot.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.tricontourplot[](#loadpullblock-tricontourplot "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.tricontourplot(*z: str*, *\**, *ax=None*, *sca: bool = False*, *levels: int | ArrayLike | None = None*, *add\_xlabel: bool = True*, *add\_ylabel: bool = True*, *xlabel: str | None = None*, *ylabel: str | None = None*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.tricontourplot "Link to this definition")
:   Plot contour lines on a matplotlib Axes using triangulation.

    This method is similar to `contourplot` except that this method
    uses triangulation instead of interpolating onto a regular grid.
    Therefore, it can directly support irregular scattered data.

    A matplotlib TriContourSet object is returned, which can be used
    to further customize the plot. If you need access to the
    underlying Axes object, use the TriContourSet’s `axes`
    attribute.

    LoadPullBlocks with a power ivar or outer ivars are not supported.
    If you want to plot contours for a LoadPullBlock with a power
    ivar, you must first get rid of the power dependnecy by selecting
    a subset of the data. There are several ways of doing this,
    including `LoadPullBlock.loc` and the following
    LoadPullBlock methods: [`at_power()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power"), [`at_gcomp()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp"), and
    `xs()`.

    The same limitation is true of LoadPullBlocks with outer ivar(s),
    such as frequency, bias, etc. You must select a subset of the
    data before plotting.

    Parameters:
    :   * **z** – Name of the z variable to plot. The x and y variables are
          always the real and imaginary parts of the gamma ivar.
        * **ax** – The matplotlib Axes to plot on. If `None`, use the
          current axes. Defaults to `None`.
        * **sca** – A bool that determines whether to set the Axes used for plotting
          as the current Axes. Invokes `matplotlib.pyplot.sca()`.
          Defaults to `False`.
        * **levels** (*int or array-like, optional*) – Determines the number and positions of the contour lines or
          regions. If an int, use matplotlib’s MaxNLocator, which tries to
          automatically choose no more than n+1 “nice” contour levels
          between minimum and maximum numeric values of Z. If array-like,
          draw contour lines at the specified levels. The values must be in
          increasing order.
        * **add\_xlabel** – If `True`, add an x label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **add\_ylabel** – If `True`, add a y label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **xlabel** – If `add_xlabel` is `True`, use this as the x label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use the
          name of the x variable. If name of the variable is unknown, use
          the default of label, which is `'x'`. Defaults to `None`.
        * **ylabel** – If `add_ylabel` is `True`, use this as the y label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use the
          name of the y variable. If name of the variable is unknown, use
          the default of label, which is `'y'`. Defaults to `None`.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Additional keyword arguments are passed on to
          `matplotlib.pyplot.tricontour`. See the matplotlib documentation
          for more info.

    Return type:
    :   matplotlib.contour.TriContourSet


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.update.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.update[](#loadpullblock-update "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.update(*other=None*, *\*\*kwargs*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.update "Link to this definition")
:   Update the Block with Vars and/or data from another mapping.

    Parameters:
    :   * **other** (*mapping of str to VarLike, optional*) – If a VarLike object is an ArrayLike, it is assumed to be the
          Var’s data, and a Var is created. If a [`Var`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var"), it is
          directly added to the Block.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Vars and/or data to add to the Block.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If any value in other is not a Var and the varname already
        exists. In that scenario, use the [`set_data()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data")
        method instead.
