LoadPullBlock.z\_idx() → [DimScale](keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale "keysight.pwdatatools._core.indexing.DimScale") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_idx "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_ivar.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.z\_ivar[](#loadpullblock-z-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.z\_ivar(*form: Literal['complex', 'real', 'imag', 'mag', 'angle', 'polar\_str', 'rect\_str'] = 'complex'*, *\*\*kwargs*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return the impedance independent variable (ivar) in the form specified.

    If an impedance is not present in the ivars, return None.
    A variable is considered to be a Z if its role base is “impedance”
    in the LoadPullBlock’s metadata.

    Parameters:
    :   **form** – The form of the Z ivar to return. Must be one of
        `"complex"`, `"real"`, `"imag"`, `"mag"`, `"angle"`,
        `"polar_str"`, or `"rect_str"`. Defaults to `"complex"`.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **enable\_prefix** (*bool*) – If `True`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          prefixed with the real\_prefix, imag\_prefix, mag\_prefix,
          or angle\_prefix, depending on the value of `form`. If
          `False`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          not prefixed. The default is `True`.
        * **enable\_suffix** (*bool*) – If `True`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          suffixed with the real\_suffix, imag\_suffix, mag\_suffix,
          or angle\_suffix, depending on the value of `form`. If
          `False`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          not suffixed. The default is `True`.
        * **real\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="real"`. The default is `calc.REAL_PREFIX_DEFAULT`.
        * **real\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="real"`. The default is `""`.
        * **imag\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="imag"`. The default is `calc.IMAG_PREFIX_DEFAULT`.
        * **imag\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="imag"`. The default is `""`.
        * **mag\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="mag"`. The default is `calc.MAG_PREFIX_DEFAULT`.
        * **mag\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="mag"`. The default is `""`.
        * **angle\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="angle"`. The default is `calc.ANGLE_PREFIX_DEFAULT`.
        * **angle\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="angle"`. The default is `""`.
        * **angle\_deg** (*bool*) – If `True`, the angle values are returned in degrees.
          If `False`, the angle are returned in radians.The default
          is `True`.

    Returns:
    :   The Z ivar in the form specified, or None if there is no Z ivar.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var") | *None*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> lpblock = LoadPullBlock(...)
    >>> real_z_ivar = lpblock.z_ivar("real")
    >>> imag_z_ivar = lpblock.z_ivar("imag")
    >>> # Get rectangular string representation of Z ivar,
    >>> # which is ideal for plotting labels:
    >>> rect_str_z_ivar = lpblock.z_ivar("rect_str")
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_to_gamma.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.z\_to\_gamma[](#loadpullblock-z-to-gamma "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.z\_to\_gamma(*gamma\_name: str*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_to_gamma "Link to this definition")
:   Return LoadPullBlock with gamma sweep, converted from impedance sweep.

    Parameters:
    :   **gamma\_name** – The name of the new gamma variable added to the sweep and as a new
        column in the DataFrame.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> lpblock = LoadPullBlock(...)
    >>> lpblock_g = lpblock.z_to_gamma("GammaLoad")
    >>> lpblock_g.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock_g.z_ivarname is None
    True
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.zrefload.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock.zrefload[](#loadpullblock-zrefload "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.zrefload(*suffix: str | None = None*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.zrefload "Link to this definition")
:   Return a variable with the role “impedance.load.reference”.

    If there is only one zrefload variable, the `suffix` parameter
    is not needed. If there are multiple zrefload variables, the
    `suffix` parameter is required to select the desired one.
    This assumes that zrefload variables can be distinguished by
    suffixes on their names.

    Returns:
    :   The zrefload variable if it exists, otherwise None.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var") | *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If multiple zrefload variables are found and suffix is not provided.
          - If suffix is provided but doesn’t match the suffix of any zrefload
          variable’s name.

    Examples

    Many times, a LoadPullBlock will contain only one zrefload variable.
    In this case, the suffix parameter is not needed:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = LoadPullBlock(...)
    >>> zrefload_var = lpblock.zrefload()
    ```

    If there are multiple zrefload variables, the suffix parameter is
    required to select one. A common reason for having more than one zrefload
    variable is that zrefload varies vs. frequency. It is typical to use
    enumerated frequency suffixes, such as “\_F1”, “\_F2”, “\_F3”, etc. So the
    zrefload variable names might be “ZrefLoad\_F1”, “ZrefLoad\_F2”,
    “ZrefLoad\_F3”, etc.

    ```
    >>> lpblock = LoadPullBlock(...)
    >>> zrefload_var = lpblock.zrefload(suffix="F1") # this works
    >>> zrefload_var = lpblock.zrefload(suffix="_F1") # this works too
    >>> zrefload_var = lpblock.zrefload(suffix="1") # this also works
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.__init__.md === -->

# LoadPullDerivedVars.\_\_init\_\_[](#loadpullderivedvars-init "Link to this heading")

LoadPullDerivedVars.\_\_init\_\_(*\**, *main: Iterable[str] = FrozenRolesSet(frozenset({'gain.transducer', 'power.delivered.load', 'gain.power', 'power.delivered.input', 'distortion.ampm', 'power.available.source', 'efficiency.drain', 'gamma.load', 'gamma.input', 'power.available.input', 'efficiency.power\_added'}))*, *freq\_enums: Iterable[str] = FrozenRolesSet(frozenset({'gamma.load', 'gamma.input'}))*, *power\_units: Literal['dBm', 'mW', 'W'] = 'dBm'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize instance with optional kwargs.

    Enumerated frequencies are usually (but not necessarily)
    harmonically-related. There are options to individually control
    whether each derived variable is calculated at the enumerated
    frequencies.

    Parameters:
    :   * **main** (*iterable of str, default ALL\_DERIVED\_VARS\_ROLES*) – The derived variables that are calculated at the first
          enumerated frequency. Defaults to deriving all variables.
        * **freq\_enums** (*iterable of str, default DERIVED\_VARS\_FREQ\_ENUMS\_DEFAULT*) – The derived variables that are calculated at the enumerated
          frequencies. Defaults to deriving only the gamma.input and
          gamma.load variables.
        * **power\_units** (*{‘dBm’, ‘mW’, ‘W’}, default ‘dBm’*) – A string that determines the units of the power variables. Must
          be one of `"dBm"`, `"mW"`, or `"W"`.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md === -->

# LoadPullDerivedVars[](#loadpullderivedvars "Link to this heading")

*class* LoadPullDerivedVars[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "Link to this definition")
:   Define loadpull variables to be calculated from A/B Waves.

    This object is immutable, so its properties cannot be changed after
    instantiation.

    Notes

    Some loadpull data file formats contain A/B wave data, which can be
    used to calculate additional “derived” loadpull variables. You can
    control which variables are derived by setting the [`main`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.main.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.main "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.main") and
    [`freq_enums`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.freq_enums.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.freq_enums "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.freq_enums") attributes.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`freq_enums`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.freq_enums.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.freq_enums "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.freq_enums") | Return the enumerated derived variables. |
    | [`main`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.main.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.main "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.main") | Return the main derived variables. |
    | [`power_units`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.power_units.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.power_units "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.power_units") | Return the power units. |
    | [`read_or_write`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.read_or_write.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.read_or_write "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.read_or_write") | Return literal 'read' or 'write'. |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.__init__")(\*[, main, freq\_enums, power\_units]) | Initialize instance with optional kwargs. |
    | [`get_formats`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.get_formats.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.get_formats "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.get_formats")() | Return formats supported by this class. |
    | [`replace`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.replace.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.replace "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.replace")(\*\*kwargs) | Return a new instance with the specified properties replaced. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.__init__.md === -->

# LoadPullReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_[](#loadpullreadoptions-init "Link to this heading")

LoadPullReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_(*\**, *derived\_vars: [LoadPullDerivedVars](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullDerivedVars") | None = LoadPullDerivedVars(main=frozenset({'gain.transducer', 'power.delivered.load', 'gain.power', 'power.delivered.input', 'distortion.ampm', 'power.available.source', 'efficiency.drain', 'gamma.load', 'gamma.input', 'power.available.input', 'efficiency.power\_added'}), freq\_enums=frozenset({'gamma.load', 'gamma.input'}), power\_units='dBm')*, *always\_freq\_suffixed: Iterable[str] = {'gamma.input', 'gamma.load', 'gamma.source', 'impedance.input', 'impedance.load', 'impedance.source', 'wave.enumerated.incident', 'wave.enumerated.reflected'}*, *power\_ivar\_pref: Literal['power.available.input', 'power.available.source', None] = None*, *uniform\_ivars: bool = False*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize instance.

    Parameters:
    :   * **derived\_vars** (*LoadPullDerivedVars, optional*) – Derived loadpull variables to include in data. If `None`,
          no derived variables will be calculated. Defaults to
          `LOADPULL_DERIVED_VARS_DEFAULT`.
        * **always\_freq\_suffixed** (*iterable of str, default {‘gamma.input’, ‘gamma.load’}*) – Roles that have an explicit fundamental frequency suffix in their
          variable names, even when it’s the only frequency component present.
          See notes for more information.
        * **power\_ivar\_pref** (*POWER\_IVAR\_PREF\_DEFAULT, optional*) – If `"power.available.input"`, the power ivar is set to the
          power available at the input. If `"power.delivered.input."`,
          the power ivar is set to the power delivered to the input of the
          device. If `"power.available.source"`, the power ivar is set to
          the power available at the source. If `None`, the
          `"power.available.input"` is preferred, followed by
          `"power.delivered.input"`, and finally `"power.available.source"`.
        * **uniform\_ivars** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, frequency and power ivars will be added, even if
          they are not swept in the datafile.

    Notes

    Regarding the fund\_freq\_suffix parameter:
    Many variables are assumed to be at the fundamental frequency, and therefore
    often have their frequency suffixes omitted. For example gain, efficiency,
    AMPM, etc. are all typically understood to be at the fundamental frequency. In
    cases where one of these variables only has a fundamental frequency component,
    the frequency suffix is left off, and it is instead implicitly understood to
    be at the fundamental. For example, “Gain\_F1” becomes “Gain”.

    This set includes the variables for which there should ALWAYS be a frequency
    suffix, even when there is only a fundamental frequency component. For example,
    use the variable names “GammaLoad\_F1” and “GammaIn\_F1” instead of “GammaLoad”
    and “GammaIn”.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.md === -->

# LoadPullSweep[](#loadpullsweep "Link to this heading")

*class* LoadPullSweep[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep "Link to this definition")
:   An immutable object to store ivarnames and idxnames for a LoadPullBlock.

    Parameters:
    :   * **outer\_ivarnames** (*sequence of str, optional*) – Arbitrary names of the outer independent variables (ivars).
          Examples of outer swept variables are frequency, bias, and
          temperature. The outermost ivar should be first, and the
          innermost last. For example, if frequency is swept at each
          bias level, then `outer_ivarnames=["Bias", "Frequency"]`.
        * **outer\_idxnames** (*sequence of str, optional*) – Names of indexes for outer ivars. If `None`, then
          `outer_ivarnames` must also be `None`.
        * **gamma\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – Name of swept gamma variable (ivar). If `None`, then gamma
          is not swept.
        * **gamma\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – Name of gamma index. If `None`, then `gamma` must also be
          `None`.
        * **z\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – Name of swept impedance variable (ivar). If `None`, then
          impedance is not swept.
        * **z\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – Name of impedance index variable. If `None`, then z must
          also be `None`.
        * **power\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – Name of swept power variable (ivar). If `None`, then power
          is not swept.
        * **power\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – Name of power index variable. If `None`, then power must
          also be `None`.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`gamma_idxname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_idxname") | Name of gamma index variable (str or None). |
    | [`gamma_ivarname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_ivarname") | Name of gamma variable (str or None). |
    | [`gamma_or_z_idxname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_idxname") | Return name of gamma or z index variable (str or None). |
    | [`gamma_or_z_ivarname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_ivarname") | Return name of gamma or z indepedent variable (str or None). |
    | [`idxnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames") | Names of the index variables (idxs) as a tuple of str. |
    | [`idxnames_map`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames_map.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames_map "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames_map") | A frozen mapping of varnames to idxnames. |
    | [`ivarnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.ivarnames") | Names of the independent variables (ivars) as a tuple of str. |
    | [`outer_idxnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_idxnames") | Names of the outer index variables (outer idxs) as a tuple of str. |
    | [`outer_ivarnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_ivarnames") | Names of the outer independent variables (outer ivars) as a tuple of str. |
    | [`power_idxname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_idxname") | Name of power index variable (str or None). |
    | [`power_ivarname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_ivarname") | Name of power variable (str or None). |
    | [`z_idxname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_idxname") | Name of impedance index variable (str or None). |
    | [`z_ivarname`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_ivarname") | Name of impedance variable (str or None). |

# MDIFReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_[](#mdifreadoptions-init "Link to this heading")

MDIFReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_(*\**, *engine\_pref: Literal['python', 'ads'] | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFReadOptions.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize MDIFReadOptions instance.

    Parameters:
    :   **engine\_pref** – If `"python"`, MDIFs will be directly read using an engine
        written in Python. If `"ads"`, the MDIF is converted to an
        intermediate ADS dataset, then the dataset is read using the
        `engine_pref` setting for ADS reading. If `None`, the
        Python engine is used. Defaults to `None`.

# MDIFWriteInvalid.\_\_init\_\_[](#mdifwriteinvalid-init "Link to this heading")

MDIFWriteInvalid.\_\_init\_\_(*boolean: Literal['float\_w\_nan', 'int\_w\_null'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *complexfloating: Literal['complex\_w\_nan'] = 'complex\_w\_nan'*, *floating: Literal['float\_w\_nan'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *integer: Literal['float\_w\_nan', 'int\_w\_null'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *string: Literal['str\_w\_null'] = 'str\_w\_null'*, *nan\_rep: float | str = 'NaN'*, *integer\_null\_rep: int = 99999*, *string\_null\_rep: str = 'null'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize instance of MDIFWriteInvalid.

    Parameters:
    :   * **boolean** (*{“float\_w\_nan”, “int\_w\_null”}, default “float\_w\_nan”*) – Setting for writing boolean variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **complexfloating** (*{“complex\_w\_nan”}, default “complex\_w\_nan”*) – Setting for writing complex variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **floating** (*{“float\_w\_nan”}, default “float\_w\_nan”*) – Setting for writing float variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **integer** (*{“float\_w\_nan”, “int\_w\_null”}, default “float\_w\_nan”*) – Setting for writing integer variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **string** (*{“str\_w\_null”}, default “str\_w\_null”*) – Setting for writing string variables with invalid
          datapoints.
        * **nan\_rep** (*Union[float, str], default “NaN”*) – Setting for writing NaN values in complex or float
          variables.
        * **integer\_null\_rep** (*int, default 99999*) – Setting for writing null values in integer variables.
        * **string\_null\_rep** (*str, default “null”*) – Setting for writing null values in string variables.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.md === -->

# MDIFWriteInvalid[](#mdifwriteinvalid "Link to this heading")

*class* MDIFWriteInvalid[](#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid "Link to this definition")
:   Options for writing variables with invalid datapoints to MDIF.

    Each of the 5 generic datatypes has one or more available options for
    writing invalid data to MDIFs. Additionally, there are options for
    NaN and null representation in MDIF files. NaN values only apply to
    floating and complexfloating datatypes.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`boolean`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean") | Option for boolean data. |
    | [`boolean_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean_options "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean_options") | All valid options for [`boolean`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.boolean") property. |
    | [`complexfloating`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.complexfloating.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.complexfloating "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.complexfloating") | Return option for complexfloating data. |
    | [`complexfloating_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.complexfloating_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.complexfloating_options "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.complexfloating_options") | Return all options for complexfloating data. |
    | [`floating`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.floating.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.floating "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.floating") | Return option for floating data. |
    | [`floating_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.floating_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.floating_options "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.floating_options") | Return all options for floating data. |
    | [`integer`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer") | Return option for integer data. |
    | [`integer_null_rep`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer_null_rep.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer_null_rep "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer_null_rep") | Return int that represents null values. |
    | [`integer_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer_options "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.integer_options") | Return all options for integer data. |
    | [`nan_rep`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.nan_rep.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.nan_rep "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.nan_rep") | Return float or str that represents nan values. |
    | [`read_or_write`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.read_or_write.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.read_or_write "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.read_or_write") | Return literal 'read' or 'write'. |
    | [`string`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string") | Return option for string data. |
    | [`string_null_rep`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string_null_rep.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string_null_rep "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string_null_rep") | Return str that represents null values. |
    | [`string_options`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string_options.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string_options "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.string_options") | Return all options for string data. |

    Methods

    |  |  |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.read_file.md === -->

# read\_file[](#read-file "Link to this heading")

read\_file(*src: StrPathLike | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *loc: Literal['/'] = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.read_file "Link to this definition")

read\_file(*src: StrPathLike | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *loc: str*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group") | [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")
:   Read a datafile and return a Group or Block.

    Known readable file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extensions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Group or Block.
          If `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read and
          returned as a Group. Otherwise, it represents some portion of the
          source file. This may be the name of a single Block (for example,
          in an MDIF or ADS dataset), or the full path to a Group or Block
          (e.g. `'/G1/X'`, where ‘G1’ is the name of a Group and ‘X’ is
          the name of the desired Group or Block).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are [`ADSReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions"),
          [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions"), and [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Returns:
    :   A [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group") or [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block") containing the contents of
        `src`. If `loc` is `'/'`, a [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group") is always
        returned. Otherwise, a [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block") is returned if the contents
        of `loc` can be represented by a single [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block"). If the
        contents of `loc` cannot be represented by a single
        [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block"), a [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group") is returned.

    Return type:
    :   *Union*[[*Group*](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group"), [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")]

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in
    `pwdatatools.options.reading.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> g1 = pwdt.read_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # Using a pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> g2 = pwdt.read_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # Using a DataFile with known ext (.mdm)
    >>> b3 = pwdt.read_file(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use the
    `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `src`
    argument and use DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to define
    the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using src_format parameter since src has missing ext
    >>> g1 = pwdt.read_file('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using src_format parameter since src has non-standard ext
    >>> g2 = pwdt.read_file('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using DataFile with format_override
    >>> b1 = pwdt.read_file(pwdt.DataFile('/data/noise.txt', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter. You
    have to know the name of the part that you want to read from `src`.
    A Group or Block may be returned, depending on the contents of the
    part.

    ```
    >>> # partial read of ADS datasets always returns a Block
    >>> block1 = pwdt.read_file('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP1.SP')
    >>> # partial read of a .pwdt file may return Group or Block
    >>> group1 = pwdt.read_file('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/Network')
    ```

    See also

    [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group"), [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.read_file_as_block.md === -->

# read\_file\_as\_block[](#read-file-as-block "Link to this heading")

read\_file\_as\_block(*src: StrPathLike | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.read_file_as_block "Link to this definition")
:   Read a datafile and return a new instance of Block.

    Known readable file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extensions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **loc,** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Block. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents some portion of the source file. This
          may be the name of a single Block (for example, in an MDIF or
          ADS dataset), or the full path to a Group or Block (e.g.
          `'/G1/X'`, where ‘G1’ is the name of a Group and ‘X’ is the
          name of the desired Block).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are [`ADSReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions"),
          [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions"), and [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If data cannot be represented as a single Block.

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.lpd)
    >>> b1 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/home/device.lpd')
    >>> # Using a pathlib.Path with known ext (.spl)
    >>> b2 = pwdt.read_file_as_block(Path('/home/device.spl'))
    >>> # Using a DataFile with a known ext (.mdm)
    >>> b3 = pwdt.read_file_as_block(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use
    the `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an
    instance of `DataFile` as
    the `src` argument and use DataFile’s `format_override`
    parameter to define the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using the ``src_format`` parameter since the file extension is missing
    >>> b1 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using the ``src_format`` parameter since the file extension is non-standard
    >>> b2 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using a DataFile with ``format_override`` is another option
    >>> b3 = pwdt.read_file_as_block(pwdt.DataFile('/noise', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter.
    You have to know the name of the part that you want to read from
    `src`. A Block is always returned. If the contents of the part
    cannot be represented by a single Block, a ValueError is raised.

    ```
    >>> b1 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP1.SP')
    >>> b2 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/Network')
    ```

    See also

    `Block.from_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.read_file_as_group.md === -->

# read\_file\_as\_group[](#read-file-as-group "Link to this heading")

read\_file\_as\_group(*src: StrPathLike | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.read_file_as_group "Link to this definition")
:   Read a datafile and return a new instance of Group.

    Known readable file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extensions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Group. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents some portion of the source file. This
          may be the full path to a Group or Block (e.g. `'/parent/X'`,
          where ‘parent’ is the name of the parent Group and ‘X’ is the
          name of the desired Group or Block). If the contents of `loc`
          can be represented by a single Block, the Block is placed into a
          Group and the Group is returned.
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are [`ADSReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSReadOptions"),
          [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions"), and [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in
    `pwdatatools.options.reading.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> g1 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # Using pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> g2 = pwdt.read_file_as_group(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # Using DataFile with a known ext (.mdm)
    >>> g3 = pwdt.read_file_as_group(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use the
    `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `src`
    argument and use DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to define
    the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using the `src_format` parameter since the file extension is missing
    >>> g1 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using the `src_format` parameter since the file extension is non-standard
    >>> g2 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using DataFile's `format_override` parameter is another option
    >>> g3 = pwdt.read_file_as_group(
    ...     pwdt.DataFile('/noise.txt', format_override='mdif')
    ... )
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter. You
    have to know the name of the part that you want to read from `src`.
    A Group will always be returned, even if the contents could be
    contained within a single Block (the Block is placed into a Group and
    the Group is returned).

    ```
    >>> # partial read of ADS dataset returns Group containing one Block
    >>> group1 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP1.SP')
    >>> # partial read of a .pwdt file
    >>> group2 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/Network')
    ```

    See also

    `Group.from_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.read_file_as_loadpullblock.md === -->

# read\_file\_as\_loadpullblock[](#read-file-as-loadpullblock "Link to this heading")

read\_file\_as\_loadpullblock(*src: StrPathLike | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *\**, *format\_specific: [LoadPullReadOptions](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions") | [LoadPullDerivedVars](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars") | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [LoadPullBlock](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.read_file_as_loadpullblock "Link to this definition")
:   Read a load pull datafile and return a LoadPullBlock.

    The source file must have a known file extension that is associated
    with Keysight, Focus, or Maury load pull files. Known readable load
    pull file extensions are stored in
    `options.reading.format_to_extension_map['loadpull']`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are [`LoadPullReadOptions`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions") and
          [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars").
        * **varnames** (*mapping of str to str, optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    Using all the default options:
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.read\_file\_as\_loadpullblock(“/data/loadpull.cst”)

    Modifying global options to control how variables are named and
    which variables are derived from a and b waves:

    ```
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.varnames["power.delivered.load"] = "Pout"
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.format_specific.loadpull.derived_vars.ampm = False
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.read_file_as_loadpullblock("/data/loadpull.cst")
    ```

    Passing in a dict of roles to varnames and an instance of
    [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars") to override the global settings:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.read_file_as_loadpullblock(
    ...     "/data/loadpull.cst",
    ...     varnames={'gamma.load': 'GL'},
    ...     format_specific=pwdt.LoadPullDerivedVars(ampm=False),
    ... )
    ```

    See also

    `LoadPullBlock.from_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.roles[](#module-keysight.pwdatatools.roles "Link to this heading")

The PathWave Data Tools roles module.

The `keysight.pwdatatools` library uses a concept of “roles” to
classify variables according to the functions they serve in a dataset.
Roles are used to generate variable names, find variables and blocks
in hierarchical datasets, and to make methods and functions easier to use.

See also

See the [Use Roles](../../howto/use_roles.md#how-to-use-roles) section of the documentation for more information.

Here are the functions and classes available in this module:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`VARNAMES_DEFAULT`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.VARNAMES_DEFAULT.md#keysight.pwdatatools.roles.VARNAMES_DEFAULT "keysight.pwdatatools.roles.VARNAMES_DEFAULT") | An immutable mapping of variable roles to their names. |
| [`FrozenRolesSet`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.FrozenRolesSet.md#keysight.pwdatatools.roles.FrozenRolesSet "keysight.pwdatatools.roles.FrozenRolesSet") | An immutable set of roles. |
| [`RolesSet`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.RolesSet.md#keysight.pwdatatools.roles.RolesSet "keysight.pwdatatools.roles.RolesSet") | A mutable set of roles. |
| [`finalize_role`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.finalize_role.md#keysight.pwdatatools.roles.finalize_role "keysight.pwdatatools.roles.finalize_role")(role) | Finalize and validate a role. |
| [`is_subrole`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.is_subrole.md#keysight.pwdatatools.roles.is_subrole "keysight.pwdatatools.roles.is_subrole")(first\_role, second\_role) | Return bool indicating whether first role is a subrole of the second role. |
| [`is_valid_role`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.is_valid_role.md#keysight.pwdatatools.roles.is_valid_role "keysight.pwdatatools.roles.is_valid_role")(role) | Return True if role is a valid role. |

# SMatrixIOReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_[](#smatrixioreadoptions-init "Link to this heading")

SMatrixIOReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_(*\**, *engine\_pref: Literal['python', 'ads'] | None = None*, *network\_blockname: str = 'Network'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.SMatrixIOReadOptions.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize SMatrixIOReadOptions instance.

    Parameters:
    :   * **engine\_pref** (*{None, “python”, “ads”}, default None*) – If `"python"`, SMatrixIO files are read using a Python engine.
          If `"ads"`, the file is converted to an intermediate ADS
          dataset, then the dataset is read using the `engine_pref`
          setting for ADS reading. If `None`, internal logic is used to
          determine which engine to use.
        * **network\_blockname** (*str, default “Network”*) – Name of the block containing the network parameters in the
          SMatrixIO file.

TouchstoneReadOptions.\_\_init\_\_(*\**, *engine\_pref: Literal['rust', 'python', 'ads'] | None = None*, *network\_blockname: str = 'Network'*, *noise\_blockname: str = 'Noise'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.TouchstoneReadOptions.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize TouchstoneReadOptions instance.

    Parameters:
    :   * **engine\_pref** (*{None, “python”, “rust”, “ads”}, default None*) – If `"rust"`, a Rust-based Touchstone parser is used. If
          `"python"`, a Python-based engine is used. If `"ads"`,
          the file is converted to an intermediate ADS dataset, then
          the dataset is read using the ``` engine_pref``setting for
          ADS reading. If ``None ```, internal logic is used to
          determine which engine to use (which usually picks “rust”).
        * **network\_blockname** (*str, default “Network”*) – Name of the network parameters block in the Touchstone file.
        * **noise\_blockname** (*str, default “Noise”*) – Name of the noise block in the Touchstone file.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.translate_file.md === -->

# translate\_file[](#translate-file "Link to this heading")

translate\_file(*src: StrPathLike | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *dst: StrPathLike | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\*\*kwargs*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.translate_file "Link to this definition")
:   Translate a datafile from one format to another.

    There are several supported source and destination formats. The
    formats can be set explicitly using optional keyword parameters
    `src_format` and `dst_format`. However, if `src` and `dst` have
    recognized file extensions, the formats can be automatically
    inferred and the keyword arguments do not need to be used.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path. If `src_format=None`, attempts are
          made to ascertain the format by examining the source file’s
          extension. Known file extensions are stored in
          `options.reading.extensions`.
        * **dst** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path. If `dst_format=None`, attempts are
          made to ascertain the format by examining the destination file’s
          extension. Known file extensions are stored in
          `options.writing.extensions`.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other than `None`
          overrides the format inferred from or set by `src`.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Notes

    This function always uses the global format-specific read and write
    options. The only way to override the global options is to perform
    translation as a two-step process (read then write).

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import translate_file, DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the source file `src` and destination file `dst` both have
    known file extensions, simply input `src` and `dst` and nothing
    else.

    ```
    >>> # str src & dst with known exts (.s4p and .ds)
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param.s4p', '/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # pathlib.Path src & dst with known exts (.ds and .mdf)
    >>> translate_file(Path('/home/noise.ds'), Path('/home/noise.mdf'))
    >>> # str src & DataFile dst with known exts (.ds and .pwdt)
    >>> translate_file('/home/noise.ds', DataFile('/home/noise.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the the source file and/or destination file have missing or unknown
    file extensions, use the `src_format` and/or `dst_format` parameters.
    Alternatively, input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `src` and/or
    `dst` arguments and use DataFile’s `format_override` parameter
    to define the format(s).

    ```
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param', '/home/s_param.ds', src_format='touchstone')
    >>> translate_file('/noise', '/noise_new', src_format='ads', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # Or, use DataFiles:
    >>> src = DataFile('/home/noise', format_override='mdif')
    >>> dst = DataFile('/home/noise_translated', format_override='pwdt')
    >>> translate_file(src, dst)
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> # overwrite dst, if it exists
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param.mdf', '/home/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')
    >>> # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param.ds', '/home/s_param.mdf', dst_mode='a')
    >>> # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param.ds', '/home/s_param.pwdt', dst_mode='a')
    ```

    See also

    `DataFile.translate()`
    `read_file()`
    `write_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__init__.md === -->

# Var.\_\_init\_\_[](#var-init "Link to this heading")

Var.\_\_init\_\_(*data: ArrayLike*, *dtype: [DataType](keysight.pwdatatools.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools.DataType") | Type[[DataType](keysight.pwdatatools.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools.DataType")] | None = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *dims: [Dims](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims") | None = None*, *role: str | None = None*, *unit: str | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize a new Var instance with data and optional metadata.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*ArrayLike*) – An array-like object containing the data values.
        * **dtype** (*DataType, optional*) – The datatype. If not specified, it is inferred from the data.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the variable. If not specified, the name is set
          to an empty string.
        * **dims** (*Dims, optional*) – Optional metadata relating to the variable’s dimensions.
        * **role** (*str, optional*) – Metadata defining the part or function this variable serves
          within a dataset (for example, ‘frequency’ or ‘gamma.load’).
        * **unit** (*str, optional*) – An optional string representing the unit of the variable.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – An optional dict or dict-like object with arbitrary attributes
          (metadata) for the variable. The keys must be str and the values
          must be str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.

    Examples

    Below we create a 1D Var. The data is required, but all other
    parameters are optional.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.array([1, 2, 3])
    >>> vout = Var(data, name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    >>> vout.info()
    kind           -
    role     voltage
    dtype      Int32
    shape       (3,)
    dims           -
    unit           V
    min            1
    max            3
    null           -
    nan            -
    attrs          -
    Name: Vout, dtype: string
    ```

    Now, let’s instantiate a 1D Var with a numpy MaskedArray to represent
    missing or null data points. Note that the masked value(s) become
    the null value(s) and are not included in the min and max values.

    ```
    >>> ma = np.ma.masked_array([3, 4], mask=[True, False])
    >>> v_masked = Var(ma, name='masked')
    >>> v_masked.info()
    kind         -
    role         -
    dtype    Int32
    shape     (2,)
    dims         -
    unit         -
    min          4
    max          4
    null         1
    nan          -
    attrs        -
    Name: masked, dtype: string
    ```

    Next, let’s create a 2D Var.

    ```
    >>> data = np.random.random(6).reshape((3, 2))
    >>> portz = Var(data, name='PortZ', role='impedance.port', unit='Ohm')
    ```

    Using pandas or polars can be very complementary to pwdatatools. Here,
    let’s create a pandas DataFrame in order to more easily visualize our
    2D Var. Note that 1-based integer indexes are used for the higher
    dimension (the numbers inside of the square brackets).

    Note

    There are other options for indexing into the higher dimensions
    of a variable, such as 0-based integer and string name indexes.

    ```
    >>> df = portz.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
    PortZ[1]  PortZ[2]
    0  0.551544  0.467092
    1  0.813803  0.629148
    2  0.146644  0.529128
    ```

    Or, if you prefer, you can use MultiIndex columns to get rid of the delimiters.

    ```
    >>> df = portz.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=2)
    >>> print(df)
    varname     PortZ
    i               1         2
    0        0.551544  0.467092
    1        0.813803  0.629148
    2        0.146644  0.529128
    ```

    See also

    For more information on pandas DataFrame indexing, see
    [pandas DataFrame Indexing](../../core_concepts/pandas_dataframe_indexing.md#pandas-dataframe-indexing) and also the pandas documentation.

    Finally, let’s create a 3D Var. This time, we have two higher
    dimensions (always named “i” and “j”). Let’s define string names to
    use as indexes along these higher dimensions instead of integers.

    ```
    >>> data = np.random.random(20).reshape((5, 2, 2))
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=3, i_names=['P1', 'P2'], j_names=['P1', 'P2'])
    >>> s = Var(data, name='S', dims=dims, role='s_parameters')
    >>> df = s.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=3)
    >>> print(df)
    varname         S
    i              P1                  P2
    j              P1        P2        P1        P2
    0        0.551284  0.070031  0.586010  0.885456
    1        0.626966  0.234664  0.932699  0.882455
    2        0.137112  0.213657  0.657413  0.388838
    3        0.410304  0.545891  0.158400  0.034383
    4        0.009548  0.688676  0.918804  0.848939
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.add_role.md === -->

# Var.add\_role[](#var-add-role "Link to this heading")

Var.add\_role(*role: str*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.add_role "Link to this definition")
:   Add a role to this Var.

    If the role to be added is equal to or a subrole of the existing
    role, then the existing role is not changed. Otherwise, the new
    role replaces the existing role.

    Parameters:
    :   **role** (*str*) – The role to add to this Var.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain')
    >>> gain.role
    ''
    >>> gain.add_role('gain')
    >>> gain.role
    'gain'
    >>> gain.add_role('gain.power')
    >>> gain.role
    'gain.power'
    >>> gain.add_role('gain')
    >>> gain.role
    'gain.power'
    ```

    If we try to add a role with a different role base, we get an
    error.

    ```
    >>> gain.add_role('impedance')
    <ValueError: Cannot add role 'impedance' to Var with role 'gain.power'.>
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.attrs.md === -->

# Var.attrs[](#var-attrs "Link to this heading")

*property* Var.attrs*: [AttrsDict](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools._core.properties.AttrsDict")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.attrs "Link to this definition")
:   Get or set a mapping of arbitrary attributes (metadata).

    The attributes are stored in an [`AttrsDict`](keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict"), which is a
    type-restricted dict. The keys must be str, and the values must
    be one of the following types: str, int, float, complex, bool,
    None, `numpy.ndarray`, dict, or list. Nested dicts and
    lists are also supported.

    Examples

    Initialize a Var with no attrs.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain')
    ```

    Add some attrs to the empty AttrsDict.

    ```
    >>> gain.attrs['foo'] = 'bar'
    >>> gain.attrs['baz'] = 1
    >>> gain.attrs['qux'] = [1, 2, 3]
    >>> gain.attrs['quux'] = {'a': 1, 'b': 2}
    >>> gain.attrs
    AttrsDict({'foo': 'bar', 'baz': 1, 'qux': [1, 2, 3], 'quux': {'a': 1, 'b': 2}})
    ```

    Set the value of attrs.

    ```
    >>> gain.attrs = {'foo': 'bar'}
    >>> gain.attrs
    AttrsDict({'foo': 'bar'})
    ```

    Initialize a Var with attrs.

    ```
    >>> gain = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain', attrs={'foo': 'bar'})
    >>> gain.attrs
    AttrsDict({'foo': 'bar'})
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.block.md === -->

# Var.block[](#var-block "Link to this heading")

*property* Var.block*: BaseBlock | None*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.block "Link to this definition")
:   Get the Block that contains this Var, or else None if this Var is not contained in a Block.

    This property is automatically set when a Var is added to a Block,
    and is not settable by the user. It is used internally to manage the
    association between a Var and its Block.

    Any Var may be associated with one Block at most. If the Var is not
    associated with a Block, then this property is None.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy.md === -->

# Var.copy[](#var-copy "Link to this heading")

Var.copy() → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Return a deep copy of this Var.

    Returns:
    :   A deep copy of this Var.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([1, 2, 3], name='Gain', unit='dB', role='gain', attrs={'foo': 1})
    >>> gain2 = gain.copy()
    >>> gain2.name == gain.name
    True
    >>> gain2.attrs == gain.attrs
    True
    >>> gain2.attrs is gain.attrs
    False
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.count_observations.md === -->

# Var.count\_observations[](#var-count-observations "Link to this heading")

Var.count\_observations() → int[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.count_observations "Link to this definition")
:   Return the number of observations in this Var.

    The number of observations is equal to the size of the data along
    axis 0.

    Return type:
    :   *int*


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims.md === -->

# Var.dims[](#var-dims "Link to this heading")

*property* Var.dims*: [Dims](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools._core.indexing.Dims")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims "Link to this definition")
:   Get this Var’s dimensional metadata.

    It is an instance of [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims"), which can store scales that can be used for
    indexing along each of the variable’s dimensions.

    Examples

    THe [`Var.dims`](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims") attribute always stores an instance of [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims"), even
    if a Var is initialized without a dims argument.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> x = Var([10, 12, 13], name='x')
    >>> x.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=1,
        idx=None,
    )
    ```

    But the dims are empty, meaning that the Var’s dims have no scales.

    ```
    >>> x.dims.is_empty()
    True
    ```

    Any instance of [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims") can be initialized with an “idx” dim scale. This is
    an optional scale that can be used for indexing along the first dimension. The
    idx scale is always an unsigned integer index, but the integers are completely
    arbitrary.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var, Dims
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=1, idx=[100, 200, 300])
    >>> x = Var([10, 12, 13], name='x', dims=dims)
    >>> x.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=1,
        idx=[100, 200, 300],
    )
    ```

    The an “idx” dim scale is present, so the Var’s dims are not empty.

    ```
    >>> x.dims.is_empty()
    False
    ```

    The “idx” dim scale enables indexing along the first dimension using
    arbitrary integers, rather than just 0-based or 1-based integers.

    ```
    >>> x.select(idx=200)
    ```

    Initializing a 2D Var or 3D Var without explicitly providing a
    dims argument also results in empty Dims, just like the 1D case.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> data_2d = np.random.random(6).reshape((3, 2))
    >>> var_2d = Var(data_2d, name='var_2d')
    >>> var_2d.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=2,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=None,
        i_names=None,
    )
    >>> var_2d.dims.is_empty()
    True
    >>> data_3d = np.random.random(20).reshape((5, 2, 2))
    >>> var_3d = Var(data_3d, name='var_3d')
    >>> var_3d.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=3,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=None,
        i_names=None,
        j_nums=None,
        j_names=None,
    )
    >>> var_3d.dims.is_empty()
    True
    ```

    Now, let’s initialize a 2D Var with dims that include i\_names.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=2, i_names=['P1', 'P2'])
    >>> var_2d_i_names = Var(data_2d, name='var_2d_i_names', dims=dims)
    >>> var_2d_i_names.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=2,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=None,
        i_names=['P1', 'P2'],
    )
    ```

    Because the i\_names are not empty, the Var’s dims are not empty.

    ```
    >>> var_2d_i_names.dims.is_empty()
    False
    ```

    The i\_names dim scale enables indexing along the “i” dimension using
    strings instead of integers.

    ```
    >>> var_2d_i_names.select(i='P1')
    Var(
        <Float64 data with shape (3, 1)>,
        name='var_2d_i_names',
        dims=<Dims with names>,
        role='',
        unit=None,
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Below, we initialize a 3D Var with dims that include i\_nums and j\_nums.

    ```
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=3, i_nums=[3, 4], j_nums=[7, 8])
    >>> var_3d_nums = Var(data_3d, name='var_3d_nums', dims=dims)
    >>> var_3d_nums.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=3,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=[3, 4],
        i_names=None,
        j_nums=[7, 8],
        j_names=None,
    )
    ```

    The which i\_nums and j\_nums scales enable indexing along the “i” and
    “j” dimensions using arbitrary integers, rather than just 0-based or
    1-based integers.

    ```
    >>> var_3d_nums.select(i=3, j=7)
    Var(
        <Float64 data with shape (5, 1, 1)>,
        name='var_3d_nums',
        dims=<Dims with nums>,
        role='',
        unit=None,
        attrs={},
    )
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.drop_observations.md === -->

# Var.drop\_observations[](#var-drop-observations "Link to this heading")

Var.drop\_observations(*condition: BoolArrayLike1D*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.drop_observations "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Var with points along axis 0 removed where condition is True.

    Parameters:
    :   **condition** (*bool 1D array-like*) – A boolean 1D array-like of the same length as the Var’s data
        along axis 0.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var with the same name, attrs, role, and unit as
        this Var, but with a new data array and possibly new dims.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([10, 11, 12, 13, 14], name='Gain')
    >>> gain_filtered = gain.drop_observations([True, False, False, True, False])
    >>> gain_filtered.to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([11, 12, 14])
    ```

    Now let’s do the same thing, but with a 2D Var.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> data = np.arange(20).reshape((5, 4))
    >>> portz = Var(data, name='PortZ')
    >>> portz_filtered = portz.drop_observations([True, False, False, True, False])
    >>> portz_filtered.to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([[ 4,  5,  6,  7],
           [ 8,  9, 10, 11],
           [16, 17, 18, 19]])
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dtype.md === -->

# Var.dtype[](#var-dtype "Link to this heading")

*property* Var.dtype*: [DataType](keysight.pwdatatools.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools._core.datatypes.DataType")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dtype "Link to this definition")
:   Get the data type.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan.md === -->

# Var.fill\_nan[](#var-fill-nan "Link to this heading")

Var.fill\_nan(*value: Number*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan "Link to this definition")
:   Fill all NaN values and return a new Var or a reference to this Var.

    Parameters:
    :   * **value** (*float | int | complex | numpy.number*) – The value used to fill NaN values.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If True, fill NaN values in place and return a reference to this Var.
          If False, return a new Var with NaN values filled, leaving this Var unchanged.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var or reference to this Var with the same name, attrs, role,
        dims, and unit as before, but with filled NaN values.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Raises:
    :   **TypeError** – If the value is not a number or a complex number is input for a Var
        with a non-complex dtype.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.array([1, np.nan, 3])
    >>> v = Var(data, name='v')
    >>> v_filled = v.fill_nan(42)
    >>> v_filled.to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([ 1., 42.,  3.])
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_null.md === -->

# Var.fill\_null[](#var-fill-null "Link to this heading")

Var.fill\_null(*value: Scalar*, *\**, *in\_place: bool = False*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_null "Link to this definition")
:   Fill all nulls values with the given value and return a new Var or a reference to this Var.

    Parameters:
    :   * **value** (*scalar*) – The value used to fill null values.
        * **in\_place** (*bool, default False*) – If True, fill null values in place and return a reference to this Var.
          If False, return a new Var with null values filled, leaving this Var unchanged.

    Returns:
    :   A Var with the same name, attrs, role, dims, and unit as
        before, but with filled null values.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Raises:
    :   **TypeError** – If the value is not compatible with this Var’s dtype.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.ma.array([1, 2, 3], mask=[False, True, False])
    >>> v = Var(data, name='v')
    >>> v_filled = v.fill_null(42)
    >>> v_filled.to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([ 1, 42,  3])
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_1D_vars.md === -->

# Var.from\_1D\_vars[](#var-from-1d-vars "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Var.from\_1D\_vars(*vars1D: Iterable[[Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")]*, */*, *\**, *name: str*, *dims: [Dims](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools._core.indexing.Dims") | None = None*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_1D_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Make a multi-dimensional Var by combining 1D Vars.

    Parameters:
    :   * **vars1D** (*Iterable of Var*) – Iterable of 1D Vars to combine.
        * **name** (*str*) – Name of the new Var.
        * **dims** (*Dims, optional*) – Defines the dims for the new multi-dim Var.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")
