<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# Var.from\_pandas\_dataframe[](#var-from-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Var.from\_pandas\_dataframe(*dataframe: pd.DataFrame*, */*, *\**, *idx: bool = True*, *has\_varname: bool = True*, *name: str | None = None*, *dtype: NumpyDtypeLike = None*, *cols\_dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a Var from a pandas DataFrame.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dataframe** (*pandas.DataFrame*) – The DataFrame to convert to a Var.
        * **idx** (*bool, default True*) – If `True` and the DataFrame has a non-default index, it is
          used to create the Var’s idx. Otherwise, the Var’s idx is set
          to `None`. If the DataFrame has a default index, this
          parameter has no effect.
        * **has\_varname** (*bool, default True*) – Set this parameter to `True` if the DataFrame’s columns
          contain the variable’s name. The variable’s name may be
          in the first level of MultiIndex columns, or as part of a flat
          column index. If `False`, the DataFrame’s columns must not
          contain the variable’s name.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – An optional name of the returned Var. If provided, it takes
          precedence over the varname in the DataFrame’s columns.
          Otherwise, the Var’s name is obtained from the DataFrame’s
          columns.
        * **dtype** (*dtype-like, optional*) – The dtype of the Var’s data ndarray. If `None`, the dtype is
          inferred from the dtypes in the DataFrame.
        * **cols\_dimscales\_delim** (*tuple[str, str, str], default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the labels of a flat string columns index. Defaults to
          `('[', ',', ']')`. The first character is the opening
          delimiter, the second is the delimiter between dimension
          scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For example,
          the default delimiters would work for labels like
          `'S[1,1]'`, `'S[1,2]'`, etc.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If `has_varname=True` and the DataFrame’s columns do
          not appear to contain the variable’s name.
          - If `has_varname=False` and `name=None`.

    Notes

    The returned Var’s unit, role, and attrs are always empty.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_series.md === -->

# Var.from\_pandas\_series[](#var-from-pandas-series "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Var.from\_pandas\_series(*series: pd.Series*, */*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *dtype: NumpyDtypeLike = None*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_series "Link to this definition")
:   Create a Var from a pandas Series.

    The Var’s name is set by the name input arg, not by the input
    `series.name` attribute. The Var’s dims are set to
    `None` if the Series has an unnamed zero-based integer index.
    The Var’s counts, unit, role, and attrs are always
    `None`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **series** (*pandas.Series*) – The pandas Series to convert to a Var.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – Optional name of the new Var. If provided, the name of the
          input Series is ignored. If not provded, the name of the input
          Series is used.
        * **dtype** (*dtype-like, optional*) – The dtype of the Var’s data ndarray. If `None`, the dtype is
          inferred from the dtype of the input Series.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_dims.md === -->

# Var.has\_dims[](#var-has-dims "Link to this heading")

Var.has\_dims() → bool[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_dims "Link to this definition")
:   Return bool indicating whether this Var has non-empty dims.

    Empty dims are defined as Dims having no scales.

    Return type:
    :   *bool*

    Examples

    Let’s illustrate with a 1D Var with no dims.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var, Dims
    >>> gain = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain')
    >>> gain.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=1,
        idx=None,
    )
    >>> gain.has_dims()
    False
    ```

    Now let’s create a Var with an idx.

    ```
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=1, idx=[1, 2, 3])
    >>> gain_with_idx = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain', dims=dims)
    >>> gain_with_idx.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=1,
        idx=[1, 2, 3],
    )
    >>> gain_with_idx.has_dims()
    True
    ```

    Now, let’s demonstrate with a 2D Var with no dims.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> data = np.random.random(6).reshape((3, 2))
    >>> var_2d = Var(data, name='var_2d')
    >>> var_2d.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=2,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=None,
        i_names=None,
    )
    >>> var_2d.has_dims()
    False
    ```

    Now, let’s create a 2D Var with dims that include i\_nums.

    ```
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=2, i_nums=[1, 2])
    >>> var_2d_i_nums = Var(data, name='var_2d_i_nums', dims=dims)
    >>> var_2d_i_nums.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=2,
        idx=None,
        i_nums=[1, 2],
        i_names=None,
    )
    >>> var_2d_i_nums.has_dims()
    True
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_idx.md === -->

# Var.has\_idx[](#var-has-idx "Link to this heading")

Var.has\_idx() → bool[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_idx "Link to this definition")
:   Return bool indicating whether this Var has an idx.

    Return type:
    :   *bool*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain')
    >>> gain.has_idx()
    False
    ```

    Set the idx.

    ```
    >>> gain.idx = [1, 2, 3]
    >>> gain.has_idx()
    True
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_role.md === -->

# Var.has\_role[](#var-has-role "Link to this heading")

Var.has\_role(*role: str*, *\**, *exact: bool = False*) → bool[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_role "Link to this definition")
:   Return bool indicating whether this Var has the given role.

    Parameters:
    :   * **role** (*str*) – The role to match.
        * **exact** (*bool, default False*) – If `False`, the role is considered a match if it is either
          a subrole or an exact match of an existing role. If `True`,
          the role is considered a match only if it is an exact match
          of an existing role. Even if `True`, the role is finalized
          before testing for a match (auxiliary roles are sorted, etc.).

    Return type:
    :   *bool*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain', role='gain.power')
    ```

    The default behavior is to match to any subrole or exact role.

    ```
    >>> gain.has_role('gain')
    True
    >>> gain.has_role('gain.operating')
    True
    >>> gain.has_role('gain.power')
    True
    ```

    If `exact=True`, only exact matches are considered, and subrole matches
    are not considered.

    ```
    >>> gain.has_role('gain', exact=True)
    False
    >>> gain.has_role('gain.power', exact=True)
    True
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_nan.md === -->

# Var.is\_nan[](#var-is-nan "Link to this heading")

Var.is\_nan() → NDArray[np.bool\_][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_nan "Link to this definition")
:   Return a boolean array indicating where the data is NaN.

    Returns:
    :   A boolean array of the same shape as the data, where True
        indicates that the data is NaN.

    Return type:
    :   numpy.ndarray

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.array([1, np.nan, 3])
    >>> v = Var(data, name='v')
    >>> v.is_nan()
    array([False,  True, False])
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_null.md === -->

# Var.is\_null[](#var-is-null "Link to this heading")

Var.is\_null() → NDArray[np.bool\_][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_null "Link to this definition")
:   Return a boolean array indicating where the data is null.

    Returns:
    :   A boolean array of the same shape as the data, where True
        indicates that the data is null.

    Return type:
    :   numpy.ndarray

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.ma.array([1, 2, 3], mask=[False, True, False])
    >>> v = Var(data, name='v')
    >>> v.is_null()
    array([False,  True, False])
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.keep_observations.md === -->

# Var.keep\_observations[](#var-keep-observations "Link to this heading")

Var.keep\_observations(*condition: BoolArrayLike1D*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.keep_observations "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Var with observations along axis 0 kept where condition is True.

    Parameters:
    :   **condition** (*bool 1D array-like*) – A boolean 1D array-like of the same length as the Var’s data
        along axis 0.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var with the same name, attrs, role, and unit as
        this Var, but with a new data array and possibly new dims.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([10, 11, 12, 13, 14], name='Gain')
    >>> gain_filtered = gain.keep_observations([True, False, False, True, False])
    >>> gain_filtered.to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([10, 13])
    ```

    Now let’s do the same thing, but with a 2D Var.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> data = np.arange(20).reshape((5, 4))
    >>> portz = Var(data, name='PortZ')
    >>> portz_filtered = portz.keep_observations([True, False, False, True, False])
    >>> portz_filtered.to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([[ 0,  1,  2,  3],
           [12, 13, 14, 15]])
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md === -->

# Var[](#var "Link to this heading")

*class* Var[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "Link to this definition")
:   A dataset variable with data and metadata.

    A Var can exist by itself, but typically it is stored inside of a Block.

    Examples

    Initialize a 1D Var with data and metadata:

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.array([1, 2, 3])
    >>> vout = Var(data, name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    ```

    Note

    The data is required, but all other parameters are optional.

    Access the Var’s attributes:

    ```
    >>> vout.name
    'Vout'
    >>> vout.role
    'voltage'
    >>> vout.unit
    'V'
    ```

    Get a summary of the Var with the info() method:

    ```
    >>> vout.info()
    kind           -
    role     voltage
    dtype      Int32
    shape       (3,)
    dims           -
    unit           V
    min            1
    max            3
    null           -
    nan            -
    attrs          -
    Name: Vout, dtype: string
    ```

    Instantiate with a numpy MaskedArray to represent missing or null data points:

    ```
    >>> ma = np.ma.masked_array([3, 4], mask=[True, False])
    >>> v_masked = Var(ma, name='masked')
    >>> v_masked.info()
    kind         -
    role         -
    dtype    Int32
    shape     (2,)
    dims         -
    unit         -
    min          4
    max          4
    null         1
    nan          -
    attrs        -
    Name: masked, dtype: string
    ```

    Note

    The masked values become null values and are not included in the min and max.

    See also

    The Var methods related to null and nan values, such as [`is_null()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_null.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_null "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_null"),
    [`is_nan()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_nan"), [`has_nan()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_nan"), [`has_null()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_null.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_null "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_null"), [`fill_nan()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan"), [`fill_null()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_null.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_null "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_null").

    Initialize a 2D Var.

    ```
    >>> data = np.random.random(6).reshape((3, 2))
    >>> portz = Var(data, name='PortZ', role='impedance.port', unit='Ohm')
    ```

    Using pandas or polars can be very complementary to pwdatatools. Here,
    let’s create a pandas DataFrame in order to more easily visualize our
    2D Var. Note that 1-based integer indexes are used for the higher
    dimension (the numbers inside of the square brackets).

    Note

    There are other options for indexing into the higher dimensions
    of a variable, such as 0-based integer and string name indexes.

    ```
    >>> df = portz.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
       PortZ[1]  PortZ[2]
    0  0.551544  0.467092
    1  0.813803  0.629148
    2  0.146644  0.529128
    ```

    Or, if you prefer, you can use MultiIndex columns to get rid of the delimiters.

    ```
    >>> df = portz.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=2)
    >>> print(df)
    varname     PortZ
    i               1         2
    0        0.551544  0.467092
    1        0.813803  0.629148
    2        0.146644  0.529128
    ```

    See also

    For more information on pandas DataFrame indexing, see
    [pandas DataFrame Indexing](../../core_concepts/pandas_dataframe_indexing.md#pandas-dataframe-indexing) and also the pandas documentation.

    Initialize a 3D Var with two higher dimensions (always named “i” and “j”).
    Let’s define string names to use as indexes along these higher dimensions
    instead of integers.

    ```
    >>> data = np.random.random(20).reshape((5, 2, 2))
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=3, i_names=['P1', 'P2'], j_names=['P1', 'P2'])
    >>> s = Var(data, name='S', dims=dims, role='s_parameters')
    >>> df = s.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=3)
    >>> print(df)
    varname         S
    i              P1                  P2
    j              P1        P2        P1        P2
    0        0.551284  0.070031  0.586010  0.885456
    1        0.626966  0.234664  0.932699  0.882455
    2        0.137112  0.213657  0.657413  0.388838
    3        0.410304  0.545891  0.158400  0.034383
    4        0.009548  0.688676  0.918804  0.848939
    ```

    See also

    For more detailed examples, see the [Use the Var Class](../../howto/use_var_class.md#use-var-class) in the
    “How To” section of the documentation.

    Attributes

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`attrs`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.attrs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.attrs "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.attrs") | Get or set a mapping of arbitrary attributes (metadata). |
    | [`block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.block "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.block") | Get the Block that contains this Var, or else None if this Var is not contained in a Block. |
    | [`dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims") | Get this Var's dimensional metadata. |
    | [`dtype`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dtype.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dtype "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dtype") | Get the data type. |
    | [`idx`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.idx.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.idx "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.idx") | Get or set an optional index along the first dimension (axis 0). |
    | [`idxname`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.idxname") | Get a string name for the idx (None if an idx is not present). |
    | [`kind`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.kind.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.kind "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.kind") | Get the string literal "ivar" or "dvar" if this Var is part of a Block, otherwise None. |
    | [`name`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.name "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.name") | Get or set the arbitrary string identifier. |
    | [`ndim`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.ndim.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.ndim "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.ndim") | Get the number of dimensions. |
    | [`role`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.role.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.role "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.role") | Get or set a string defining the function or part this Var serves within a Block. |
    | [`shape`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.shape.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.shape "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.shape") | Get the shape of the variable's data. |
    | [`size`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.size.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.size "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.size") | Get the number of elements in the variable's data. |
    | [`unit`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.unit.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.unit "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.unit") | Get or set an optional string representing a unit of measure associated with this Var. |

    Methods

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`__init__`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__init__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__init__ "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__init__")(data[, dtype, name, dims, role, ...]) | Initialize a new Var instance with data and optional metadata. |
    | [`add_role`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.add_role.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.add_role "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.add_role")(role) | Add a role to this Var. |
    | [`copy`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy")() | Return a deep copy of this Var. |
    | [`count_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.count_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.count_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.count_observations")() | Return the number of observations in this Var. |
    | [`drop_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.drop_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.drop_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.drop_observations")(condition) | Return a new Var with points along axis 0 removed where condition is True. |
    | [`fill_nan`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_nan")(value, \*[, in\_place]) | Fill all NaN values and return a new Var or a reference to this Var. |
    | [`fill_null`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_null.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_null "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.fill_null")(value, \*[, in\_place]) | Fill all nulls values with the given value and return a new Var or a reference to this Var. |
    | [`from_1D_vars`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_1D_vars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_1D_vars "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_1D_vars")(vars1D, /, \*, name[, dims]) | Make a multi-dimensional Var by combining 1D Vars. |
    | [`from_pandas_dataframe`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_dataframe")(dataframe, /, \*[, ...]) | Create a Var from a pandas DataFrame. |
    | [`from_pandas_series`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_series.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_series "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.from_pandas_series")(series, /, \*[, name, dtype]) | Create a Var from a pandas Series. |
    | [`has_dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_dims")() | Return bool indicating whether this Var has non-empty dims. |
    | [`has_idx`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_idx.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_idx "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_idx")() | Return bool indicating whether this Var has an idx. |
    | [`has_nan`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_nan")() | Return bool indicating whether this Var has NaNs. |
    | [`has_null`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_null.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_null "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_null")() | Return bool indicating whether this Var has nulls. |
    | [`has_role`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_role.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_role "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.has_role")(role, \*[, exact]) | Return bool indicating whether this Var has the given role. |
    | [`info`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.info.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.info "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.info")() | Return a pandas Series containing summary information about this Var. |
    | [`is_nan`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_nan.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_nan "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_nan")() | Return a boolean array indicating where the data is NaN. |
    | [`is_null`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_null.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_null "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.is_null")() | Return a boolean array indicating where the data is null. |
    | [`keep_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.keep_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.keep_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.keep_observations")(condition) | Return a new Var with observations along axis 0 kept where condition is True. |
    | [`rename`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.rename.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.rename "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.rename")(name) | Return a new Var instance with the new name. |
    | [`repeat_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.repeat_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.repeat_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.repeat_observations")(counts) | Return new Var with obervations along axis 0 repeated according to counts. |
    | [`replace`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.replace.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.replace "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.replace")(\*\*kwargs) | Return a new Var with specified data and/or metadata replaced with new values. |
    | [`select`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select")([indexer\_dict, method, limit, tolerance]) | Return new Var, created by indexing into this Var's dims. |
    | [`set_data`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.set_data.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.set_data "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.set_data")(data, \*[, dtype, in\_place]) | Set the data of this Var (in place) or return a new Var with the new data. |
    | [`set_data_in_place`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.set_data_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.set_data_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.set_data_in_place")(data[, dtype]) | Set the data of this Var (in place). |
    | [`sort_observations`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.sort_observations.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.sort_observations "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.sort_observations")(indices, \*[, in\_place]) | Sort along axis 0 using indices and return a new Var or a reference to this Var. |
    | [`to_numpy_maskedarray`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_numpy_maskedarray.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_numpy_maskedarray "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_numpy_maskedarray")() | Return data as a `numpy.MaskedArray`. |
    | [`to_numpy_ndarray`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_numpy_ndarray.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_numpy_ndarray "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_numpy_ndarray")() | Return data as a `numpy.ndarray`. |
    | [`to_pandas_dataframe`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_dataframe")(\*[, dtype, idx, ...]) | Make a pandas DataFrame from this Var. |
    | [`to_pandas_series`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_series.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_series "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_series")(\*[, dtype, idx, name\_nlevels]) | Make a pandas Series from this Var. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.ndim.md === -->

# Var.ndim[](#var-ndim "Link to this heading")

*property* Var.ndim*: int*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.ndim "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.rename.md === -->

# Var.rename[](#var-rename "Link to this heading")

Var.rename(*name: str*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.rename "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Var instance with the new name.

    Parameters:
    :   **name** (*str*) – The new Var’s name.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var instance with the new name.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> vout = Var([1, 2, 3], name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    >>> vout2 = vout.rename('Vout2')
    >>> vout2.name
    'Vout2'
    >>> vout.name
    'Vout'
    ```

# Var.repeat\_observations[](#var-repeat-observations "Link to this heading")

Var.repeat\_observations(*counts: UIntArrayLike1D*) → [Var](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.repeat_observations "Link to this definition")
:   Return new Var with obervations along axis 0 repeated according to counts.

    Parameters:
    :   **counts** (*unsigned integer 1D array-like*) – A 1D integer array-like of the same length as the Var’s data
        along axis 0. Each integer is the number of times to repeat
        the corresponding point in the Var’s data.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var with the same name, attrs, role, and unit as
        this Var, but with a new data array and possibly new dims.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.replace.md === -->

# Var.replace[](#var-replace "Link to this heading")

Var.replace(*\*\*kwargs*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.replace "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Var with specified data and/or metadata replaced with new values.

    Parameters:
    :   **\*\*kwargs** – The new values to replace. Any of the following may be provided:
        - `data`: The new data.
        - `name`: The new name.
        - `attrs`: The new attrs.
        - `dims`: The new dims.
        - `role`: The new role.
        - `unit`: The new unit.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var instance with the new data and/or metadata.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.array([1, 2, 3])
    >>> vout = Var(data, name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    >>> new_data = np.array([4.5, 5.5, 6.5]) # new array is float
    >>> vout2 = vout.replace(data=new_data, role='voltage.dc')
    >>> vout
    Var(
        <Float64 data with shape (3,)>,
        name='Vout',
        dims=<empty Dims>,
        role='voltage.dc',
        unit='V',
        attrs={},
    )
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.role.md === -->

# Var.role[](#var-role "Link to this heading")

*property* Var.role*: str*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.role "Link to this definition")
:   Get or set a string defining the function or part this Var serves within a Block.

    It cannot be any arbitrary string; it must be one of the known,
    valid roles. A role is a string that consists of a base role
    and zero or more qualifiers, separated by periods.

    Examples

    Initialize a Var without a role results in the Var having a role
    equal to an empty str.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> v = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Vin')
    >>> v.role
    ''
    ```

    Initialize a Var with a role.

    ```
    >>> v = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Vin', role='voltage.input')
    >>> v.role
    'voltage.input'
    ```

    Set the value of role. Note also how the 2 auxiliary roles
    (dc and input) are sorted when stored in the Var.

    ```
    >>> v.role = 'voltage.input.dc'
    >>> v.role
    'voltage.dc.input'
    ```

    Initialize a Var with an invalid role.

    ```
    >>> v = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Vin', role='foo')
    <ValueError: Invalid role: 'foo'>
    ```

    See also

    `The`
    :   ref:How to Use Roles section of the documentation.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select.md === -->

# Var.select[](#var-select "Link to this heading")

Var.select(*indexer\_dict: IndexerDict | None = None*, */*, *\**, *method: Literal[None, 'pad', 'ffill', 'backfill', 'bfill', 'nearest'] = None*, *limit: int | None = None*, *tolerance: Scalar | ArrayLike | NDArray | pd.Series | None = None*, *\*\*indexer\_kwargs*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select "Link to this definition")
:   Return new Var, created by indexing into this Var’s dims.

    This uses pandas indexing under the hood and supports any types of
    indexers that pandas label-based indexing supports. The selection is
    applied to the data and the dims.

    Parameters:
    :   * **indexer\_dict** (*dict of str to Indexer, optional*) – A dict with keys equal to the dimnames and values of scalar
          labels or collection of labels (list, ndarray, Series, Index).
        * **method** (*{‘pad’, ‘ffill’, ‘backfill’, ‘bfill’, ‘nearest’}, optional*) – The method to use for filling holes in reindexed data. If `None`,
          use exact matches only.
        * **limit** (*int, optional*) – The number of consecutive elements to forward- or back-fill.
        * **tolerance** (*scalar or array-like, optional*) – The maximum distance between the original index and the new
          index when `method` is not `None`. The values of the
          index at the matching locations most satisfy the equation
          `abs(index[indexer] - target) <= tolerance`.
        * **indexer\_kwargs, optional** – The keyword arguments form of indexer\_dict. Either
          indexer\_dict or indexer\_kwargs must be provided, but not
          both.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var with the same name, attrs, role, and unit as this Var,
        but with a subset of the data and dims.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Raises:
    :   * **ValueError** –
          + If indexer\_dict and indexer\_kwargs are both provided.
            - If neither indexer\_dict nor indexer\_kwargs are provided.
            - If the selector contains invalid values.
            - If more than one indexer is porivded along the same axis.
            - If the Var has empty dims.
        * **TypeError** – If indexer\_dict is not a dict.

    Examples

    The [`Var.select()`](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select") method is a powerful way to index into
    multi-dimensional Vars. It allows indexing based upon arbitrary
    strings and numbers, rather than 0-based or 1-based integer
    positions. Let’s create a new Var to illustrate. We also need to
    create an instance of [`Dims`](keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims") to store metadata about the
    higher dimensions.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims, Var
    >>> data = np.arange(160).reshape(10, 4, 4)
    >>> dims = Dims(
    ...    ndim=3,
    ...    i_nums=[1, 2, 3, 4],
    ...    i_names=['P1', 'P2', 'P3', 'P4'],
    ...    j_nums=[1, 2, 3, 4],
    ...    j_names=['P1', 'P2', 'P3', 'P4']
    ... )
    >>> s = Var(data, name='S', dims=dims)
    >>> s
    Var(
        <Int32 data with shape (10, 4, 4)>,
        name='S',
        dims=<Dims with nums and names>,
        role='',
        unit=None,
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Let’s use the [`Var.select()`](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select") method to get S(2,1) from the
    S-parameters variable. Note the new shape of the data and the new dims.

    ```
    >>> s21 = s.select(i=2, j=1)
    >>> s21
    Var(
        <Int32 data with shape (10, 1, 1)>,
        name='S',
        dims=<Dims with nums and names>,
        role='',
        unit=None,
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> s21.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=3,
        i_nums=[2],
        i_names=['P2'],
        j_nums=[1],
        j_names=['P1'],
    )
    ```

    Let’s do the same thing, except let’s use the portnames this time.

    ```
    >>> s21 = s.select(i='P2', j='P1')
    >>> s21
    Var(
        <Int32 data with shape (10, 1, 1)>,
        name='S',
        dims=<Dims with nums and names>,
        role='',
        unit=None,
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> s21.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=3,
        i_nums=[2],
        i_names=['P2'],
        j_nums=[1],
        j_names=['P1'],
    )
    ```

    Now, let’s use the [`Var.select()`](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.select") method to get all the
    S-parameters associated with ports 3 and 4. Just for
    illustration purposes, this time we use a dict instead of
    keyword arguments.

    ```
    >>> s_p3_p4 = s.select({'i': [3, 4], 'j': [3, 4]})
    >>> s_p3_p4
    Var(
        <Int32 data with shape (10, 2, 2)>,
        name='S',
        dims=<Dims with nums and names>,
        role='',
        unit=None,
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> s_p3_p4.dims
    Dims(
        ndim=3,
        i_nums=[3, 4],
        i_names=['P3', 'P4'],
        j_nums=[3, 4],
        j_names=['P3', 'P4'],
    )
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# Var.to\_pandas\_dataframe[](#var-to-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

Var.to\_pandas\_dataframe(*\**, *dtype: DtypeLike = None*, *idx: bool = True*, *cols\_nlevels: int = 0*, *cols\_level\_names: bool = True*, *cols\_dimscales\_delim: tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *cols\_dimscales\_pref: Literal[None, 'nums', 'names'] = None*, *cols\_default\_ints: Literal[1, 0] = 1*, *cols\_default\_ints\_forced: bool = False*) → pd.DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Make a pandas DataFrame from this Var.

    Some of the Var’s metadata is not included in the DataFrame, such as
    role, unit, and attrs.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dtype** (*dtype-like, optional*) – The dtype of the returned DataFrame. If `None`, the dtype is
          inferred from the dtype of the data and whether it has null
          data points.
        * **idx** (*bool, default True*) – If True and the Var has an idx dimscale, it is used to create
          the DataFrame’s index. Otherwise, the DataFrame’s index is a
          default 0-based inhteger RangeIndex. If the Var has no idx,
          this parameter has no effect.
        * **cols\_nlevels** (*int, default 0*) – The number of levels to use for MultiIndex columns. If `-1`,
          use the minimum number of levels needed to represent the dims
          of the Var. If `0`, use a flat Index for the cols, embedding
          dimscales, if present. If `1`, use a MultiIndex with 1 level.
          If the value is greater than ndim + 1, fill the extra levels
          with empty strings.
        * **cols\_level\_names** (*bool, default True*) – If `True` set the names of the levels in the returned
          MultiIndex to `'varname'`, `'i'`, `'j'`, etc. Ignored if
          the returned columns Index is flat.
        * **cols\_dimscales\_delim** (*3-tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the labels of a flat string columns index. The first character
          is the opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters produce columns like
          `'PortZ[1]'`, `'S[1,1]'`, `'S[1,2]'`, etc.
        * **cols\_dimscales\_pref** (*{None, ‘nums’, ‘names’}, optional*) – The type of dimension scales preferred for the DataFrame
          columns if the Var’s dims are used. If `'nums'`, use the
          numeric dimscales. If `'names'`, use the string dimscales.
          If `None` (the default), prefer numeric dimscales if they
          exist, otherwise use string dimscales. In all cases, if the
          dimscales are not present, use the integers set by
          cols\_default\_ints.
        * **cols\_default\_ints** (*{1, 0}, default 1*) – The integer-based indexing to use for the DataFrame columns in
          the event that the Var’s dims are not used. If `1`, use
          1-based ints. If `0`, use 0-based ints.
        * **cols\_default\_ints\_forced** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, the integer-based indexing is forced, even if the
          Var’s dims are present. This is useful for creating DataFrames
          with integer-based columns, even if the Var has dims.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame

    Examples

    Let’s illustrate with a 3D Var.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims, Var
    >>> data = np.arange(24).reshape(6, 2, 2)
    >>> dims = Dims(
    ...     ndim=3,
    ...     i_names=['P1', 'P2'],
    ...     j_names=['P1', 'P2'],
    ... )
    >>> s = Var(data, name='S', dims=dims)
    ```

    If we use all the default settings, the returned DataFrame uses the
    Var’s dims for columns. The columns index is flat (not a MultiIndex)
    and its labels have embedded dimension scales with the default
    delimiter characters.

    ```
    >>> s.to_pandas_dataframe()
       S['P1','P1']  S['P1','P2']  S['P2','P1']  S['P2','P2']
    0             0             1             2             3
    1             4             5             6             7
    2             8             9            10            11
    3            12            13            14            15
    4            16            17            18            19
    5            20            21            22            23
    ```

    Let’s change the settings to use a MultiIndex for the columns.
    If we set `cols_nlevels=-1`, the number of levels is determined
    by the number of dims of the Var.

    ```
    >>> s.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1)
    varname   S
    i        P1      P2
    j        P1  P2  P1  P2
    0         0   1   2   3
    1         4   5   6   7
    2         8   9  10  11
    3        12  13  14  15
    4        16  17  18  19
    5        20  21  22  23
    ```

    Let’s turn off the column level names.

    ```
    >>> s.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1, cols_level_names=False)
         S
        P1      P2
        P1  P2  P1  P2
    0    0   1   2   3
    1    4   5   6   7
    2    8   9  10  11
    3   12  13  14  15
    4   16  17  18  19
    5   20  21  22  23
    ```

    Let’s use integers instead of the Var’s dimscales (using a flat
    columns index this time).

    ```
    >>> s.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_default_ints_forced=True)
       S[1,1]  S[1,2]  S[2,1]  S[2,2]
    0       0       1       2       3
    1       4       5       6       7
    2       8       9      10      11
    3      12      13      14      15
    4      16      17      18      19
    5      20      21      22      23
    ```

    Now let’s change the delimiters for the flat columns index.

    ```
    >>> new_delims = ('(', ',', ')')  # must be a 3-tuple of str
    >>> s.to_pandas_dataframe(
    ...     cols_default_ints_forced=True, cols_dimscales_delim=new_delims
    ... )
       S(1,1)  S(1,2)  S(2,1)  S(2,2)
    0       0       1       2       3
    1       4       5       6       7
    2       8       9      10      11
    3      12      13      14      15
    4      16      17      18      19
    5      20      21      22      23
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_series.md === -->

# Var.to\_pandas\_series[](#var-to-pandas-series "Link to this heading")

Var.to\_pandas\_series(*\**, *dtype: DtypeLike = None*, *idx: bool = True*, *name\_nlevels: int = 0*) → pd.Series[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_series "Link to this definition")
:   Make a pandas Series from this Var.

    Some of the Var’s metadata is not included in the Series, such as
    role, unit, and attrs.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dtype** (*dtype-like, optional*) – The dtype of the returned Series. If `None`, the dtype is
          inferred from the dtype of the data array and whether the
          the Var has null data points.
        * **idx** (*bool, default True*) – If True and the Var has an idx dimscale, it is used to create
          the Series’ index. Otherwise, the Series’ index is a
          default 0-based inhteger RangeIndex. If the Var has no idx,
          this parameter has no effect.
        * **name\_nlevels** (*int, default 0*) – Provided for compatiblity with the `cols_nlevels` parameter
          of the [`to_pandas_dataframe()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.to_pandas_dataframe") method. This parameter
          allows the returned Series to be easily concatenated with a
          DataFrame that has MultiIndex columns. If this parameter is set
          to anything other than `0`, the Series name will be a tuple
          with a length equal to nlevels, filled with empty strings if
          necessary. If `0`, the Series name is a str, not a tuple.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.Series

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> x = Var([1.0, 1.5, 2.0], name='x')
    >>> x.to_pandas_series() # using all defaults
    0    1.0
    1    1.5
    2    2.0
    Name: x, dtype: float64
    ```

    Setting `name_nlevels=3` creates a tuple of length 3 for the
    Series name. This makes it easier to later combine the Series
    with a DataFrame that has MultiIndex columns.

    ```
    >>> x.to_pandas_series(name_nlevels=3)
    0    1.0
    1    1.5
    2    2.0
    Name: (x, , ), dtype: float64
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.viz[](#module-keysight.pwdatatools.viz "Link to this heading")

The PathWave Data Tools visualization module.

The [`keysight.pwdatatools.viz`](#module-keysight.pwdatatools.viz "keysight.pwdatatools.viz") module contains functions for plotting and visualization.
Here are the functions available in this module:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`complex_vector_to_str_series`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.complex_vector_to_str_series.md#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.complex_vector_to_str_series "keysight.pwdatatools.viz.complex_vector_to_str_series")(data, form) | Return a pandas Series of stringified complex numbers in rect or polar form. |
| [`contourplot`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.contourplot.md#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.contourplot "keysight.pwdatatools.viz.contourplot")(\*, x, y, z[, ax, sca, data, ...]) | Plot contour lines on a matplotlib Axes. |
| [`float_vector_to_str_series`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.float_vector_to_str_series.md#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.float_vector_to_str_series "keysight.pwdatatools.viz.float_vector_to_str_series")(data[, format\_spec]) | Return a str Series formatted using format\_spec. |
| [`make_contour_levels`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.make_contour_levels.md#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.make_contour_levels "keysight.pwdatatools.viz.make_contour_levels")(z, \*[, data, stepsize, ...]) | Make array of contour levels for use in contour plotting. |
| [`smith_chart`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.smith_chart.md#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.smith_chart "keysight.pwdatatools.viz.smith_chart")([ax, j\_labels, r\_labels, sca]) | Create a Smith Chart on a matplotlib Axes. |
| [`tricontourplot`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.tricontourplot.md#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.tricontourplot "keysight.pwdatatools.viz.tricontourplot")(\*, x, y, z[, ax, sca, data, ...]) | Plot contour lines on a matplotlib Axes using triangulation. |
| [`use_keysight_theme`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.use_keysight_theme.md#keysight.pwdatatools.viz.use_keysight_theme "keysight.pwdatatools.viz.use_keysight_theme")([option]) | Use Keysight's color and style theme for matplotlib and seaborn charts. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.write_file.md === -->

# write\_file[](#write-file "Link to this heading")

write\_file(*group\_or\_block: [Group](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group") | [Block](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")*, *dst: StrPathLike | [DataFile](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *\*\*kwargs*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.write_file "Link to this definition")
:   Write a Group or Block to a file.

    Known writeable file extensions are stored in `options.writing.extensions`.
    Known writeable file formats are stored in `options.writing.formats`.

    Whether metadata is written depends on the format of output file.
    If the output file’s format has places to store metadata (such as
    in the native pwdatatools .pwdt format or HDF5 format), then metadata
    is automatically included in the output file.

    Parameters:
    :   * **group\_or\_block** (*Group or Block*) – An instance of [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group") or [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block") to write to a
          file.
        * **dst** (*str, PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path. If it has a missing or unknown file
          extension, you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
          `format_override`, or 2) use the `dst_format` parameter.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificWriteOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for writing `dst`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.writing.format_specific` sets format-specific
          writing options, but this parameter overrides the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificWriteOptions`. See the docs for a list
          of all classes that are format-specific write options. Some
          examples are `ADSWriteOptions`,
          `MDIFWriteOptions`, [`ADSWriteInvalid`](keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools.ADSWriteInvalid"), and
          [`MDIFWriteInvalid`](keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFWriteInvalid").
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The write location within `dst`. Only applies to file
          formats that support multiple levels of hierarchy (for
          example, native and HDF5 formats). This parameter sets the
          write location within the file’s directory-like (or tree-like)
          structure. The “root” location is ‘/’.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Examples

    The below examples assume that `group` is an instance of
    [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group"), `block` is an
    instance of [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block"), and
    the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import write_file, DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, you can
    simply input `dst` as a str, `pathlib.Path`, or
    `DataFile`. Known file
    extensions are stored in `options.writing.extensions`.

    ```
    >>> # str path with a known ext (.ds)
    >>> write_file(group, '/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # pathlib.Path with a known ext (.mdf)
    >>> write_file(block, Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # DataFile with a known ext (.pwdt)
    >>> write_file(group, DataFile('/home/noise_param.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a missing or non-standard file
    extension, you can use the `dst_format` parameter. Alternatively,
    you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `dst`
    argument and use the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to
    define the destination’s format.

    ```
    >>> # no ext solved by dst_format
    >>> write_file(group, '/data/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # non-standard ext solved by dst_format
    >>> write_file(block, '/data/noise_param.txt', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # no ext solved by DataFile
    >>> write_file(group, DataFile('/data/noise_param', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> # overwrite dst, if it exists
    >>> write_file(group, '/home/data/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')
    >>> # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> write_file(block, '/home/data/noise_param.mdf', dst_mode='a')
    >>> # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> write_file(group, '/home/data/run1.pwdt', dst_mode='a')
    ```

    See also

    [`Group`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group"), [`Block`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")

# Data Types[](#data-types "Link to this heading")

The `pwdatatools` library defines datatypes that describe the data stored in the [`Var`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var") class. The base datatype class is [`DataType`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools.DataType"), and all other datatypes are subclasses of this class. The following datatypes are defined:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`DataType`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools.DataType") | Base class for all data type objects. |
| [`Boolean`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Boolean.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Boolean "keysight.pwdatatools.Boolean") | Boolean data type. |
| [`Complex64`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Complex64.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Complex64 "keysight.pwdatatools.Complex64") | Complex floating data type consisting of 32-bit float real and imaginary parts. |
| [`Complex128`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Complex128.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Complex128 "keysight.pwdatatools.Complex128") | Complex floating data type consisting of 64-bit float real and imaginary parts. |
| [`Float32`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Float32.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Float32 "keysight.pwdatatools.Float32") | 32-bit floating point data type. |
| [`Float64`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Float64.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Float64 "keysight.pwdatatools.Float64") | 64-bit floating point data type. |
| [`Int8`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Int8.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Int8 "keysight.pwdatatools.Int8") | 8-bit signed integer data type. |
| [`Int16`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Int16.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Int16 "keysight.pwdatatools.Int16") | 16-bit signed integer data type. |
| [`Int32`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Int32.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Int32 "keysight.pwdatatools.Int32") | 32-bit signed integer data type. |
| [`Int64`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Int64.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Int64 "keysight.pwdatatools.Int64") | 64-bit signed integer data type. |
| [`String`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.String.md#keysight.pwdatatools.String "keysight.pwdatatools.String") | String data type. |
| [`UInt8`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.UInt8.md#keysight.pwdatatools.UInt8 "keysight.pwdatatools.UInt8") | 8-bit unsigned integer data type. |
| [`UInt16`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.UInt16.md#keysight.pwdatatools.UInt16 "keysight.pwdatatools.UInt16") | 16-bit unsigned integer data type. |
| [`UInt32`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.UInt32.md#keysight.pwdatatools.UInt32 "keysight.pwdatatools.UInt32") | 32-bit unsigned integer data type. |
| [`UInt64`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.UInt64.md#keysight.pwdatatools.UInt64 "keysight.pwdatatools.UInt64") | 64-bit unsigned integer data type. |

In addition to the DataType objects, the `datatypes` module also defines the [`FillValues`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues.md#keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues "keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues") class, which contains scalar values that can be used to fill null values in Vars.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`FillValues`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues.md#keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues "keysight.pwdatatools.FillValues") | Define fill values for masked data. |

| [`CSVReadOptions`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.CSVReadOptions") | Options for CSV file reading. |
| [`CSVWriteOptions`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.CSVWriteOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.CSVWriteOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.CSVWriteOptions") | A class to store immutable options for writing to CSV files. |
| [`DataFile`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") | An object that represents a datafile on a filesystem. |
| [`LoadPullReadOptions`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullReadOptions") | Options for LoadPull datafile reading. |
| [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullDerivedVars") | Define loadpull variables to be calculated from A/B Waves. |
| [`MDIFReadOptions`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.MDIFReadOptions") | Options for MDIF reading. |

| [`MDMReadOptions`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.MDMReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.MDMReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.MDMReadOptions") | Options for MDM file reading. |
| [`SMatrixIOReadOptions`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.SMatrixIOReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.SMatrixIOReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.SMatrixIOReadOptions") | Options for SMatrixIO file reading. |
| [`TouchstoneReadOptions`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.TouchstoneReadOptions.md#keysight.pwdatatools.TouchstoneReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools.TouchstoneReadOptions") | Options for Touchstone file reading. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/global_options.md === -->

# Global Options[](#module-keysight.pwdatatools._api.config "Link to this heading")

The `pwdatatools` library provides a set of global options that can
be used to control certain behaviors. These options are divided into
four categories: licensing, logging, reading, and writing. The options
can be accessed and modified through the
`keysight.pwdatatools.options` object.

Notes

In cases where you do not want to rely on the global options when
reading or writing files, you can always override the global options
by parameters in the specific functions and methods. Some of these
parameters can be provided as instances of immutable options classes.

Examples

```
>>> import keysight.pwdatatools as pwdt
```

Let’s check the current logging level. By default, the logging level is
set to ‘error’, which prints only errors:

```
>>> pwdt.options.logging.level
'error'
```

Let’s change logging level to ‘info’, which prints info, warnings, and
errors, but suppresses debug messages:

```
>>> pwdt.options.logging.level = 'info'
```

Let’s access the readable and writeable file extensions and formats:

```
>>> pwdt.options.reading.extensions
<returns list of readable extensions>
>>> pwdt.options.writing.formats
<returns list of writeable types>
>>> pwdt.options.reading.format_to_extension_map
<returns mapping of readable file extensions to formats>
```

Let’s set the variable name associated with a particular role:

```
>>> pwdt.options.reading.varnames['impedance.reference'] = 'Zref'
```

Let’s get the current settings for writing invalid datapoints to ADS datasets:

```
>>> ads_invalid = pwdt.options.writing.format_specific.ads.invalid
>>> ads_invalid
ADSWriteInvalidGlobal(
    boolean='float_w_nan',
    complexfloating='complex_w_nan',
    floating='float_w_nan',
    integer='float_w_nan',
    string='str_w_null',
    nan_rep=nan,
    integer_null_rep=99999,
    string_null_rep='null',
)
```

Let’s change settings for writing invalid datapoints to ADS datasets:

```
>>> # First, check for other boolean options
>>> ads_invalid.boolean_options
('float_w_nan', 'int_w_null')
```

There’s only one other boolean option, so let’s pick that one:

```
>>> ads_invalid.boolean = 'int_w_null'
```

Now, ADS datasets with bool variables with bad datapoints will be
converted to ints with a sentinel value. Let’s see what that sentinel
value will be:

```
>>> ads_invalid.integer_null_rep
99999
```

Let’s also change the default string used for bad datapoints:

```
>>> ads_invalid.string_null_rep = 'invalid'
```

Now, ADS datasets with string variables with bad datapoints will use
the string ‘invalid’ to mark bad points.

Let’s look at all of our changes:

```
>>> ads_invalid
ADSWriteInvalidGlobal(
    boolean='int_w_null',
    complexfloating='complex_w_nan',
    floating='float_w_nan',
    integer='float_w_nan',
    string='str_w_null',
    nan_rep=nan,
    integer_null_rep=99999,
    string_null_rep='invalid',
)
```

# API Reference[](#api-reference "Link to this heading")

This section contains documentation for all public objects in the `keysight.pwdatatools` library. All classes, functions, and submodules below are public and available in the keysight.pwdatatools.\* namespace.

Note

If you encounter any object or submodule in the `keysight.pwdatatools` library whose name has a leading underscore, it is not public and may be removed or changed at any time without warning. The only notable exception to this rule are dunder methods (also called magic methods), which are typically public even though they have leading and trailing double underscores.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [Main](main.md#main-api-reference) | The Var, Block, and Group classes |
| [Metadata](metadata.md#metadata-api-reference) | Classes that store metadata for Vars, Blocks, and Groups |
| [File I/O](fileio.md#file-io-api-reference) | Classes and functions related to file I/O |
| [Load Pull](loadpull.md#load-pull-api-reference) | Classes and functions for working with load pull data |
| [Public Submodules](public_submodules.md#public-submodules-api-reference) | Public submodules in the library such as calc, roles, and viz |
| [Data Types](datatypes.md#data-types-api-reference) | Classes that define the data types for Vars |
| [Concatenation](concatenation.md#concatenation-api-reference) | Functions for concatenating Blocks and Vars |
| [Global Options](global_options.md#global-options-api-reference) | Global options for pwdatatools |

# Main[](#main "Link to this heading")

The section contains documentation for the Var, Block, and Group classes. These are the main classes in the `pwdatatools` library and are used to store data and metadata for datasets.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`Var`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var") | A dataset variable with data and metadata. |
| [`Block`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block") | A collection of Vars that represents a non-hierarchical dataset or a portion of a hierarchical dataset. |
| [`Group`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group") | A collection of Members that represents a hierarchical dataset or a portion of one. |


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/metadata.md === -->

# Metadata[](#metadata "Link to this heading")

This section contains documentation for the classes that store metadata for Vars, Blocks, and Groups.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`AttrsDict`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict") | A type-restricted dict that stores arbitrary attributes (metadata) for [`Var`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var"), [`Block`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block"), and [`Group`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group"). |
| [`Dims`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims") | Dimensional metadata for a [`Var`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var"). |
| [`DimScale`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale "keysight.pwdatatools.DimScale") | An object representing a index along a single dimension of a Var. |
| [`Expr`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr") | An object that represents an expression. |
| [`ExprsDict`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict.md#keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.ExprsDict") | A mapping of string names (keys) to [`Expr`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Expr.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Expr "keysight.pwdatatools.Expr") objects (values). |

# Public Submodules[](#public-submodules "Link to this heading")

This section contains documentation for all public submodules in the `keysight.pwdatatools` library. The public submodules contain functions and classes that, while useful, are not considered as central or commonly-used as the objects available in the main keysight.pwdatatools namespace. This keeps the main top-level namespace from becoming over-crowded. To access objects in these submodules, you must first import the submodule. For example:

```
>>> from keysight.pwdatatools import viz
>>> viz.use_keysight_theme()
```

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`calc`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.calc.md#module-keysight.pwdatatools.calc "keysight.pwdatatools.calc") | The PathWave Data Tools calculation module. |
| [`roles`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.roles.md#module-keysight.pwdatatools.roles "keysight.pwdatatools.roles") | The PathWave Data Tools roles module. |
| [`viz`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.viz.md#module-keysight.pwdatatools.viz "keysight.pwdatatools.viz") | The PathWave Data Tools visualization module. |
